# Collaborative research: requirements and rules

**Date**: 2026-08-09
**Status**: **Designed, not built.** Every rule carries `RS-`. Everything in §2 was executed or read
from the built code on 2026-08-09, and everything in §0.3 was read from a primary source, by three
workflows and about twenty agents before a line of this document was written; the distillation is
`research-evidence.md` and the transcripts are under `<tasks>/`. Where a document and an execution
disagreed, the execution is what is recorded.
**Scope**: a new loop in `pkb.agents` (`research.py`, `researcher.py`, `search.py`, `mcp.py`), ~~two~~
**three** new `GateReason` members and **three** new gated tools (amended 2026-08-09, skills: the
third is `file_skill` under `SKILL_PROPOSAL`, RS-146), one new recognised frontmatter field in
`pkb.core`, one membership and one ordering amendment in `pkb.packs`, ~~one new public entry point in
`pkb.sources`~~ **nothing at all in `pkb.sources`** (RS-90 as amended), one credential read in
`pkb.daemon`, and deployment configuration on `RuntimeConfig`. **No new transport, no new route, no new table, no
new agent id.** One addition to Layer 3 sits outside that line and is stated rather than buried: `/close`
decides at Layer 2 and closes a Telegram topic at Layer 5, and no signal carries between them today, so
Q46 encodes one typed run event on the existing SSE stream. One event kind, no route, no table. Written
against the built seams: `pkb.agents.routing` (the workflow template),
`pkb.agents.ingestion` (the graph-free loop template), `pkb.agents.gates`, `pkb.agents.permissions`,
`pkb.sources` and `pkb.packs`. Layer 5 carries consequences of its own, listed in §10 as requirements
on named `TG-` rules rather than written here, because that spec is owned elsewhere.

**Reviewed 2026-08-09.** Three independent reviews read this document against the built code. Their
confirmed defects are fixed in place and §9 records every one, including the four places a reviewer
was wrong and what settled it. Rule ids already published here were **not** renumbered; corrections
amend a rule's text and new rules take the next free id, so `RS-1 … RS-78` still mean what they
meant and `RS-79 … RS-95` sit in the sub-section they belong to rather than at the end.

**Amended 2026-08-09 (the session model), after the human described sessions.** Every rule up to
`RS-95` was written when a research session was a **bounded activity that closed when a synthesis was
filed**, and that is wrong. A session is a **channel**: one line of work, opened when the human opens
a channel for a goal, alive across weeks of research, trials and results carried out elsewhere, and
closed by `/close` and by nothing else. Filing ends an enquiry inside a session and leaves the
session open. Most sessions file nothing at all. §1.11 (`RS-96 … RS-126`) rules the session, the two
directives, the push-back that runs both ways, the goal-spec hand-off and the Librarian's `/learn`
fan-out. Every rule the reframe touched is **amended in place with its original text kept**, marked
*Amended 2026-08-09 (session model)*, so a reader can see what the rule used to promise: `RS-1`,
`RS-3`, `RS-4`, `RS-6`, `RS-7`, `RS-13`, `RS-14`, `RS-49`, `RS-52`, `RS-57`, `RS-84`, `RS-86` and
`RS-95`, plus §1.1's step table, §1.7's title and §8's build order.

**Reviewed again 2026-08-09, against the built code, after §1.11 landed.** The session model was
written in one pass and a review executed it against the validator, the routing workflow and the gate
chain. Two defects stopped the feature outright and are fixed: `RS-109` filed a lesson as
`source_type: note` tagged `type.solution`, which VA-11's bijection refuses before the gate is ever
asked (executed: `SOURCE_TYPE_TAG_MISMATCH`), and the Librarian's `/learn` fan-out had no way to
carry the session record across a thread boundary, no producer for its target list, and no landing
place for the human's answer. Eight new rules carry the mechanisms that were missing
(`RS-119 … RS-126`: the fan-out's scope, the record's passage across a thread boundary, the human's
answer, the expert that files nothing, the repeat guard, the rendered file's metadata, its path
resolver, and the threads that carry no session). Sixteen rules are **amended in place with their
original text kept**: `RS-49`, `RS-50`, `RS-87`, `RS-96`, `RS-102`, `RS-104`, `RS-105`, `RS-106`,
`RS-108`, `RS-109`, `RS-111`, `RS-112`, `RS-113`, `RS-116`, `RS-117`, `RS-118`, plus §1.11's step
table, §5.2's contract, §5.5's `RT-32` amendment, §8's increment 2 and §10. Contradictions `C-49`,
`C-50` and `C-51` and questions `Q50` and `Q51` are new, and `C-47` is marked resolved in place.
§9.3 records every verdict, including the three where the review's own fix was refused or narrowed.
No `RS-` id moved and no decision was re-litigated.

**Amended 2026-08-09 (search settled), by execution rather than by a vendor comparison.** Every rule
up to `RS-126` treated the search provider as an open dependency: a comparison across Brave, Tavily
and Exa, a key, a bill, and a day-one increment that ran over the tree alone because no vendor was
chosen. A probe run in this repo's venv settled it. The model provider this project already pays
ships search, its client sits in the venv today, and both calls answered in under a second. `P-48 … P-52`
carry the executed evidence with the method beside each one, and `F-27 … F-30` carry the facts about
the outside world. Four consequences reach the rules:

* **Search returns page content rather than a snippet**, 8,701 to 22,448 characters per result, so
  the harness holds the text at the moment of search. RS-127 states the admissibility check as a
  comparison against those bytes, and RS-28, RS-29 and RS-34 drop the hedging the snippet case
  forced on them.
* **Search is not an MCP server**, so decision AR narrows to the capabilities it still governs and
  Q38 resolves. RS-128 … RS-130 name the tool the researcher calls, what it returns, and the line
  between research reading and ingestion fetching. RS-90 withdraws a public entry point this design
  no longer needs.
* **One provider now serves the models and the search**, so one outage takes both. RS-131 and RS-132
  rule what a session does when search is unreachable, which nothing covered.
* **The credential is `OLLAMA_API_KEY`** and it walks `PKB_TELEGRAM_TOKEN`'s path. §1.12 (RS-133,
  RS-134) rules it.

Every rule the settlement touched is **amended in place with its original text kept**, marked
*Amended 2026-08-09 (search settled)*: `RS-28`, `RS-29`, `RS-34`, `RS-68`, `RS-74`, `RS-75`,
`RS-76`, `RS-77`, `RS-90`, `RS-91`, `RS-92`, `RS-94`, plus decision `AR`, question `Q38`, §1.8's
title, §2's `P-46`, §5's new §5.3a, §6.4's live table and §8's increments 4, 6 and 7. Eight rules are new
(`RS-127 … RS-134`), two contradictions are new (`C-52`, `C-53`), and `AT` is the last decision
either way, because nothing here needed one that execution had not already made.

**Amended 2026-08-09 (sessions, and the Hermes reconciliation).** The human ruled two things after
the search settlement landed. First, **`research/` is the wrong name for the extension folder and it
becomes `sessions/`**, in their words: *"Sessions are just long conversation that could be research,
could be collaborative discussion, or just really anything."* Layer 1 treats either name as an
extension folder, so no mechanism moves; the claim the folder makes about its own contents is what
changes. Decision `BA` carries the ruling and §1.1a states the two names the rename now separates.
Second, they asked for **Nous Research's Hermes Agent** to be read and reconciled with this design.
It was read from its source rather than from `hermes-agent.org`, whose copy is marketing, and
`F-31 … F-37` carry the seven findings with the file and line behind each one. Four consequences
reach the rules:

* **Hermes has no session-end ceremony at all** (`F-31`), so `/learn` at close is novel here and none
  of Hermes's numbers transfer as evidence for a once-per-session write. The document claims no
  support from them and says so.
* **Its exclusion list is the reusable half** (`F-35`), naming five things a session produces that
  look like knowledge and are not. §1.13 rules all five as `RS-135 … RS-139`.
* **Its prompt is biased toward writing** (`F-34`), and copying that bias here would be wrong.
  Hermes writes into a skill library with archive, snapshots and rollback; this project writes into a
  tree with no undo (D6), so `RS-140` inverts the prior and decision `BB` carries the reasoning.
* **Two of its guards are enforced in code rather than in prose** (`F-36`), and this design needs
  both. `RS-141` refuses an amendment to bytes nobody loaded this turn, and `RS-142` records a
  write's origin in the file so nothing autonomous edits what the human wrote. Decision `BC` carries
  them.

`Q54` raises the question the reconciliation exposes and leaves it for the human: Hermes learns how
to work and this project learns what is true, so `/learn` today may propose a note and never a skill.
Rules amended in place with their original text kept, marked *Amended 2026-08-09 (sessions)*:
`RS-4`, `RS-5`, `RS-45`, `RS-49`, `RS-52`, `RS-82`, `RS-93`, `RS-105`, `RS-109`, `RS-110`, `RS-125`,
plus §0.1's RT-15 bullet, §0.2's layout and its four-uses paragraph, §5.2's constants, §5.4's
`VA-43`, §7, §8's increments 2a and 3, and §9.2's third row, which refused a rename the human has now
ruled. Contradictions `C-54` and `C-55` are new. No `RS-` id moved and no decision was re-litigated.

**Amended 2026-08-09 (skills at `/learn`), after the human ruled `Q54`.** Three sentences carry the
ruling. `/learn` may propose a **skill** and not a note alone. On read-only, in their words: *"Skills
dont have to be read only, there is no hard requirement for that."* On placement: *"Skill would live
in the topic if it is topic specific, or in librarian root if it is process (like research)
specific."* §1.14 rules it as `RS-143 … RS-153`, decisions `BD`, `BE` and `BF` carry the reasoning,
and `P-53 … P-58` carry the grounding, every row of it executed in this repo on 2026-08-09. Four
consequences reach the rules, and three of the four **delete** work this document expected to owe:

* **The placement rule is already the schema's shape.** `resolve_skills` merges the root's skills
  under each topic's and lets the topic win (`P-56`, `paths.py:572-574`), and the packaged mount is
  a third source underneath both, merged by the harness rather than by Layer 1 (`P-58`). `RS-145`
  spends one field on the scope and invents no mechanism for either seam.
* **The permission layer blocks nothing here, and no glob moves.** `SKILLS_DENY_GLOBS` is
  `("/skills/**",)`, the **packaged mount**, and a topic expert's own-subtree allow already reaches
  `/kb/<Topic>/skills/**` (`P-53`, executed through the harness's own checker). `RS-147` states what
  the list already denies and why the Layer 2 change is a tool rather than a rule edit.
* **The gate exists and it is built.** `requires_approval` returns `skill-overload` for a write under
  any `skills/` folder today (`P-54`, `GateReason.SKILL_OVERLOAD`, RT-29). `RS-146` answers the gate
  question with a third tool rather than a third argument, because RT-65's tool-keyed branches sit
  ahead of every path question and a content-free filing never reaches the path chain.
* **`SK-5`'s permanent fork governs a shadowed name and nothing else.** A skill written under a new
  name shadows nothing and forks nothing, so `RS-150` names the collision case three times and
  refuses it none of the three.

`Q54` resolves to **(b) as amended** and §7 moves it out of *Deferred*. `RS-153` gives the
read-before-revise guard the id the README's claim carried no rule for, one step ahead of `RS-141`.
Decision `BF` keeps `provenance: researched` under a folder now named `sessions/` and states the
reason. Rules amended in place with their original text kept, marked *Amended 2026-08-09 (skills)*:
`RS-102`, `RS-116`, `RS-123`, `RS-124`, `RS-125`, `RS-140`, `RS-141`, `RS-142`, plus §1.11's step
table, §5.2's `Proposal`, §5.5's `RT-65` and RT-18 rows, `C-38`, §7 and §8's increments 2 and 4.
Contradictions `C-56 … C-59` and questions `Q55 … Q57` are new. No `RS-` id moved and no decision was
re-litigated.

**Three shared-sequence ids were taken while this document was being written, and all three are
corrected here.** Layer 5's §12 landed `AK`, `AL` and `Q36` on this same branch, after the check
recorded in §"Why the prefix" ran. This document's decisions were `AK … AR` and its questions
`Q36 … Q44`;
they are now `AM … AT` and `Q37 … Q45`. The unbuilt document gives way to the built one, and the
whole point of a shared sequence is that one id names one ruling. No `RS-` id moved.

### What is verified and what is not

| Marking | Means |
|---------|-------|
| **executed (grounding)** | Run against this repo and, where the row says so, against the live internet on 2026-08-09, during the three grounding workflows. Rows `P-34 … P-46` carry it, each naming what was run. |
| **executed (here)** | Run while writing this document, in this repo's venv: the installed-package probe of §2 (`P-47`) and the identifier greps behind §0.2. **Extended 2026-08-09 (search settled)**: `P-48 … P-52` and `F-27 … F-30`, each row naming the call or the grep that produced it, so a reader re-runs the probe rather than trusting the row. Two of them reached the live internet and say so. **Extended again 2026-08-09 (skills)**: `P-53 … P-58`, run against the built permission checker, the built gate table, the built validator, the built generators and the harness's own skill middleware, on a scaffolded fixture tree. Four of the six contradict what this feature's brief or its own first draft assumed, so each row prints the call and its answer rather than describing them. |
| **measured (literature)** | A published number with a sample size, recorded with its source in §0.3. Never a rounded recollection. |
| **built** | Already implemented and covered by the passing suite (2198 tests, 6 deselected, five import contracts). This spec binds against it and never around it. |
| **designed** | Specified here, not implemented. Every `RS-` rule carries it. |
| **unverified** | Looked for and not found. The row says where the answer was sought and states that no rule depends on it. `F-30` is the only one. Added 2026-08-09 (search settled), because a document that carries five strength labels and no label for "we tried and could not tell" pushes an unknown into `asserted`, where a later reader reads it as somebody's judgement. |
| **read (source)** | Read from another project's source tree, with the file and line named so a reader opens the same lines. `F-31 … F-37` carry it, all seven from a clone of `NousResearch/hermes-agent`. Added 2026-08-09 (sessions). **A claim taken from that project's landing page is never this label**: `hermes-agent.org` says *"when Hermes solves a hard problem, it writes a reusable skill"*, and the code shows a nudge counter firing every ten tool iterations (`F-31`). The two disagree about the mechanism, so the marking exists to keep a marketing sentence out of a rule's evidence column. |

## Why the prefix is `RS-`

`FM-`, `PA-`, `VA-`, `GE-`, `MA-`, `SC-`, `TG-`, `RT-`, `RG-`, `MW-`, `LB-`, `PR-`, `SK-`, `EX-`,
`SV-`, `RO-`, `SS-`, `AR-`, `ST-`, `MC-`, `PK-`, `CL-`, `DC-`, `TU-` and `LS-` are in use. A grep of
`docs/`, `src/` and `tests/` for `RS-<digits>` returns zero matches (executed here). `RE-` reads as
*reference* against a schema whose whole vocabulary turns on that word, and `CR-` reads as
*conflict-review*, which is a status tag this design touches. `RS-` collides with neither the taken
prefixes nor the domain vocabulary, and it is two letters like every other prefix in the project.

Numbering continues the project's shared sequences rather than restarting them: package findings at
`P-34`, contradictions at `C-38`, open questions at `Q37`, live facts at `F-14`, decisions at `AM`.

**The sequences were re-checked against the working tree, not against `HEAD`.** The Layer 5 Telegram
change is uncommitted on this same branch and it claims `F-13`, `Q35`, `AI` and `AJ`; at `HEAD` those
were free, so a check against `HEAD` alone would have published four duplicate ids on one branch.

**Amended 2026-08-09 (session model): a check run once is a check that expires.** Layer 5's §12
landed later the same day and claimed `AK`, `AL` and `Q36`, which this document had already taken.
Re-grepped over the working tree: `AL` and `Q36` are the last taken, so the decisions here run
`AM … AT` and the questions `Q37 … Q45`, and the new decisions of §1.11 run `AU` onward. The rule
this teaches is worth stating rather than fixing quietly: **a shared sequence is only safe to claim
in the commit that publishes it**, so a document that sits unbuilt beside an active branch re-checks
before it lands.
Verified by grep over `docs/`, `src/` and `tests/` in the working tree: `P-33`, `C-37`, `Q35`, `F-13`
and `AJ` are the last taken, so this document starts one past each. Two ids inside this document were
taken and are corrected here: `VA-42` is `RELATED_TOPICS_PREFIXED` (`validation.py:530`, a *warning*,
cited by MW-12) so the provenance value check is **`VA-42`**, and `RT-65` is "Layer 2 exposes no
thread listing" (Layer 2 rules line 263, cited at `runtime.py:766,893,961`) so the `GateReason`
amendment is **`RT-65`**. `FM-16` was checked the same way and is free.
