## Read this first

Seven findings are load-bearing: four about this repo's own code (`P-34 … P-37`, restated in §2 with
the rest) and three measured elsewhere (`F-24 … F-26`, restated in §0.3 with the rest). Each one
turns a plausible design into a broken one, and each one would fail **silently**: the loop runs, the
file lands, the human reads a research summary, and the summary is fiction.

| | The obvious design | What the grounding actually found |
|---|---|---|
| **P-34** | Research sub-agents are deepagents `task` delegations from the topic expert. | **This repo already lost that bet one layer up.** `routing.py`'s own docstring records it: the Librarian held `task`, and measured against a real model it did not delegate. It ran `ls`/`read_file`/`grep` and answered from raw files, and on another run it stated *"The Cooking expert checked the knowledge base"* when no expert had run. LB-12's answer was to take `task` out of the model request and make the fan-out code. A researcher reached through `task` is the same bet with the same model: a delegation the model may skip, returning prose the harness cannot check. RS-15 rules it out. |
| **P-35** | The walk-with-me checkpoint is an approval interrupt: the human sees candidates and answers. | **An approval blocks every other message on its thread.** RT-39 refuses a turn while an interrupt is pending and Layer 3 maps it to a 409. So *"those three look right, keep going, but drop the second"* is not expressible: the only answers an approval accepts are the ones in `allowed_decisions`, and any other message on that thread is refused until the interrupt clears. The checkpoint has to be an ordinary assistant message that ends the turn, with the session state held durably (RS-8 … RS-13). |
| **P-36** | The `type.*` tag says what kind of knowledge a file carries, so a web result is distinguishable from a book. | **The type tag restates the path.** VA-13 and VA-14 force the declared type to agree with the folder, so the type carries no independent information. A web result files as `type.reference`, indistinguishable from a book the human owns, or as `type.note`, indistinguishable from something they proved by hand. The grounding pass called the second one silent trust laundering. `trust.*` is refused (`UNKNOWN_TAG_NAMESPACE`/TG-2) and `source_type: clipping` is refused (`UNKNOWN_SOURCE_TYPE`/FM-6), both closed. RS-42 adds a recognised optional field instead. |
| **P-37** | An unknown frontmatter key is preserved, so provenance can ride in the body or in an ad-hoc key. | **VA-32 tells the model to delete it.** Unknown keys are preserved with a warning whose hint reads *"Check for a typo of a known field (title, description, topic, tags, …); the key is preserved either way"*, and warnings ride along on the success message the model sees. A model that follows advice removes provenance on the next edit and nothing anywhere records that it was ever there. RS-43 and RS-44 handle the trap: the field becomes known before any writer emits it, and VA-32's hint gains a clause. |
| **F-24** | The human's design point 4: notes are a prior, so consult them first and let them shape the search. | **Stating a belief as prompt context suppressed disconfirmation detection by 16 to 93 percentage points** across four models, over ~10,000 queries on 250 CVE patch pairs with code and instructions held identical. A consonant conversational search drove the confirmatory query rate to 42.92% against 15.57% neutral (CHI 2024, N=223, d=1.01), and the effect came from the querying rather than the answering. The human's own framing settles it: a prior multiplies the likelihood, it does not sample it. RS-37 keeps the prior out of the query-planning call and puts it in the weighting. |
| **F-25** | Ask the model whether a page contradicts a note. | **Near chance.** WikiContradict, 253 human-annotated pairs: with one non-conflicting passage models score 87 to 97%; with both contradictory passages supplied, GPT-4 scores **6.3%** and Llama-3-70b **10.4%**. Explicit instruction lifts Llama to 43.8% and GPT-4 to 10.4%, and almost all the gain lands on conflicts that were already explicit. A model cannot be the gate. RS-38 selects the pairs in code and lets the model label them, never filter them. |
| **F-26** | More tool calls means better coverage, and cost is not a constraint anyway. | **Accuracy collapses.** Factual accuracy fell from 79% to 17% as tool calls rose from 2 to 150; 77 to 94% of steps in long-horizon search add no new evidence; incorrect trajectories run 1.9 to 2.9 times longer than correct ones. A budget here is a quality mechanism, and the length of a trajectory is a signal that it is going wrong. RS-68 … RS-73 bound it and treat exhaustion as an ordinary outcome the file records. |

### Decisions applied on top of the grounding

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|----------|-----|
| **AM** | **A researcher is a bounded coroutine, not a registered agent and not a `task` delegation.** No agent id, no thread, no `expert.md`, no catalog entry, no reachability from any transport. Code invokes it under a semaphore, exactly as `FanOut` invokes an expert. | P-34. The measurement is in this repo. A `task` call is a decision the model may decline and a return the harness cannot check; the fan-out shape is the one this project already fixed the same failure with. It also keeps `list_agents()` honest: a researcher in the catalog would be addressable from Telegram and the TUI, and a channel bound to something with no topic and no persona is a mis-file waiting to happen (TG-1). |
| **AN** | **The checkpoint ends the turn. It is never an interrupt.** The session is durable in the thread's own scratch filesystem, and the human's next ordinary message resumes it through one typed tool call. | P-35, and an executed fact from the grounding: a probe wrote `/goal.md` to a thread's `StateBackend` scratch filesystem, `kill -9`'d the daemon, restarted, and read it back byte for byte. No KB write, no gate, no schema change, and deepagents itself uses the same route. The durable home already exists and nothing uses it. |
| **AO** | **The harness fetches; the model never supplies bytes or a locator.** Every admissible quote is checked against the bytes `pkb.sources` retrieved in this session, after whitespace normalisation, using `pkb.agents.ingestion`'s existing check. Never an offset. | F-14, F-15, and LS-13, which was written for exactly this failure one layer down after a probe filed a fabricated quotation verbatim with `gainful: True, findings: ()`. One implementation of the check, cited rather than copied. |
| **AP** | **A research fetch stages nothing under `<kb>/.inbox/`.** Candidate bytes live in a bounded in-session cache and are discarded when the enquiry ends (amended 2026-08-09: a session lives for weeks under AU, so holding 32 MiB of HTML for its whole life is the failure this decision was written to avoid, one object up). Staging happens when the human accepts a source for ingestion, through the built `ingest_source` path. | `.inbox` has no lifecycle, no expiry, no sweep and no marker separating a rejected candidate from a pending one, and nothing above Layer 2 can see it (LS §14.4). Browsing thirty candidates would write thirty folders into a tree with no undo, and LS-16's cap exists for the same reason. |
| **AQ** | **Provenance becomes a recognised optional frontmatter field with a closed vocabulary**, added to `pkb.core` in the same commit as the first writer that emits it, with VA-32's hint amended so no model is advised to delete it. | P-36, P-37. The body-block alternative already exists (`## Provenance`, written by deterministic code, read back by `_recorded_origin`), and its limit is exactly the problem: the fact survives and no generator can act on it. The third pillar needs to change what a pack loads, and a pack reads `Metadata`. |
| **AR** | **An external capability is an MCP server and nothing else.** Web search included. No vendor SDK enters `pkb.agents`; the fetch half is `pkb.sources`, which is built, guarded and already accepts a URL. **Amended 2026-08-09 (search settled): search is the one exception, and it is the model provider's own client.** Original text, kept, and it still governs every capability the human binds to a topic: a recipe API, a market-data service, a domain tool. The clause that falls is *"Web search included"*, and execution rather than argument is what felled it. `ollama.web_search` and `ollama.web_fetch` sit in the venv today, arrive through `langchain-ollama`, need only `httpx` and `pydantic`, and answer in under a second (`P-50`, `P-52`, `F-27`). Binding them as an MCP server would mean writing a server, running a process and configuring a catalog entry to reach two function calls in a package the harness already imports for its models. The decision's own reasoning survives the exception: one mechanism, one failure mode and one configuration surface is exactly what one provider serving both the models and the search delivers, and `F-29` names the cost that comes with it. | One mechanism, one failure mode, one configuration surface. It also makes the day-one increment coherent: with no server bound, the loop runs over the knowledge base alone and needs no vendor and no key (RS-74, §8 increment 1). **Amended**: the day-one increment is now coherent for a better reason. Nothing waits on a vendor comparison, a signup or a second bill, because the account that already runs the models is the account that runs the search. Day one still lacks a key, and RS-77 keeps knowledge-base mode the keyless default. |
| **AS** | **The synthesis is prose only. Code renders the evidence table and every source line.** A citation marker that resolves to nothing outside the admissible set blocks the proposal. | F-16, F-14, and LB-18's precedent: the merge is deterministic code because a model asked to write it reports work that never happened. A sources section a model writes is the same object as a merge a model writes. |
| **AT** | **The Librarian never holds a topic's MCP servers, and multi-topic research produces no cross-topic file.** It fans research sessions out, one per expert, and merges by attribution. Each expert files inside its own subtree. | The human ruled the first half after push-back: the Librarian reaches topic data by fan-out, not by holding the topic's tools. The second half follows from RT-15 and README §2.2: the Librarian writes nothing into the knowledge base, and no topic owns a cross-topic file, so there is nowhere for one to live that does not break the subtree rule. |

### Decisions the human ruled on 2026-08-09, after the first draft

*Each one reframes work this document had already specified as bounded. `AU … AZ` continue the shared
sequence past Layer 5's `AL`.*

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|----------|-----|
| **AU** | **A channel is a session. They are one object, and `/close` ends both.** A channel holds one line of work: the goal, the research, the trials run elsewhere, the results reported back, and whatever the work turns out to be worth. Several channels may address one expert, each named for its goal (`Trading · Trend Signal` beside `Trading · Market Regime`). | The human's ruling, and the second half of their reasoning is the stronger one. A thread replays its whole history every turn, message count exactly `2n`, until 170,000 tokens force a compaction to a summary plus the last six messages. Two goals in one channel means every trend-signal turn re-reads the regime conversation and invites the model to blend them. On the human side, separate topics on a phone are manageable and one merged topic is not. |
| **AV** | **Two directives, `/learn` and `/close`, and `/new` and `/end` are gone.** `/learn` runs at any time and as often as the human likes; `/close` ends the session and the channel, offering `/learn` once on a channel where it never ran. | `/end` assumed a session ends where a file lands, which AU deletes. `/new` rotates a channel onto a fresh conversation, which splits a session in half now that a channel is a session; starting new work means opening a new channel. Mid-session `/learn` is what a months-long channel needs: something learned in week two is gone by the verdict if it was never captured, because the channel compacted. |
| **AW** | **No lifecycle taxonomy.** There is no "research session" against "experiment" against "line of inquiry". One object. Work starts as anything and becomes whatever it becomes, and the system never asks the human to declare in advance what a conversation will turn into. | An earlier draft carried three lifetimes and the human rejected it as furniture. A taxonomy asked at the start is asked at the moment the human knows least, and it is then enforced for weeks by code that has no way to notice it was wrong. |
| **AX** | **Push-back is constant and runs both ways, and the human can be wrong.** The expert objects during the work rather than in a retrospective, and it objects at `/learn` as well. The same checks run against a proposed lesson as against a research finding: one mechanism, not a second one. | The human's framing: work with the expert the way you would work with a human expert. If the human says the rub needs more sugar and their own note from March says sugar burned at that temperature, a good expert says so while they can still act on it. And if they say "file it as: sugar burns above 250" when trial two ran at 260 without burning, a good expert says that too, then files what they decide, because meaning is theirs to curate. |
| **AY** | **The knowledge base writes goal specs and never executes.** A session may produce a spec saying why the work is being done and what it should achieve, never how to build it. Something else picks it up and runs it, and results come back into the channel as ordinary conversation. | The human's ruling and the boundary that keeps this system one thing. A knowledge base that runs tasks acquires a scheduler, a runtime, a failure surface and a second source of truth about what happened, and it stops being the thing that holds what was learned. |
| **AZ** | **A session's durable record is a file, not the conversation.** The session file holds the human's own words verbatim as they arrive, and `/learn` distils from that file rather than from the message history. | Measured: a months-long channel compacts away its own early trials by the time the verdict lands. The large-source ingestion loop solved this already and its docstring is the argument: *"There is deliberately no second store of progress: a second source of truth about what was read is a second thing that can be wrong, and the one a human can check is the file."* |

### Decisions the human ruled on 2026-08-09, after the search settlement

*`BA` is the human's ruling on the folder name. `BB` and `BC` are the two rulings the Hermes
reconciliation forced, and both are answers to `F-34` and `F-36` rather than to anything Hermes
advertises. `BA … BC` continue the shared sequence past `AZ`.*

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|----------|-----|
| **BA** | **The extension folder is `sessions/`, and `research/` is gone.** In the human's words: *"Sessions are just long conversation that could be research, could be collaborative discussion, or just really anything."* The folder holds what a session filed, and a synthesis of things read is one of the shapes that lands there. | Naming a folder for one of the things a session sometimes does makes every other thing it does look like a mis-file. The rename costs nothing mechanical: `STRUCTURAL_DIRS` is `{references, notes, media, skills, sub-topics}` and neither name is in it, so Layer 1 classifies both as `FileRole.EXTENSION_ITEM` and every rule in §1.6 and §1.7 keeps its enforcement. `provenance` is what says which route a file's content took (RS-42), so the folder was carrying a claim a field already carries, and carrying it worse. |
| **BB** | **The prior on filing inverts Hermes's: "nothing to file" is the expected outcome, and no prompt in this design pushes toward producing.** `RS-140` states it and §1.13's exclusion rules give it teeth. | `F-34`. Hermes tells its reviewer *"A pass that does nothing is a missed learning opportunity, not a neutral outcome"*, and it can afford that because its skill library carries archive, snapshots and rollback. D6 says there is no undo here, and RS-105 already rules that most sessions file nothing. A prompt carrying Hermes's bias would fight two rules this document already settled, and it would fight them invisibly, because a proposal the human approves without reading looks identical to one they wanted. |
| **BC** | **Both Hermes guards are adopted, and both stay in code.** No amendment to bytes nobody loaded this turn (`RS-141`), and a write records its origin in the file so nothing autonomous edits what the human wrote (`RS-142`). | `F-36`. Hermes puts both in code rather than in its review prompt, on the same reasoning this document uses everywhere else: RS-17's standard is to make it structural, and C-38 already rules that no guarantee here is carried by a skill. The first guard closes the case RS-89 leaves open, since RS-89 checks the bytes that land and nothing checks the bytes they were derived from. The second is the answer to a question this design could not otherwise answer on a tree with no undo: an autonomous `/learn` and a human's own editor write into the same `notes/` folder, and nothing today tells them apart. |

### Decisions the human ruled on 2026-08-09, after the Hermes reconciliation

*`BD` is the human's ruling on `Q54` and on read-only. `BE` and `BF` are the two rulings `BD` forced
and the human left open. `BD … BF` continue the shared sequence past `BC`.*

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|----------|-----|
| **BD** | **`/learn` may propose a skill, a skill in the tree is writable, and placement follows specificity.** In the human's words: *"Skills dont have to be read only, there is no hard requirement for that"*, and *"Skill would live in the topic if it is topic specific, or in librarian root if it is process (like research) specific."* `Proposal.kind` gains a third member and `RS-143 … RS-153` carry the contract. | The human's ruling, and the obstacle `Q54` named is smaller than `Q54` claimed. `SK-3`'s read-only mount is package data inside an installed wheel, so it was never the thing a written skill would land in; what becomes writable is the knowledge base's own `skills/`, at the root and inside a topic, which `resolve_skills` already merges (`P-56`). `SK-5`'s permanent fork is a property of **shadowing a shipped name**, and a new name shadows nothing. `SK-17`'s invisibility to every index and tag artifact is the file class working as designed (`P-55`), and a skill reaches an agent through directory resolution rather than through an index. `Q54`'s option (d), a fourth file class, is what the answer no longer needs. |
| **BE** | **The skill filing is a third gated tool with its own reason, and a root skill is written by harness code on RT-18's allow-list.** `file_skill(session_id, proposal_id)` raises `GateReason.SKILL_PROPOSAL` with `("approve", "reject")`, and `research.py:_file_skill` becomes RT-18's fifth sanctioned writer. The Librarian gains no write capability. | `RS-146`, `RS-148`. The gate question answers itself from the built table: a write under any `skills/` folder gates today as `skill-overload` (`P-54`), so the human already agreed that a skill is more consequential than a note. The tool-keyed branch adds the framing `RS-116` argues for: a human should read *"a skill would change how this expert works"* rather than *"a lesson is ready to file"*. The root write follows `create_topic`'s precedent exactly, and `create_topic` is the precedent because it is the one mutation the Librarian's own design already permits: the model calls a gated tool, and code outside the permission layer performs the write. `RT-16`'s stated reason for the Librarian not writing is about a **note** needing the topic's skills, voice and `expert.md`, and a process skill needs none of the three, so the reasoning does not reach it. The rule refuses it anyway for two reasons `RT-16` does not carry: `RS-112` says the Librarian writes nothing and every guarantee in §1.11 leans on that, and `RS-119`'s fan-out means an expert authored the proposal even on a Librarian channel. |
| **BF** | **`provenance: researched` keeps its name under a folder named `sessions/`.** No enum value is renamed and `RS-42`'s four values stay `ingested`, `researched`, `practised`, `stated`. | The value names **the route the content took**, and the folder names **where a session filed**. `RS-46` already forbids cross-checking one against the other, and `RS-45` as amended already moved the mandate off the folder and onto the renderer. The pair is many-to-one by design: `RS-109` files a lesson out of the same session with `provenance: practised`, and `RS-45` files a synthesis with `provenance: researched`, both from a channel the human calls a session. Renaming the value after the container would collapse that pair and make `provenance` decorative on the one file class it was added for, which is `C-48`'s hazard word for word. The oddness a reader feels comes from expecting the folder to name the value, and decision `BA` is the ruling that took that job away from folders. `FM-16` and `VA-42` are unbuilt, so the rename would cost nothing mechanical, which is what makes this a decision about meaning alone. |

---

## 0. Conventions

Same severity convention as Layers 1 to 5. **Rule ids are stable**; a test that changes cites the
rule that changed. Every acceptance test says **no key** or **live**.

**"live" means a live model here**, as it did in Layers 1 to 4, plus in a handful of cases a
reachable MCP server. Both carry `@pytest.mark.live` and are deselected by default. The default
suite runs the whole loop against a scripted model and a fake toolset, opens no socket, and needs no
key.

### 0.1 Hard constraints inherited

- **I3**: nothing above Layer 1 writes a derived file, and nothing above Layer 2 writes under
  `kb_root` at all. The research loop is Layer 2. Its write is a **harness write**, not a model tool
  call, so it does not pass through `KbValidationMiddleware` and this document never claims it does:
  it calls `validate_content`, then the gate, then the bytes under `KbWriteLock`, then returns the
  touched paths so MW-18's flush runs (RS-20). That is `ingestion._write`'s order, verbatim, and
  RT-18's allow-list gains this loop as its fourth entry (RS-86).
- **RT-15**: an expert writes only inside its own topic subtree, enforced by
  `kb_permissions(topic_path)` and verified: a Cooking expert's write into `BBQ/` returned a
  permission error and no file appeared. A research file lands under `<topic>/sessions/`, inside the
  subtree, and nothing in this design widens the permission list. **Amended 2026-08-09 (sessions):
  the folder was `<topic>/research/`** (decision BA); the subtree argument is untouched by its name.
- **RG-21**: the model is a registry concern, and deployment configuration is never read from
  knowledge-base content. The MCP catalog and the bindings follow it exactly (RS-58, RS-59): a
  `mcp:` key in `topic.md` would let an agent's own write change the tools it holds, which is a
  privilege escalation dressed as a note.
- **RG-3 / RG-4 / SV-8**: the catalog scan compiles nothing, graphs compile lazily on first use and
  cache, and fifty threads build zero graphs. MCP attachment happens at the lazy build (RS-60) and
  changes none of those three facts.
- **D6**: there is no undo. Every gate, every hold and every refusal in this document exists
  because a wrong write cannot be taken back.

### 0.2 Package layout (extends arch §3, L2 §0.2)

```
pkb/
├── core/
│   └── frontmatter.py       # gains `provenance` in OPTIONAL_FIELDS and CANONICAL_ORDER (RS-42)
│   └── models.py            # Metadata gains `provenance: str | None` (RS-42)
│   └── validation.py        # gains the closed-vocabulary check; VA-32's hint amended (RS-43, RS-44)
├── packs.py                 # `<topic>/sessions/*.md` JOINs the research pack (RS-93), then provenance
│                            # ordering inside each role group (RS-56)
├── daemon.py                # reads OLLAMA_API_KEY beside PKB_TELEGRAM_TOKEN and hands it down as a
│                            # constructor argument. The only module in the repo that names it
│                            # (RS-133, RS-134)
├── sources.py               # every redirect hop guarded (RS-91). `stage()` is untouched, and
│                            # `fetch_text` is withdrawn: research reading no longer opens a socket
│                            # from this process, so RS-90's entry point has no caller left
└── agents/
    ├── research.py          # THE SESSION and THE LOOP. Code. No graph, no `task`, no agent id
    │                        # (RS-1 … RS-14 the enquiry, RS-96 … RS-126 the session it runs inside,
    │                        # RS-135 … RS-142 what it must not file and the two write guards,
    │                        # RS-143 … RS-153 the skill a session may propose. `_file_skill` is
    │                        # RT-18's FIFTH sanctioned harness writer).
    │                        # The module keeps the word `research`; the FOLDER it writes into is
    │                        # `<Topic>/sessions/` (decision BA)
    ├── researcher.py        # the bounded worker: model + read-only tools (RS-15 … RS-27)
    ├── search.py            # the two read-only tools the researcher calls, over an injected
    │                        # `ollama.AsyncClient` that already carries the Bearer header. Reads no
    │                        # environment variable and imports no `os` (RS-128 … RS-134)
    ├── mcp.py               # the client catalog, the bindings, the degraded build (RS-58 … RS-67)
    ├── gates.py             # GateReason gains RESEARCH_OUTPUT, SESSION_LESSON and SKILL_PROPOSAL,
    │                        # keyed by the file_research, file_lesson and file_skill tools; GateEnv
    │                        # gains research_render, which all three share
    │                        # (RS-50, RS-87, RS-88, RS-116, RS-146)
    ├── routing.py           # one new caller of the built `route` + FanOut: the /learn fan-out's
    │                        # scope and its per-expert brief (RS-119, RS-120). No new step in the
    │                        # routing workflow itself.
    ├── permissions.py       # one deny, anchored at the scratch root: /sessions/** is code's,
    │                        # not the model's (RS-82). NOT a suffix glob: `/kb/<Topic>/sessions/`
    │                        # is a knowledge-base path this deny must never reach.
    │                        # NOTHING here changes for skills: SKILLS_DENY_GLOBS is the packaged
    │                        # mount and the topic allow already reaches /kb/<Topic>/skills/**
    │                        # (RS-147, executed as P-53)
    └── runtime.py           # RuntimeConfig gains the catalog, the bindings and the budgets;
                             # PkbRuntime gains the async MCP warm-up (RS-60)
```

`research.py` is the sibling of `ingestion.py` and mirrors its shape on purpose: a loop driven by
code that asks a model bounded questions, writes as it goes, and keeps its resume state in a file a
human can read.

**Amended 2026-08-09 (sessions): "research" now names three things, and the fourth became
`sessions/`.** Original text, kept: *""Research" now names four things, and this is the only place
that says so." `pkb.packs`' `research_pack` (PK-9, breadth-first context for a project agent), the
shipped `research` skill (breadth-first exploration inside the tree), the new `research/` extension
folder, and the new `pkb.agents.research` module. The folder and the section keep the word because it
is the human's own word for what they asked for, and renaming their vocabulary to relieve a collision
in our module names is the wrong way round.* The last sentence was right about the principle and
wrong about the fact: the human's own word for the object is **session**, and `research` was this
document's word for it. Decision BA settles it. The three that keep the word are `research_pack`, the
shipped `research` skill and the `pkb.agents.research` module, none of which a human ever sees in
their tree. C-38 amends the skill and states which of its two halves is which.

**Two objects are now called a session file, and §1.1a separates them**: the **session record** in
thread scratch (RS-4), which no human reads through the knowledge base, and the **session summary**
under `<Topic>/sessions/` (RS-49), which is a knowledge file with frontmatter, a tag and a place in
a pack. Those are README §2.7's two names for the same two objects. The rules below say which one
they mean every time, and they call the summary a research file where the code does.

### 0.3 The measured facts this design is built on

Recorded once, cited by rule id afterwards. Strength labels are the grounding pass's own.

| # | Fact | Strength |
|---|------|----------|
| **F-14** | Deep-research agents fabricate 3 to 13% of citation URLs, and 5 to 18% of the URLs they emit do not resolve. In shipped generative search, 51.5% of generated sentences were fully supported by their citations. | measured |
| **F-15** | Models miscount positions and hallucinate spans, so an offset into a document is unreliable. Anthropic's Citations API extracts `cited_text` server-side rather than asking for a span. | reported |
| **F-16** | Anthropic runs a dedicated CitationAgent over the finished report rather than asking researchers to cite inline: attribution is a separate pass. | reported |
| **F-17** | Every shipped multi-agent research system converges on the same line: sub-agents read, the lead writes. Anthropic's lead prompt says *"NEVER create a subagent to generate the final report."* LangChain: *"Read actions are inherently more parallelizable than write actions."* | reported |
| **F-18** | A sub-agent needs an objective, an output format, guidance on tools and sources, and clear task boundaries. Skipping this is the documented cause of duplicated work: from a vague brief, *"one subagent explored the 2021 automotive chip crisis while 2 others duplicated work investigating current 2025 supply chains."* | reported |
| **F-19** | A sub-agent burns tens of thousands of tokens internally and returns 1,000 to 2,000. Compression is the contract. | reported |
| **F-20** | Anthropic's shipped lead prompt says *"default to using 3 subagents for most queries"*, and their agent-teams documentation converges: *"Three focused teammates often outperform five scattered ones."* The blog's 3 to 5 is the medium tier of a four-tier ladder. | asserted |
| **F-21** | Models abandon their own correct prior in favour of wrong retrieved content. ClashEval, 1,294 questions across six domains, context bias: 0.157 (Opus), 0.201 (Sonnet), 0.245 (Gemini 1.5), 0.264 (Llama-3), 0.304 (GPT-4o), 0.313 (GPT-3.5). | measured |
| **F-22** | Deep research agents are unreliable at revising a report across turns: they remove correct material without acknowledgement and introduce errors while refining. | reported |
| **F-23** | Nobody has published this design. Personalized Deep Research (SIGIR 2026) conditions sub-queries on a profile and never asks whether that narrows the evidence; NotebookLM excludes the web. An orchestrator that consults a private notes store first and uses it to bias the plan is undocumented, so its failure modes are the human's to find. | n/a |
| **F-24** | Stating a belief as prompt context suppressed disconfirmation detection by 16 to 93 percentage points across four models, over ~10,000 queries on 250 CVE patch pairs with code and instructions held identical. A consonant conversational search drove the confirmatory query rate to 42.92% against 15.57% neutral (CHI 2024, N=223, d=1.01), and the effect came from the querying rather than the answering. | measured |
| **F-25** | WikiContradict, 253 human-annotated pairs: with one non-conflicting passage models score 87 to 97%; with both contradictory passages supplied, GPT-4 scores 6.3% and Llama-3-70b 10.4%. Explicit instruction lifts Llama to 43.8% and GPT-4 to 10.4%, and almost all the gain lands on conflicts that were already explicit. | measured |
| **F-26** | Factual accuracy fell from 79% to 17% as tool calls rose from 2 to 150. 77 to 94% of steps in long-horizon search add no new evidence, and incorrect trajectories run 1.9 to 2.9 times longer than correct ones. | measured |
| **F-27** | **Search returns page content rather than a snippet.** Method, re-runnable: `ollama.Client(headers={"Authorization": f"Bearer {key}"}).web_search("reverse sear steak internal temperature resting", max_results=3)` on 2026-08-09, against the live internet. Three results, 22,448, 15,543 and 8,701 characters of `content`, in 0.9 seconds. `web_fetch("https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sous_vide")` returned title `Sous vide`, 17,591 characters and 3 links in 0.8 seconds. | executed (here), live |
| **F-28** | **A search call takes 0.9 seconds, against ~16 seconds for a whole cloud turn and 284 seconds for a local one.** Method: the two timings in F-27 against CLAUDE.md's measured ~16 seconds per cloud turn, 284 seconds per local turn and ~25 seconds per local model call, a turn being 8 to 12 calls. Twelve searches inside one researcher add about 11 seconds against the 900-second deadline RS-72 sizes for the local fallback, so no budget in RS-68 needs a search term and `researcher_max_steps` still bounds the model calls that read the results. **Corrected on review 2026-08-09**: the first draft of this row read *"about a twentieth of a model call on the cloud default and about a three-hundredth of one on the local fallback"*, which divided a search by a whole **turn** and named the denominator a **call**. Measured per call the local ratio is about a thirtieth and the cloud ratio is about a half, so the row overstated the margin tenfold on one side. The conclusion survives the correction and the deadline comparison above is what carries it. | executed (here), live |
| **F-29** | **One provider serves the models and the search, so one outage takes both.** The local `gemma4:31b` fallback exists for the moment the cloud is unreachable, and search is unreachable at that same moment. The offline story is therefore "no research, and an expert 18 times slower" rather than "research is slow". Method: read from the failover predicate in `pkb.agents.models` and from CLAUDE.md's measured 284 seconds against ~16. | executed (here) |
| **F-30** | **Whether search is included in the Ollama Pro plan or metered separately is unverified.** The documented pricing path 404s. Recorded as unknown rather than guessed, because a budget rule that assumes "included" and turns out wrong is a bill, and one that assumes "metered" and turns out wrong is a capability nobody used. RS-68's `research_search_results` bounds the calls either way, so no rule here depends on the answer. | unverified |
| **F-31** | **Hermes Agent has no session-end ceremony.** `agent_init.py:1798` sets `_skill_nudge_interval = 10` and `agent_init.py:1698` sets `_memory_nudge_interval = 10`: skill review fires at the end of a turn once ten tool iterations have accumulated, and memory review fires every ten user turns. `turn_finalizer.py:768-773` notes explicitly that `on_session_end()` is **not** called there. The human's `/learn` at close is therefore novel relative to Hermes, and no measurement Hermes publishes is evidence about a once-per-session write. | read (source) |
| **F-32** | **Hermes's reviewer is a separate forked agent with a capability floor**, rather than the conversing agent handed one more tool. `background_review.py:930-985` restricts it to memory and skill tools through a runtime whitelist, and hard-wires a deny on dangerous commands. Cost is about 30,000 tokens an event. | read (source) |
| **F-33** | **Hermes suppresses the review when no human was in the loop** (`turn_finalizer.py:750-757`), on the stated ground that *"cron sessions have no human-in-the-loop benefit from the review"*. | read (source) |
| **F-34** | **Hermes's review prompt is biased toward writing.** `background_review.py:182-186`: *"Be ACTIVE. Most sessions produce at least one skill update, even if small. A pass that does nothing is a missed learning opportunity, not a neutral outcome."* And `background_review.py:301`: *"'Nothing to save.' is a real option but should NOT be the default."* Hermes writes into a skill library carrying archive, snapshots and rollback, which is what makes that prior affordable there. | read (source) |
| **F-35** | **Hermes's exclusion list names five things a session produces that look like knowledge and are not.** Verbatim from `background_review.py:288-296` for the first: *"Unresolved failures: if the session ended WITHOUT actually finding a working method, you tried several things, none worked, and told the user to check manually, do NOT write those attempts up as a 'reliable workflow' or 'recommended approach'. That presents an untested sequence of failures as validated guidance a future session will trust and repeat."* The other four are environment-dependent failures; negative claims about tools, of which it says *"These harden into refusals the agent cites against itself for months after the actual problem was fixed"*; transient errors that resolved, of which it says *"If retrying worked, the lesson is the retry pattern, not the original failure"*; and one-off task narratives. | read (source) |
| **F-36** | **Two Hermes guards are enforced in code rather than in prose.** `skill_manager_tool.py:425-451` refuses a patch to a file whose current content the reviewer has not loaded verbatim in that turn: *"The autonomous review fork is allowed to evolve skills, but it must not patch or rewrite content it has only inferred from the transcript."* And `skill_provenance.py` tags every write with its origin so autonomous curation only ever touches what the autonomous process itself created: *"Skills a user asks a foreground agent to write belong to the user and must never be auto-curated."* | read (source) |
| **F-37** | **Hermes splits memory from skills, in its own words**: *"Agent-curated memory with periodic nudges. Autonomous skill creation after complex tasks. Skills self-improve during use."* Memory holds facts about the human; skills hold procedural knowledge in portable `SKILL.md` files compatible with agentskills.io. The split is the reconciliation this design has to answer: Hermes learns **how to work**, and this project learns **what is true**. | read (source) |

---

## 1. Rule table

### 1.0 Already ruled: restated so this document is self-contained

*Ruled by the human during the design conversation of 2026-08-09 and recorded in the large-source
ingestion spec §14.2 and §14.3. These are not re-litigated here.*

| ID | Rule (ruled 2026-08-09) | Sev |
|----|-------------------------|-----|
| RS-0a | **Packs put human content first.** An implementation pack carries every reference in full and gives the human's own material only `notes/summary.md` plus `type.solution` notes, so a plain `type.note` reaches neither pack. README §1.7 says human content always wins over static knowledge, so the shipped behaviour inverts the stated principle. The amendment lives in PK-9 and PK-10; RS-56 extends it with provenance ordering. | error |
| RS-0b | **A research summary gates before it lands.** Today `requires_approval` returns `None` for `<Topic>/notes/<anything>.md`: an AI-authored note arrives `status.draft` and nothing in Layers 3 or 4 surfaces a queue of drafts, so the marker is carried by a tag nobody reads. Research output is the material that becomes the human's prior, and they asked to see it first. RS-50 gives it a gate reason of its own. | error |
| RS-0c | **A rejected source is recorded in the research file.** LS-6 says a source that yields nothing leaves no trace in the topic, and that rule is right for its case: an expert declining fanned-out material is a cheap machine judgement that would be made the same way again. A human rejecting a candidate mid-research is a different act, and nothing records it today, so the next session surfaces the same article and the human declines it again. RS-53 and RS-54 build it. | error |
| RS-0d | **The Librarian holds no topic MCP servers.** It reaches topic data by fan-out, never by holding the topic's tools. The human accepted this after push-back. RS-64 and RS-65 enforce it at startup and at the graph build. | error |

---

### 1.1 The enquiry loop (RS)

*The template is `routing.py`. Each step is marked CODE or MODEL, and the marking is the rule: a step
marked CODE is not a tool the model may decline to call.*

**Amended 2026-08-09 (session model): this table is one enquiry, and an enquiry is not a session.**
It was written as the whole life of a session, opening at step 0 and closing at step 14 or 15. Under
AU the session is the channel: it opens when the channel opens, hosts as many enquiries as the work
needs, hosts the trials and the results that arrive between them, and closes on `/close`. Step 14
files, and the session carries on. §1.11 rules the outer object; §1.11's own table gives the session's
steps, which are ordinary conversation with three places where code intervenes.

**Amended 2026-08-09 (search settled): step 7 was `Fetch` and is now `Hold the bytes`.** The step
count, the actors and the CODE/MODEL markings are unchanged. Step 7 described a second retrieval
made after a search returned locators, which is the snippet design `P-50` deleted: search returns
page content, so step 7 records what step 6 already received. Steps 6, 7 and 9 gain the rules that
say so. No step was added, removed or reordered.

| Step | Actor | What happens | Rule |
|------|-------|--------------|------|
| 0 · **Ask** | HUMAN | states a goal to a topic expert on an ordinary thread | RS-1 |
| 1 · **Open** | MODEL | one call to `start_research()`, which takes no arguments; code reads the goal off the thread | RS-2 |
| 2 · **Select the prior** | CODE | the topic's own material off the snapshot, notes first | RS-35 |
| 3 · **Extract the prior** | MODEL | one bounded call; every claim is a quote, checked in code | RS-36 |
| 3a · **Answer without searching** | CODE | a goal the prior already covers ends the enquiry here, with the paths, and the session stays open | RS-84 |
| 4 · **Plan** | MODEL | directions from the goal alone, through a planner whose signature cannot reach the prior | RS-37 |
| 5 · **Brief** | CODE | one `ResearchBrief` per direction, boundaries built from the others | RS-21 |
| 6 · **Search and read** | MODEL (researcher) | a bounded loop with two read-only tools, `search_web` and `read_page`, and no write capability | RS-15 … RS-20, RS-128, RS-129 |
| 7 · **Hold the bytes** | CODE | each result's page content enters the fetch log as it arrives; nothing is re-fetched and nothing is staged | RS-29, RS-30, RS-127 |
| 8 · **Return** | MODEL (researcher) | one typed `ResearchReturn`, capped in code | RS-24 |
| 9 · **Admit** | CODE | quote-in-bytes check against the bytes search returned; a failure is held, never filed | RS-28, RS-31, RS-127 |
| 10 · **Weigh** | CODE, then a bounded MODEL label | pairs chosen in code; the model labels and never filters | RS-38 … RS-40 |
| 11 · **Checkpoint** | CODE → HUMAN | the tool returns the rendered bytes as the turn's last message and ends the turn | RS-8 … RS-12, RS-79 |
| 12 · **Direct** | MODEL | one call to `continue_research` or `end_research` | RS-13 |
| 13 · **Synthesize** | MODEL (the expert) | prose only; code renders every source line | RS-49, RS-32 |
| 14 · **File** | MODEL calls, CODE renders, the gate fires | `file_research(session_id)` carries no content; code renders the file and the human approves those bytes | RS-50, RS-86 … RS-89 |
| 15 · **Ingest** | CODE | a candidate id the human named at the last checkpoint goes through the built `ingest_source` loop | RS-55, RS-89 |

| ID | Rule | Source | Sev | Test assertion (live?) |
|----|------|--------|-----|------------------------|
| RS-1 | **A research session runs on the topic expert's ordinary thread**, addressed to the expert the human is already talking to. No new thread is minted, no new agent id exists, and no transport learns a new verb. `list_threads`, `get_thread` and every client render it as the conversation it is. **Amended 2026-08-09 (session model): the thread is the session, and the channel is the thread.** Original text, kept: *"A research session runs on the topic expert's ordinary thread"*, which read as an activity hosted by a conversation. Under AU the two are one object (RS-96): the channel opens the thread, the thread carries the session, and an enquiry is something the session does rather than something that owns it. Everything this rule promised holds unchanged, and the count in its test tightens: `create_thread` is called once, when the channel opens, and never again for the life of the work. | arch §4; SV-6; TU-10; decision AU | error | A session opened, checkpointed and closed leaves `create_thread` called zero extra times, and `/threads` shows one conversation. **no key**. *Why*: a session on its own thread is a conversation the human has to go and find, on a channel that already lists threads pending-first; the whole feature is a dialog with an expert, and moving it off that thread deletes the dialog. |
| RS-2 | **The opening is one model call whose entire output is a call to `start_research()`, and the tool takes no arguments.** The goal is read in code off the last human message on the thread; the model decides *whether* a session opens and never *what it is about*. A model may add `scope_note`, recorded beside the goal and never in place of it. | `routing.py` (built); verified: Ollama cloud ignores the `format` parameter; review 2026-08-09 | error | `inspect.signature(start_research)` carries no `goal` and no `directions` parameter; a session's `goal` is byte-identical to the last human message for every scripted model, including one instructed to paraphrase. **no key**. *Why*: structured output does not work on Ollama cloud (verified), so a tool call is the one typed channel every provider here carries. And a `goal` *argument* makes RS-2 hold only for a cooperative model: the first draft of this rule asked for byte-identity and gave the model the parameter to violate it. A parameter that does not exist cannot be filled in wrong. |
| RS-3 | **The session id is minted by code**, never by the model, and it is derived from the thread id and a monotonic counter rather than from `uuid`. Two sessions on one thread are distinguishable in the file and in the scratch state. **Amended 2026-08-09 (session model): the counter counts enquiries, and the session id is the thread's.** Original text, kept, including *"Two sessions on one thread"*, which AU makes impossible: one thread is one session. The id minted per enquiry is `<session-id>/e<n>`, and both halves are code's. | SV-10's discipline; LB-18; decision AU | error | Two enquiries in one session yield two distinct enquiry ids under one session id, and two blocks in the file. A model that emits either id has it ignored. **no key**. *Why*: a model-chosen id collides, and a collision merges two research questions into one file with no error anywhere. |
| RS-4 | **The session's durable state is a markdown file in the thread's own scratch filesystem** at `/sessions/<session-id>.md`, written through the backend's default route. Never `/kb/`, never `/skills/`, never a new table, never adapter memory. **Amended 2026-08-09 (session model): this file is the session's durable record for the whole life of the channel**, and it holds the human's own messages verbatim under `## Log` (RS-103) beside the enquiry blocks. Original text, kept: it described state for a session that lasted a few turns. RS-102 makes it the input `/learn` distils from, which is the reason it has to hold more than the loop's own bookkeeping. | executed (grounding): `/goal.md` survived `kill -9` and a daemon restart, read back byte for byte; `CompositeBackend` default = `StateBackend` (RT-6) | error | Write a session, kill the daemon, restart, resume: the session file reads back byte-identical and the loop continues from it. The session writer refuses any path outside `SESSION_ROOT`, and a session file appears in no `KbSnapshot`. **no key**. *Why*: a research session outlives a turn by design, and in-memory state means a supervised restart silently starts the human's next message in a session that has forgotten every candidate it already showed them. **Two limits, stated rather than assumed.** Durability is at superstep boundaries, because `durability` defaults to `"sync"` and the checkpointer persists there, so "survives a `kill -9`" means at a turn boundary and not mid-round. And the permission list does **not** enforce the location: `kb_permissions` is first-match-wins over a default of *allow*, so `/sessions/…` is legal scratch precisely because nothing denies it. RS-82 supplies the enforcement the first draft of this rule wrongly credited to the permission list. **Amended 2026-08-09 (sessions): the scratch root moved with the extension folder.** Original text, kept: the path was `/research/<session-id>.md` and `SESSION_ROOT` was `/research/`, which left a constant named for sessions holding a value named for one thing a session does. Decision BA renames the extension folder and this follows it, so one word names the object in both filesystems. Nothing mechanical depends on the string: no code exists yet, and RS-82's deny rule is written against the constant. **The one hazard the rename creates is named in RS-82**: the knowledge base now has a `sessions/` folder of its own, so the deny must stay anchored at the scratch root and never become a suffix glob. |
| RS-5 | **The session file is append-only across turns.** A later turn adds a block; it never rewrites an earlier one. The only mutation in place is a status marker on a candidate line, written by code. | F-22; LS-5's precedent (the reading record) | error | Ten turns leave ten blocks and every earlier block byte-unchanged. A **planted model write** to `/sessions/<session-id>.md` through `write_file` is refused (RS-82), which is the assertion that makes this rule hold against an uncooperative model rather than a scripted one. **no key**. *Why*: deep research agents remove correct material without acknowledgement while refining, so a rewritable dossier loses evidence the human already saw with nothing recording the loss. |
| RS-6 | **A session has exactly four states**: `planning`, `running`, `awaiting-direction`, `closed`. Every transition is made by code, and the state is a line in the session file rather than an inference from its contents. **Amended 2026-08-09 (session model): five states, and `open` is where a session spends most of its life.** Original text, kept: the four values above described a session that was always in the middle of an enquiry, which is the assumption AU deletes. A session is `open` from the moment the channel opens; `planning`, `running` and `awaiting-direction` describe an enquiry in flight and return to `open` when it ends; `closed` is reached by `/close` alone (RS-104) and by no other transition. The state is still one line in the file and still written by code. | `routing.py`'s explicit `ExpertStatus`; MW-*; decision AU | error | The state machine is exhaustive over the five values, a transition table test covers every legal edge, every illegal edge raises, and **no edge from any of the three enquiry states reaches `closed` without `/close`**. **no key**. *Why*: an inferred state is a second implementation of the truth, and the two disagree on exactly the run that crashed. Without `open`, a session between two enquiries has no legal state, so the code either invents one or reports the last enquiry's, and a human asking "what is this channel doing" is told it is researching a question that finished in week two. |
| RS-7 | **One open session per thread.** A `start_research` call while a session is `awaiting-direction` or `running` is refused with a message naming the open session and its question, and it starts nothing. **Amended 2026-08-09 (session model): one enquiry in flight per session.** Original text, kept. `start_research` no longer opens a session, because AU opens it with the channel; it opens an enquiry, and the refusal fires when an enquiry is `planning`, `running` or `awaiting-direction`. From `open`, a `start_research` is accepted however many enquiries the session has already run. | RT-39's shape; D6; decision AU | error | A second `start_research` during an enquiry returns a refusal the model can relay and the in-flight count stays 1; a `start_research` from `open` on a session that already ran three enquiries is accepted. **no key**. *Why*: two enquiries in flight on one channel give the checkpoint two meanings, and the human's "drop the second" then applies to whichever the code happened to read first. Refusing a *later* enquiry outright would be the other failure: it would make a channel a one-question object and force the human to open a new channel for every follow-up, which is the split AU exists to avoid. |
| RS-8 | **The checkpoint is an ordinary assistant message that ends the turn. It is never an `interrupt`, never a gate and never a `Decision`.** | P-35; RT-39; AR-10 | error | A checkpoint leaves `get_thread(...).pending is None`, the run reaches `RunEnd`, and a following human message on the same thread is accepted rather than 409'd. **no key**. *Why*: an approval blocks every other message on its thread, so *"those three look right, keep going, but drop the second"* is not expressible, and the human's only options become the ones in `allowed_decisions`. The whole point of walking together is that the human can say something the system did not offer. |
| RS-9 | **The checkpoint message carries five things and no verdict**: the goal as stated, each direction with its status and steps used, each admissible finding as one claim sentence plus its quote and its source, each surfaced prior pair with both quotes, and what the budget did. The system recommends nothing, and **the renderer has no template for a recommendation**. | F-17; the `research` skill's "options, not a recommendation dressed as a summary"; review 2026-08-09 | error | A golden checkpoint over a scripted session asserts all five parts present, and asserts the rendered text is composed **only** of `_CHECKPOINT_TEMPLATES`' constants, verbatim quotes, KB-relative paths and candidate ids: every character is accounted for by one of the four. **no key**. *Why*: a recommendation at the checkpoint is the system deciding, and the human asked to walk through the research rather than be handed its conclusion. "No ranking language" is not a predicate anything can evaluate, so the assertion is about the renderer's constants, which is the object the rule actually governs. |
| RS-10 | **The checkpoint text is rendered by code from the session record**, not written by a model. The claim sentences and the quotes are pasted verbatim from what the researchers returned and what the harness verified. | LB-18 (the merge is code); AQ | error | A checkpoint is byte-reproducible from the session file: rendering twice gives identical bytes, and no model call is made between them. **no key**. *Why*: LB-18's measurement applies word for word. A model asked to summarise the findings will report a direction that never ran, and the human then decides on evidence that does not exist. |
| RS-11 | **Every candidate at a checkpoint carries a stable candidate id** the human can name (`c3`, `c4`), rendered beside it. The ids are assigned in code, in return order, and never reused inside a session. | LB-18's attributed sections | error | Two checkpoints in one session never reuse an id; a `drop` naming `c2` drops the finding rendered as `c2`. **no key**. *Why*: the human says "drop the second", and without an id printed beside it "the second" is resolved by a model guessing at ordinal position in prose it did not render. |
| RS-12 | **A checkpoint fires after every round**, before any file is written and before any source is ingested. A session cannot reach a write without the human seeing what it found. | RS-0b; D6 | error | A scripted session that tries to file without a checkpoint fails; every filing path is reachable only from `awaiting-direction`. **no key**. *Why*: research output becomes the human's prior. A prior assembled and filed without them looking at it is the system teaching itself. |
| RS-13 | **Direction is one typed tool call: `continue_research` or `end_research`.** `continue_research` carries `keep: tuple[str, ...]`, `drop: tuple[Decline, ...]`, `new_directions` and `notes_for_next_round`; `end_research` carries `outcome` in `("file", "ingest", "abandon")` and, for `"ingest"`, `accept: tuple[str, ...]` of candidate ids. **`Decline` carries `candidate_id` and `reason`, and code copies the reason verbatim.** An absent reason files as empty; a model-composed one is a defect. Prose after a checkpoint changes nothing, and the session stays exactly where it was. **Amended 2026-08-09 (session model): `end_research` ends the enquiry and returns the session to `open`.** Original text, kept, including the outcome set, which is unchanged. The change is in what it ends: `"file"`, `"ingest"` and `"abandon"` all leave the channel alive and the work continuing, and `"abandon"` in particular is an enquiry the human dropped rather than a session they finished. Only `/close` closes a session (RS-104), and the tool is named `end_research` rather than `end_session` for exactly this reason. | `routing.py`'s `route` precedent; RS-2; RS-53; review 2026-08-09; decision AU | error | A prose reply after a checkpoint leaves the session `awaiting-direction` with its candidate set unchanged; a typed call moves it; a `Decline` reason reaches `## Declined` byte-identical to the argument, and an absent reason renders as an empty reason rather than as prose. **no key**. *Why*: a model that "understands" a direction and acts on it without a typed call is a model deciding what the human meant. RS-53 promises the human's own words in a file with no undo, and without a per-candidate reason field that promise is kept by the model's paraphrase presented in the human's voice. |
| RS-14 | **A session left in `awaiting-direction` for `SESSION_IDLE_TURNS` turns is closed by code**, marked `abandoned` in the session file with the round it reached, and the expert says so once. `SESSION_IDLE_TURNS` counts turns on the thread that carried no `continue_research` or `end_research` call. **Amended 2026-08-09 (session model): the idle timer ends the enquiry, never the session.** Original text, kept, including *"is closed by code"*, which meant the session and now means the enquiry: the state returns to `open`, the abandoned marker names the enquiry and the round it reached, and the channel carries on. **No turn count ever closes a session**, because a channel with nothing said in it for a month is a line of work waiting on a trial, and closing it would delete the human's own record of what they are in the middle of. | RS-6; LS's resume-state precedent; decision AU | warning | Three unrelated turns after a checkpoint end the enquiry, the file carries the abandoned marker, the session is `open`, and a `start_research` on the next turn is accepted; a session idle for a hundred turns is still `open`. **no key**. *Why*: an enquiry that quietly evaporates leaves the human believing research is still running and leaves the next enquiry with no record of what was already covered. RS-80 handles the other stuck state, `running`, which no turn count can clear because no code is left to count. |
| RS-79 | **The rendered checkpoint bytes are the turn's last message, and the tool ends the turn.** The checkpoint tool returns `Command(goto=END, update={"messages": [ToolMessage(...), AIMessage(render_checkpoint(session))]})`. No model call happens after it, and no model rewrites, summarises or prefaces the rendered text. | LB-18 and `PkbRuntime._deliver` (a code-authored reply ends the Librarian's turn rather than being handed back to a model); RS-10; review 2026-08-09 | error | The last message on the thread is byte-identical to `render_checkpoint(session)`; the model-call count after the tool returns is zero; a scripted model instructed to add a recommendation gets no step in which to add one. **no key**. *Why*: RS-10 renders the bytes in code and then the first draft handed them back to a model as a `ToolMessage`, which is the model re-authoring the checkpoint one inch before the human. That model can drop the candidate ids RS-11 needs, compress the five parts RS-9 requires, and add the recommendation RS-9 forbids, and it still holds `write_file` for one more step. The runtime already refuses this on the Librarian's path for the same measured reason. |
| RS-80 | **A session found `running` on the first turn after a restart is closed by code**, marked `interrupted` with the round it reached, and reported once. The thread is then free to start another. **Amended 2026-08-09 (session model): the enquiry closes as `interrupted` and the session returns to `open`.** Original text, kept. The wedge this rule exists to clear is the same one, and clearing it now costs the human nothing at all: their channel stays, their log stays, and the next `start_research` is accepted. | RS-6; RS-4 (durability is what makes this reachable); review 2026-08-09; decision AU | error | Write a `running` session, rebuild the runtime, send an ordinary message: the session closes as `interrupted`, the message is answered, and a following `start_research` succeeds rather than being refused by RS-7. **no key**. *Why*: RS-4 makes the state survive the crash, and surviving is exactly what wedges it. A daemon killed mid-round leaves `running` with no coroutine left to move it, RS-7 then refuses every later `start_research` naming an open session whose question the human never received, and the thread can never research again. |
| RS-81 | **An open session is re-announced before anything else on its thread.** The expert's first turn on a thread holding an `awaiting-direction` session re-renders that checkpoint through `render_checkpoint`, ahead of answering whatever the human actually said. | Q41; LS §14.4's finding that `.inbox` is invisible on every channel; review 2026-08-09 | error | Send an unrelated message to a thread with an open session: the checkpoint bytes appear again, identical to the first rendering, before the answer. **no key**. *Why*: §7 rules out a research view on any transport and the session lives in thread scratch, so "show me the research I have open" has no answer anywhere. Re-rendering costs nothing, needs no route, and turns Q41's cross-channel resumability into something a human can find rather than something they must remember the thread id of. |
| RS-82 | **The session file is writable by code and by nothing else.** `SESSION_ROOT` (`/sessions/`) is denied to the expert's own write tools by an explicit deny rule ahead of the default allow, and the session writer refuses any path that does not start with `SESSION_ROOT`. | RT-15's mechanism (`kb_permissions` is first-match-wins over a default allow); RS-5; review 2026-08-09 | error | A planted model `write_file` to `/sessions/<session-id>.md` is refused and the file is byte-unchanged; the session writer raises on `/kb/Cooking/x.md`; **a harness write to `/kb/Cooking/sessions/brisket.md` is permitted**, so the deny is asserted to be anchored rather than a suffix match. **no key**. *Why*: the permission list's default is *allow*, so thread scratch is writable by the model today. That is what makes RS-4's home free, and it is also what leaves RS-5's append-only property enforced by nothing but a cooperative fixture. `/sessions/**` is not a knowledge-base path, so denying it touches none of RT-15's reasoning. **Amended 2026-08-09 (sessions): the deny is anchored, and the reason is new.** Original text, kept, where `SESSION_ROOT` was `/research/` and no knowledge-base folder shared the word. Decision BA gives the knowledge base a `sessions/` extension folder, so a deny written as `**/sessions/**` rather than as a prefix on the scratch root would also match `/kb/<Topic>/sessions/<slug>.md` and refuse the very filing RS-87 exists to land. The acceptance test asserts both halves. |
| RS-83 | **While a session is open, `task` is withheld from the expert's model request.** The mechanism is `RouteMiddleware.wrap_model_call`'s: `request.override(tools=…)`, the same one deepagents uses for its own excluded tools. | LB-12 (built, and the same bypass one layer up); EX-11; RS-15; review 2026-08-09 | error | With a session `running` or `awaiting-direction`, `"task"` is absent from the tool names the scripted model is offered; with no session it is present. **no key**. *Why*: RS-15 claims a researcher is never reached through `task` "on any path", and the expert keeps `task` (LB-12 asserts it is present on every expert). EX-11 records that the auto-added `general-purpose` subagent inherits `permissions` and `interrupt_on` but not our validation middleware, so an expert told to research can spawn one that reads the web and writes into `/kb/<topic>/**` unvalidated. That is the exact object RS-17 and RS-20 forbid, reached by the exact mechanism RS-15 forbids, and the first draft's grep-based test could not see it. |
| RS-84 | **A goal the prior already covers closes the session before any searching.** When the prior extraction (RS-36) yields claims that answer the goal, the loop closes the session with the answer and the KB-relative paths it came from, writes no research file, and says why. **Amended 2026-08-09 (session model): it ends the enquiry and answers, and the channel stays open.** Original text, kept: *"closes the session before any searching"*. The answer is the same answer and the file count is the same zero; what changed is that the human is left in a live conversation where they can say "that note is out of date, search anyway" and open a second enquiry, rather than in a channel the system shut on them for asking something it already knew. | README §2.7 step 2; F-26 (a budget is a quality mechanism); review 2026-08-09; decision AU | warning | A scripted session over a fixture tree whose notes answer the goal files nothing, runs zero researchers, names the paths, and leaves the session `open` with a following `start_research` accepted. **no key**. *Why*: searching the internet for something already filed spends the budget and invites a page that contradicts the human's own note, which is F-21's context bias imported for no gain. This is the cheapest good outcome the feature has and the README promised it before any rule delivered it. |

---

### 1.1a The two names the rename separates

*Added 2026-08-09 (sessions). Decision BA renames one folder and creates one ambiguity, and this
sub-section is the whole of the answer to it.*

| The name | What it is | Where it lives | Written by | Ruled in |
|----------|------------|----------------|------------|----------|
| **the session record** | the durable record of one line of work: its state line, its `## Log` of the human's own messages, its enquiry blocks, its `## Declined` and its `## Filed` | `SESSION_ROOT` in the thread's own scratch filesystem, at `/sessions/<session-id>.md` | code, and nothing else | RS-4, RS-5, RS-82, RS-103, RS-123 |
| **the session summary** | a knowledge file the human approved byte for byte, carrying the seven required fields, a `type.*` tag and a `provenance` value | the knowledge base, at `<Topic>/sessions/<slug>.md` | code renders it, the human approves it, `_file_research` writes it | RS-49, RS-87, RS-89, RS-93 |

**The rule text below calls the summary a *research file*, and the code names keep the word too**:
`render_research_file` renders it, `_file_research` writes it, `research_pack` loads it. README §2.7
calls the same object the session summary and calls the scratch file the session record, so the two
documents use one name per object and the rules keep the identifiers their code carries.

Three properties keep them apart, and each one is asserted rather than described. The record reaches
no `KbSnapshot`, so no index, tag registry or pack can see it (RS-4). The summary validates under
`pkb.core` and the record never does. And the record's own path is denied to every model write tool
while the summary's path is denied to none of them, which is RS-82's anchoring point.

**A session with nothing to summarise writes neither.** RS-105 governs the summary and RS-4 governs
the record: the record lives in scratch, which no human browses and no artifact reads, and the
knowledge base gets no folder, no stub and no empty `sessions/`.

### 1.2 Who researches (RS)

| ID | Rule | Source | Sev | Test assertion (live?) |
|----|------|--------|-----|------------------------|
| RS-15 | **A researcher is never reached through deepagents' `task` tool**, on any path, for any direction. The fan-out is code: `asyncio.gather` over briefs under a semaphore, exactly as `FanOut` invokes experts. | P-34; LB-12, LB-15 (measured in this repo); decision AM | error | The researcher's bound tool list contains no tool named `task`; the model request the researcher issues carries no `task` tool (`RouteMiddleware._without_task` is the precedent); and with a session open the expert's request carries none either (RS-83). **no key**. *Why*: measured here, a model free to delegate did not, and then claimed the delegate had run. A `task` call is a decision the model may skip and a return the harness cannot verify; both properties are fatal for evidence. The first draft asserted this with a grep for the word `task` in two modules, which fails on correct code — `routing.py` itself carries `_TASK_TOOL: Final = "task"` and `_without_task` for exactly this purpose, and it misses the real hole, which is the expert's own `task` tool one level up. |
| RS-16 | **A researcher is not a registered agent.** No agent id, no `AgentDescriptor`, no entry in `list_agents()`, no thread, no `expert.md`, no checkpointer row, no reachability from HTTP, SSE, MCP or Telegram. | decision AM; RG-9; TG-1 | error | `list_agents()` is unchanged in length while three researchers are running; no thread row exists for any of them; a transport that asks for one gets `UnknownAgentError`. **no key**. *Why*: a researcher in the catalog is addressable from a phone, and a Telegram channel bound to something with no topic, no persona and no subtree is a mis-file with no undo. |
| RS-17 | **A researcher holds no write capability of any kind**, and this is enforced in permissions rather than stated in a prompt: its tool list contains no filesystem write tool, and its backend is constructed read-only. | F-17; `kb_permissions` (built); decision AM | error | The researcher's tool names are asserted against a fixed allow-list; a planted write tool fails the test; an attempted write through the backend raises. **no key**. *Why*: every shipped system says sub-agents read and the lead writes, and every one of them says it in prose. This repo can make it structural, and a prompt rule is one context compaction away from being forgotten. |
| RS-18 | **A researcher reads no knowledge-base file.** Its whole world is its brief and its external tools. The prior reaches it through nothing, not even indirectly. | F-24; RS-37 | error | A researcher whose fake toolset records every call makes zero reads under `/kb/`; the KB mount is absent from its backend. **no key**. *Why*: a researcher that can read the notes has the prior in its context, and the prior in the context of a querying step is the 16-to-93-point suppression F-24 measures. |
| RS-19 | **A researcher's failure is a failed return, never a raised exception.** A dead server, a refused fetch, an exhausted budget or a model error yields a `ResearchReturn` marked `failed` with the reason, and the round continues with the researchers that succeeded. It is **not** a `pkb.core.Finding`: a `Finding` carries a severity, a rule id and a KB-relative path and describes a content defect in a file, and a researcher failure has none of those. | LS-7's `FetchError`-to-refusal precedent; LB-15's declining experts | error | One of three researchers raising leaves two returns and one failed return; the checkpoint names the failure; the session state stays `running`. **no key**. *Why*: an exception from one direction aborts the superstep and takes the other two directions' work with it, and the human sees a crashed turn rather than two-thirds of an answer. |
| RS-20 | **The expert is the only writer, and one fixed order governs every write: `validate_content`, then the gate, then the bytes under `KbWriteLock`, then `kb_touched`.** Synthesis, filing and ingestion all happen on the expert's own path, inside its own subtree. A researcher's text reaches the tree only as a verified quote inside a file the expert proposed and the human approved. | F-17; RT-15; `ingestion._write` (built; its docstring calls the order "the whole safety argument"); MW-18; review 2026-08-09 | error | A full session's writes are attributed to the expert's graph; no write originates in `researcher.py`; the four calls happen in that order and in no other; the touched paths flow back through `kb_touched` so MW-18's flush runs. **no key**. *Why*: **the middleware does not intercept a harness write, and the first draft of this rule said it did.** `KbValidationMiddleware` intercepts *model tool calls* through `wrap_tool_call`; it never sees `ingestion._write`, whose own docstring calls itself "a harness write that bypasses the tool layer" and "a second sanctioned caller". Only the maintenance flush is reattached, and only because the tool returns `Command(update={KB_TOUCHED: …})`. Four named calls in a fixed order are implementable and testable; "goes through the middleware stack" is neither, and it would have been read as a guarantee nobody wrote. |

### 1.3 The brief and the return (RS)

| ID | Rule | Source | Sev | Test assertion (live?) |
|----|------|--------|-----|------------------------|
| RS-21 | **A brief is a frozen typed object built in code** (`ResearchBrief`, §5.1), carrying an objective, boundaries, a fixed output format, the named tools it may use, and its budgets. A model never composes a brief, and no field of one is free-form model text except the objective, which comes from the plan. | F-18 | error | Every field of a brief is asserted non-empty over a scripted plan; the type is frozen and slotted; a model that emits a brief-shaped blob has it ignored. **no key**. *Why*: the documented cost of a vague brief is duplicated work, and duplicated work spends the budget that bounds accuracy on the same ground twice. |
| RS-22 | **The boundaries of a brief are the other directions' objectives, verbatim.** Built by code from the plan, never authored per direction. | F-18 | error | With three directions, each brief's `boundaries` equals the other two objectives exactly. **no key**. *Why*: *"one subagent explored the 2021 automotive chip crisis while 2 others duplicated work investigating current 2025 supply chains."* Telling a researcher what the others are doing is the mechanical version of what a lead agent is otherwise asked to do politely. |
| RS-23 | **The output format is a fixed literal in code**, identical across every brief in the deployment, and it names the return type's fields. | F-18; LB-18 | error | Two briefs in different sessions carry byte-identical `output_format`. **no key**. *Why*: a per-direction format is a per-direction parser, and the parser that is wrong is the one nobody wrote a test for. |
| RS-24 | **The return is compressed by contract**: `max_chars` per return, enforced in code by cutting on a finding boundary and recording the cut, never by asking the model to be brief. A researcher over budget returns its findings up to the cap with `truncated=True`. | F-19 | error | A researcher returning 40,000 characters yields a return at the cap, on a finding boundary, with `truncated=True`, and the checkpoint says so. **A single finding longer than `max_chars` is kept whole**, `truncated=True`, and the overrun is recorded: cutting on a boundary would otherwise return zero findings, which is indistinguishable from a researcher that found nothing. The acceptance case is one 40,000-character finding. **no key**. *Why*: a sub-agent burns tens of thousands of tokens and returns one to two thousand; without a cap in code the lead's context fills with three researchers' internal monologues and the compaction that follows drops evidence nobody chose to drop. |
| RS-25 | **A return names its gaps.** `gaps` is a required field, and an empty tuple means the researcher asserted full coverage of its objective. The checkpoint renders gaps beside findings. | the `research` skill ("say what is not covered"); F-21 | warning | A scripted return with gaps renders them; a return that omits the field fails parsing. **no key**. *Why*: a gap the human can see is useful; a gap papered over with a plausible answer is a liability, and the second one is what a research summary reads like by default. |
| RS-26 | **Returns are parsed in code with the same tolerance `parse_takes` has**, and an unparseable return is a failed return (RS-19), never a retried one. | `ingestion.parse_takes` (built); RS-19 | error | A malformed return yields `failed` with the raw text preserved in the session file. **no key**. *Why*: a retry loop on a malformed return is an unbounded budget, and the model that emitted prose once emits it again. |
| RS-27 | **A round's returns are merged in code by direction**, one section per direction that ran, named by its objective and its direction id, with its findings verbatim. Never a second model call. | LB-18 | error | The merged round is byte-reproducible and contains one section per direction, including failed ones. **no key**. *Why*: LB-18 exactly. A merge written by a model reports a direction that never ran, and at a checkpoint the human then approves a direction into the next round on the strength of results that do not exist. |
