(b) is defensible for a long overnight session on a slow local model, and it needs one config field plus a rule that the file records every round the human did not see. | One flag and one rule. Getting it wrong the (c) way produces a research file whose provenance says `researched` and whose real provenance is unattended, which is the exact distinction RS-42 exists to make. |
| **Q41** | **Should a research session be resumable across channels?** | (a) yes, by construction, because the session lives in the thread; (b) explicitly TUI-only. | **(a)**, and it is free: RS-4 puts the session in the thread's scratch filesystem, and D3 already makes threads shared across channels. A checkpoint reached in the TUI is answerable from Telegram with an ordinary message, which is exactly what an approval-based checkpoint would have made impossible (P-35). | Zero code. Worth stating because the Telegram adapter renders assistant messages and needs no change to carry a checkpoint, which is a claim someone will otherwise want to verify by building something. |
| **Q42** | **Does a research file's slug come from the goal or from the human?** | (a) slugified from the goal, deduplicated in code; (b) the human names it at the gate; (c) the model proposes and the human approves at the gate. | **(a)**, with the human free to rename through the ordinary path afterwards. The resolver is **`resolve_note_path`** (RS-125) and it is new: `resolve_slug` resolves a folder under `<topic>/references/` and matches on a recorded origin, which a rendered file does not have, so the first draft of this row credited a function that would have suffixed against reference folder names. (b) adds a question to a gate that already asks the important one. | One function call. Naming is reversible in a way content is not, so this is the cheapest question on the list. |
| **Q43** | **How long does a declined URL stay declined?** | (a) forever, read from every research file in the topic and every session file on the thread; (b) with an expiry; (c) per session. | **(a)** (RS-54), because the candidate is marked rather than dropped, so a stale rejection costs the human one labelled line rather than a lost result. (b) needs a date comparison and a rule about what expiry means; (c) reproduces the exact failure RS-0c was ruled to fix. | One read path. If the labelled group grows unpleasantly long on a well-used topic, capping the rendering is a display change and not a rule change. |
| **Q44** | **May a topic's expert hold a domain tool server on an ordinary turn, with no research session open?** | (a) yes for an `expert`-use server, no for a `research`-use one (RS-94), with the notes path refused on any turn that touched external content (RS-95); (b) no, every external tool reaches researchers only; (c) yes for all servers. | **(a).** The human's design point 3 is a narrow case that should stay in one conversation: "adjust this smoking recipe for the grill in my garage" is one topic's tools, one topic's expert, no session. (b) deletes that and makes the human open a research session to ask a recipe API one question. (c) is what the first draft implied and it puts a search tool on every ordinary turn, which routes around all of §1.4. The `use` field plus RS-95's notes refusal is what makes (a) safe rather than merely convenient. | One field on `McpServerSpec` and one middleware check. Choosing (b) later removes a capability the human asked for by name; choosing (c) later reopens P-44 with internet text. |
| **Q45** | **Does accepting a source at a checkpoint need its own gate?** | (a) no: acceptance is the human's typed direction, and RS-89's candidate-id check is the enforcement; (b) yes, a `RESEARCH_SOURCE` gate reason on the ingestion that follows. | **(a)** (RS-89). The human has just typed `ingest c3` against a candidate whose claim, quote and URL were printed a message earlier, so a gate fired one message later asks the same question twice and teaches them to answer gates without reading. What makes (a) safe is structural rather than conversational: every id in `accept` must have been printed at the **last** checkpoint, and the URL comes from the session's fetch log rather than from the model, so the model cannot ingest a page nobody was shown. (b) is one enum member and one branch if a human finds themselves surprised by what landed. | One gate reason. Reversible in either direction, and the README says "the human accepts the source" either way. |
| **Q46** | **How does Layer 5 learn that `/close` closed the session, so it can close the topic?** The decision is Layer 2's and the topic is Layer 5's. | (a) one typed run event carrying `session_closed`, emitted by the loop and relayed over the existing SSE stream, which the adapter reads the way it reads every other event; (b) the adapter parses a code-rendered marker line out of the reply; (c) the adapter calls a new service method after every turn to ask. | **(a).** The event stream already exists, already carries typed events per run, and already reaches both clients, so the bit travels the route every other bit travels. (b) makes a rendered string into an interface, which RS-52 already names as the failure mode a reworded heading causes. (c) adds a call on every turn to answer a question that is false almost always. | One event kind at Layer 3 and one branch at Layer 5. Getting it wrong the (b) way is a channel that stays open after `/close` the first time somebody rewords a line, with the human believing the work is closed. |
| **Q47** | **Is `/learn` reachable outside Telegram?** RS-100 recognises a literal, and the TUI has no command surface for it. | (a) yes: the literal is transport-free, so typing `/learn` in the TUI works today and Layer 4 gains a shortcut later; (b) a Layer 4 command that sends the literal; (c) Telegram only. | **(a).** The directive is a message, so every client that sends messages already sends it, and `pkb.clients` stays transport-free and UI-free as CLAUDE.md requires. (b) is a one-line addition whenever somebody wants the shortcut. (c) would make the durable-record rule reachable from one channel. | Zero code for (a). Worth ruling because a reader will otherwise assume `/learn` is a Telegram feature and build a second path for the TUI. |
| **Q48** | **Does a channel's goal ever change?** A line of work drifts, and the title was written on day one. | (a) the human renames the topic, the goal string in the session is untouched, and the session keeps the goal it opened with; (b) a directive rewrites the goal; (c) the goal is re-read from the title on every turn. | **(a).** TG-78 already leaves a human's rename alone and binds by topic id, so a rename costs nothing and breaks nothing. The recorded goal is what the enquiries and the log were gathered against, so rewriting it would relabel evidence collected under a different question. (b) is a third directive for a case a new channel handles better. | One field's mutability. Choosing (b) later is additive; choosing (c) would make the session's record depend on a string Telegram lets anybody edit. |
| **Q49** | **How much of the log does `/learn` read on a nine-month channel?** RS-102 reads the file and the file has no bound. | (a) the whole log, with `LEARN_MAX_PROPOSALS` bounding the output rather than the input; (b) the last N entries; (c) a windowed read like the large-source loop's. | **(a) for v1** (RS-118). The log is the human's own words at `SESSION_LOG_MAX_CHARS` a turn, so a hundred turns is under 400 kB and one distillation call reads it. (c) is the right answer the day a channel outgrows a context window, and the large-source loop already implements the windowed reader to copy. (b) reintroduces the exact loss RS-102 exists to prevent, one layer down. | One read path, and the windowed version is a drop-in. State the bound honestly: at the current defaults this holds to roughly the size of one context window and needs (c) after that. |
| **Q50** | **Does a Librarian `/learn` invoke each expert twice?** RS-119 classifies, RS-112 asks what each expert takes, and RS-113's filing is a tool call on the expert's own derived thread. | (a) twice: once to answer the takes grammar, once to file what the human kept; (b) once, with the expert holding its proposal in thread state across the human's answer; (c) once, with code filing on the expert's behalf. | **(a)** (RS-113, RS-120). The human's keep-or-drop lands between the two, so a single invocation would have to survive an interval measured in minutes or days, and (b) makes the expert's thread state the thing that has to survive a restart for a decision the human has not made yet. (c) is refused for RS-87's reason: a harness write cannot raise an approval, and the filing has to be a model tool call for the gate to exist at all. The cost is honest: four kept notes cost four extra expert turns, and each one carries the brief RS-120 already wrote. | One extra invocation per kept note. Choosing (b) later is an optimisation over the same rules and needs no id to change. |
| **Q52** | **What changes if search turns out to be metered per call?** `F-30` records the plan question as unverified and the docs path 404s. | (a) nothing: `research_search_results` (3) times `research_max_directions` (3) times `research_max_rounds` (3) already caps a session at 27 searches, and the human sees the count; (b) a per-session search budget with its own name; (c) a cost estimate rendered at the checkpoint. | **(a).** The bound exists and it binds. Twenty-seven searches is the worst case for a session that runs every round to its limit, which `F-26` already argues is the session going wrong for other reasons. (b) adds a fourth budget over a fifth of an existing product, and its acceptance test is the same test. (c) needs a price this design cannot read and would render a number that goes stale without anything noticing. | Zero code for (a). If the answer arrives and it is expensive, (b) is one `RuntimeConfig` field and one hard stop, which is RS-68's existing shape. |
| **Q53** | **Does `/health` report search reachability, or only whether a credential is configured?** RS-134 rules the second and RS-131 refuses the poller that would produce the first. | (a) configured or not configured, and nothing else; (b) a cached last-call outcome with its timestamp; (c) an active probe on every `/health` request. | **(a)** (RS-134). Reachability with no poller is last-known state, and last-known state on a system that calls search a few times a day is a green light from Tuesday. (c) makes a health check spend a paid call and makes `/health` fail when a vendor fails, which RS-61 already refuses for MCP for the same reason. (b) is honest and additive the day somebody wants it, and its cost is one field the loop already knows. | One field. (b) reuses the outcome RS-131 already records per enquiry, so choosing it later reads a value rather than adding a call. |
| **Q54** | ~~**May `/learn` ever propose a *skill* rather than a note?**~~ **Resolved 2026-08-09 by the human: yes, and skills in the tree are writable.** Kept with its resolution rather than deleted, for `C-47`'s reason. `F-37` is the reconciliation this question came out of: Hermes learns **how to work** and writes procedure into a skill library; this design learns **what is true** and writes knowledge into a tree. A session that establishes brisket holds at 250 is the second. A session that establishes a better way to run a session is the first, and this design has no path for it. | (a) never: `/learn` proposes knowledge, and a better way of working reaches the system when the human edits a skill by hand; (b) yes, a proposed skill lands in `<kb>/skills/<name>/` through the built `adopt_skill` path, gated like every other filing; (c) yes, but only as an amendment to a skill the human has already adopted, so the proposal never creates a fork; (d) a fourth home: a skill class that is neither shipped package data nor an adopted fork. | **(b) as amended, ruled by the human, and the encoded default was the losing option.** In their words: *"Skills dont have to be read only, there is no hard requirement for that"*, and *"Skill would live in the topic if it is topic specific, or in librarian root if it is process (like research) specific."* Decision `BD` carries the ruling and `RS-143 … RS-153` carry the contract. **The amendment to (b) is that the route is not `adopt_skill`**: a written skill is a new file rather than a copy of a shipped one, so `SK-4` never runs and `SK-5`'s fork applies to the name-collision case alone (`RS-150`, `C-57`). **Three of this row's four stated obstacles were wrong, and each was wrong in a checkable way, which is why `P-53 … P-56` print their calls.** `SK-3`'s read-only mount is package data inside an installed wheel, so it was never where a written skill would land; what becomes writable is the knowledge base's own `skills/`, and a topic expert already writes its own (`P-53`). `SK-5`'s permanent fork is a property of shadowing a shipped **name** rather than of writing a skill (`C-57`). `SK-17`'s exclusion from every index and tag artifact is the file class working as designed, and an expert reaches a skill through directory resolution rather than through an index (`P-55`). The one obstacle that held is the one the row put last and called *"a design, not an option"*: something had to say where a written skill lives, and the human's own rule turned out to be `resolve_skills`'s merge order (`P-56`, `RS-145`). Option (c), amendment-only, survives as half of `RS-149`, which permits an amendment to a skill the session itself wrote and refuses one to a skill the human wrote. Option (d) is what the answer no longer needs. **Original recommendation, kept**: *"(a) for v1, and the default is encoded structurally rather than by prose*: `Proposal.kind` is `Literal["lesson", "summary"]` (§5.2) and a third member is the whole of the change, so nothing can propose a skill by accident and adding the path later is one enum member plus a renderer. **Name the obstacle, because it is the reason this is a question rather than a rule.** SK-3 mounts the shipped skills read-only from package data, ahead of the knowledge base's own `skills/`, which stays empty until the human adopts one. SK-4 makes `adopt_skill` the only route into the tree and SK-5 makes adoption a **permanent fork**: the copy shadows the packaged default forever and later shipped improvements stop reaching it. SK-17 excludes `skills/**` from every index and every tag artifact, so a self-written skill is invisible to everything this document uses to find things. A self-written skill therefore has nowhere to live that is not a fork of something the human never asked to fork, or a fourth file class nobody has designed. **The seed of the other half already exists and is worth naming**: the shipped `voice` skill holds a voice profile every draft is checked against, and it is the one place this system already knows something about the human rather than about cooking. (b) is the honest shape if the answer is yes, and its cost is SK-5's fork on a file the human never chose. (c) is the cheapest yes and it reaches nothing until the human has adopted a skill. (d) is a design, not an option, and it belongs in the Layer 2 spec rather than here."* | **Settled, so the radius is a build cost rather than a risk.** One `Proposal.kind` member, one `scope` field, one tool, one `GateReason`, one path resolver, one origin record and a fifth entry on RT-18's allow-list. **Zero changes to `pkb.core` and zero changes to the permission list** (`P-53`, `P-55`), which is the half of the estimate the original row could not make, because it read the obstacles as design work. The residual risk moves to where `RS-151` puts it: a skill approved once is read on every later turn in its scope, and the human sees it again only if they go looking. |
| **Q55** | **May a session ever propose an amendment to a skill the human wrote?** `RS-149` refuses it in code, on `F-36`'s boundary, and the human's own skills are the ones most worth improving. | (a) never, and the expert says so in the channel; (b) the proposal renders as prose in the channel, quoting the current text and the suggested change, and files nothing; (c) a proposal like any other, with the gate carrying the diff. | **(a) for v1, with (b) named as the cheap upgrade.** `RS-149` refuses the write and nothing stops the expert saying *"your `voice` skill says X and the last four drafts went the other way"*, since that is `RS-106`'s push-back on a different file class. (b) is honest and additive: it needs a renderer and no new write path, so it never touches the guard. (c) is refused, because `F-36`'s own sentence is the case it creates, and the human's skill is the file where a wrong autonomous edit is least visible: nobody reads a skill, they experience it. | One renderer for (b). Choosing (c) later would need the origin record to say something it cannot, since a file the human wrote carries no `ORIGIN.md` by construction. |
| **Q56** | **Does a `root`-scoped skill need a stricter gate than a `topic`-scoped one?** It changes every expert in the tree, and both scopes raise the same `SKILL_PROPOSAL`. | (a) one gate for both, with `RS-151`'s scope line carrying the difference; (b) a fourth `GateReason` for the root scope; (c) two approvals for the root scope. | **(a).** The decisions are identical, the bytes are identical in kind, and the difference is a fact about reach that a sentence states better than an enum member. (b) buys one word in a slug and costs a fourth branch on a table whose whole property is one reason per tool. (c) is a second approval on a thread that `RT-39` already blocks during the first, so the human would answer the same question twice with no new information between the answers. | One enum member and one branch. (b) stays available if a human reports approving a root skill they thought was topic-scoped, and the scope line is the thing to check first if they do. |
| **Q57** | **What checks a proposed skill's body?** `VA-6` checks `name` and `description`, `SK-15` checks that it will load, and nothing checks that the text is a procedure worth following. | (a) nothing further: `RS-144`'s kind check, `RS-152`'s load check and the human's approval of exact bytes; (b) a rubric call over the drafted body; (c) a shipped `skill-proposal` skill whose text is the rubric. | **(a) for v1, and (c) is what increment 2c ships anyway.** The skill-proposal skill is one of the three files §8 owes, so the drafting guidance exists and lives where a human can edit it, which is (c) reached as a build item rather than as a check. (b) is a second model call judging a first model's prose with no ground truth, and `F-25` is the measurement of what that is worth on this deployment. `C-38`'s rule bounds (c): the skill drafts, and every guarantee stays in code. | Zero for (a). (b) is additive and would need a golden set nobody has, which is the honest reason it is not the default. |
| **Q58** | **Who owns the scan axis that reads a filed skill?** *Added 2026-08-09 (skills reconciliation).* README §1.7 gained a fifth conflict axis, the topic's notes against the skills that topic loads, reached by path because `GE-15` and `VA-6` keep a skill out of every index and tag artifact. It is the only reader in the system that revisits a skill nobody opened, and no rule in any layer carries it. | (a) the `conflict-detection` skill and the Layer 2 rules that own the scan, amended when the scan queue is wired; (b) a rule in this document, since this document is what made written skills possible; (c) nothing, and the axis stays README prose. | **(a).** The scan is not this feature: `RS-151` bounds what a `/learn` filing may propose and stops at the gate, and every axis of the scan is owned where the scan is owned. (b) would put one axis of a five-axis scan in the research spec, where a later reader amending the scan would never look for it. (c) is how README §2.4's unwritten skills happened, and `P-57` is the cost of that. **The axis is also weaker than its own README paragraph implies**, and the note belongs with whoever takes it: a skill written as procedure states no claim, so no note can contradict it, and the axis reaches only the subset of skills that assert something about the subject. | Nothing in this build order. One row in the Layer 2 rules on the day the scan queue is wired, and the wiring itself is already a known gap (README §2.8, *Three gaps*). |
| **Q59** | **Does the procedural pillar get a breadth file?** *Added 2026-08-09 (skills reconciliation).* README §1.6 gives the theoretical and practical pillars a human-approved `summary.md` each, README §2.8 records that the human asked for the third, and `VA-6` makes every file under a `skills/` folder a skill file with no PKB frontmatter and no place in any index. | (a) no breadth file, and `topic.md` gains a skills section; (b) `skills/summary.md` as a fourth file class with its own frontmatter rules; (c) a generated file, which gives up the approval that makes a breadth file worth reading. | **(a), and the default is already encoded by `VA-6` doing nothing.** Executed on a scaffolded tree: `validate_content(root, "Cooking/skills/summary.md", …)` returns `[]`, because the skill class exempts everything under that folder, and `validate_tree` then returns one `LEGACY_SKILL_LAYOUT` **warning**, because a flat markdown file inside `skills/` is the layout `PA-14` superseded and loads as no skill. So (b)'s file is today a file nothing validates as knowledge, nothing indexes, nothing tags and nothing loads, and choosing it means writing a fourth class rather than relaxing a rule. (a) needs no class, no exemption and no generator, and the approval it gives up is one the human already gives on `topic.md`. (c) is refused on README §1.6's own ground. | `pkb.core` if the human picks (b): a fourth file class, its own validation branch and its own exclusion from two generators. Zero if they pick (a). |
| **Q51** | **Does an unregistered tag in a proposal refuse the filing, or get named and carried?** RS-50's tool key skips `NEW_TAG`. | (a) named on its own line in the proposal message and again in the gate's diff (RS-124); (b) refused before the gate, so a lesson can never introduce a tag; (c) nothing, and the diff is the only place it appears. | **(a).** (b) is the stricter reading and it makes the one path that files a lesson unable to grow the registry, against README §1.5's ruling that the AI proposes new tags and the human approves them; the human would have to register a tag by hand before a lesson could use it, on a phone. (c) is what the tool key gives for free and it is the case C-50 exists to close. | One rendering line and one check. (b) is available later as a config-free tightening if a human finds tags arriving they did not read. |

---

## 5. The exact contract

### 5.1 `pkb.agents.researcher`: the brief, the return, the worker

```python
# NO agent id, NO thread, NO `task`, NO write capability, NO KB read (RS-15 … RS-20).
# The worker is a coroutine, not a compiled graph: `ingestion.model_asker` is the precedent for
# reaching a model directly rather than through `create_deep_agent`.

@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class ResearchBrief:
    """Built in code, never composed by a model (RS-21). Every field is F-18's checklist."""
    session_id: str
    direction_id: str            # "d1", "d2": stable inside a session (RS-11's sibling)
    objective: str               # ONE question, from the plan
    boundaries: tuple[str, ...]  # the OTHER directions' objectives, verbatim (RS-22)
    output_format: str           # a fixed literal, identical in every brief (RS-23)
    toolset: tuple[str, ...]     # the MCP server names this direction may use (RS-59)
    max_steps: int               # RS-68
    max_chars: int               # RS-24, the compression contract, cut in code
    no_progress_steps: int       # RS-71
    deadline: float              # seconds, sized for the fallback model (RS-72)

@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class Evidence:
    quote: str          # VERBATIM. Never an offset, a page number or a line range (RS-30)
    source_url: str     # a URL THE HARNESS FETCHED in this session (RS-29)
    retrieved: date
    claim: str          # one sentence, the researcher's own words

@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class ResearchReturn:
    direction_id: str
    status: Literal["ok", "stalled", "failed"]     # RS-19, RS-71
    evidence: tuple[Evidence, ...]
    gaps: tuple[str, ...]                          # required; empty means full coverage (RS-25)
    steps_used: int
    truncated: bool                                # RS-24
    reason: str | None                             # set iff status != "ok"

async def research_direction(brief: ResearchBrief, toolset: McpToolset,
                             search: SearchClient | None, model: BaseChatModel) -> ResearchReturn: ...
    # Never raises for a research failure: every failure becomes `status="failed"` (RS-19).
    #
    # Amended 2026-08-09 (search settled): the third parameter was `fetcher: Fetcher`, which was
    # `pkb.sources`' injectable fetcher, and RS-90 withdrew the entry point it reached. It is now
    # `SearchClient` (§5.3a), and it is OPTIONAL because `None` IS knowledge-base mode: a researcher
    # built without one holds neither `search_web` nor `read_page`, which is RS-75's enforcement
    # made structural rather than branched. `pkb.sources` is off this path entirely (RS-130).
```

### 5.2 `pkb.agents.research`: the loop

```python
SESSION_ROOT:        Final = "/sessions/"  # thread scratch, NOT /kb/ and NOT /skills/ (RS-4).
                                            # Renamed with the extension folder (BA). The deny rule
                                            # is ANCHORED here and is never a suffix glob, because
                                            # `/kb/<Topic>/sessions/` exists now too (RS-82)
MIN_QUOTE_CHARS:     Final = 40             # identical to LS-13's threshold (RS-28)
NO_PROGRESS_STEPS:   Final = 3              # RS-71
MAX_PAIRS:           Final = 24             # RS-39
PAIR_OVERLAP_MIN:    Final = 0.15           # Jaccard over content tokens (RS-38)
SESSION_IDLE_TURNS:  Final = 3              # RS-14: ends the ENQUIRY, never the session
PAIR_STOPWORDS:      Final = frozenset({...})            # a literal, never a download (RS-38)
CHECKPOINT_GROUP_ORDER: Final = ("bears-on-prior", "no-prior", "unverified", "declined")  # RS-41

# The session's own budgets and its two literals (RS-118)
SESSION_LOG_MAX_CHARS: Final = 4_000        # per human message, cut and recorded (RS-103)
MAX_TURN_PAIRS:        Final = 6            # push-back during the work (RS-106)
LEARN_MAX_PROPOSALS:   Final = 8            # one `/learn` proposes at most this many (RS-113)
LEARN_DIRECTIVE:       Final = "/learn"     # matched EXACTLY, in code, before any model call
CLOSE_DIRECTIVE:       Final = "/close"     # RS-100, RS-104. No `/new`, no `/end` (decision AV)
LOG_HEADING:           Final = "## Log"     # read back by code, so it is an interface (RS-103)
FILED_HEADING:         Final = "## Filed"   # read back by `propose_lessons` (RS-123)
ORIGIN_HEADING:        Final = "## Sessions"          # read back as the file's ORIGIN (RS-142, RS-52)

# The skill class's own two constants (RS-149, RS-152). A SKILL.md carries no PKB frontmatter
# (VA-6) and its body is loaded into a model's prompt, so the origin record goes in a SIBLING file
# inside the skill folder and never inside SKILL.md.
SKILL_ORIGIN_FILE:     Final = "ORIGIN.md"            # <skill-folder>/ORIGIN.md, code-written (RS-149)
SKILL_SCOPES:          Final = ("topic", "root")      # RS-145. `root` resolves to <kb>/skills/

# The exclusion list, from F-35. ONE constant, rendered verbatim into the distillation context and
# never re-worded per call (§1.13). Five entries, RS-135 … RS-139, each naming the failure it
# prevents. Nothing here tells a model that producing something is expected: RS-140 forbids it, and
# the prompt test asserts against Hermes's own two biasing sentences as fixtures.
LEARN_EXCLUSIONS:      Final = (...)         # tuple[str, ...], RS-135 … RS-139

START_TOOL:          Final = "start_research"
CONTINUE_TOOL:       Final = "continue_research"
END_TOOL:            Final = "end_research"     # ends the ENQUIRY (RS-13 amended)
FILE_RESEARCH_TOOL:  Final = "file_research"    # gated (RS-50, RS-87)
FILE_LESSON_TOOL:    Final = "file_lesson"      # gated (RS-116)
FILE_SKILL_TOOL:     Final = "file_skill"       # gated, THIRD reason (RS-146)

# RS-6 as amended: `open` is where a session lives between enquiries, and `/close` is the only
# edge into `closed`.
SessionState = Literal["open", "planning", "running", "awaiting-direction", "closed"]   # RS-6

@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class PriorClaim:
    quote: str      # verified against the file bytes by the SAME check RS-28 uses (RS-36)
    path: str       # KB-relative POSIX
    role: str       # the FileRole it came from; notes first (RS-35)

@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class Decline:
    candidate_id: str   # printed at the checkpoint (RS-11)
    reason: str         # the HUMAN's words, copied verbatim by code (RS-13, RS-53)

@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class Candidate:
    candidate_id: str                  # "c3": stable, printed, never reused (RS-11)
    evidence: Evidence
    admissible: bool                   # decided ONCE, at admit time (RS-28, RS-29, RS-34)
    held_reason: str | None            # set iff not admissible (RS-31)
    bears_on: tuple[str, ...]          # PriorClaim quotes this was paired against (RS-38)
    label: Literal["agrees", "contradicts", "unrelated"] | None   # a HINT, never a filter

# Signatures ARE the enforcement. Neither of these can reach the prior or the human's phrasing,
# because neither has a parameter for it (RS-2, RS-37).
def plan_directions(goal: str, topic_name: str) -> tuple[Direction, ...]: ...
async def start_research() -> Command[Any]: ...      # goal read off the thread, in code

async def run_round(session: Session, host: ResearchHost) -> Round: ...   # steps 5 … 10
def render_checkpoint(session: Session) -> str: ...                       # code only (RS-10)
def render_research_file(session: Session) -> str: ...                    # code renders sections

# The checkpoint ends the turn with the bytes code rendered. No model call follows it (RS-79).
# `PkbRuntime._deliver` is the same move on the Librarian's path, for the same measured reason.
def _checkpoint(tool_call_id: str, session: Session) -> Command[Any]:
    return Command(goto=END, update={"messages": [
        ToolMessage(content="checkpoint rendered", tool_call_id=tool_call_id),
        AIMessage(content=render_checkpoint(session)),
    ]})

# The session surface. Directives are CODE, matched exactly, before any model call (RS-100, RS-104).
def is_directive(text: str) -> str | None: ...        # LEARN_DIRECTIVE | CLOSE_DIRECTIVE | None
def append_log(session: Session, message: str, on: date) -> None: ...     # verbatim (RS-103)
def propose_lessons(session: Session, prior: tuple[PriorClaim, ...]) -> tuple[Proposal, ...]: ...
                                              # reads the FILE, never the thread (RS-102), through
                                              # ingestion.model_asker; skips what `## Filed` holds
                                              # (RS-123)
def render_proposal(proposals: tuple[Proposal, ...], filed: tuple[FiledLine, ...]) -> str: ...
                                              # code renders it, marked repeats included (RS-101,
                                              # RS-123); an unregistered tag gets its own line
                                              # (RS-124)
def render_session_record(session: Session) -> str: ...  # the item a fan-out sends (RS-119, RS-120)
def render_lesson(session_id: str, proposal_id: str) -> str: ...
                                              # code renders the bytes; BOTH ids resolve inside the
                                              # CALLING THREAD's scratch and nowhere else (RS-120)
def resolve_note_path(topic_path: str, kind: Literal["lesson", "research"], slug: str) -> str: ...
                                              # slug == paths.slugify(title), so VA-35 holds by
                                              # construction. A taken lesson path REFUSES; a
                                              # research path joins only on a matching recorded
                                              # `## Question` AND a recorded origin naming this
                                              # thread (RS-125 amended, RS-52, RS-142)

# The skill class (RS-143 … RS-153). Three functions, and none of them is a second resolver:
# `resolve_skill_path` answers WHERE, `pkb.core.resolve_skills` answers WHAT THE TREE'S OWN SKILLS
# RESOLVE TO, `DEFAULT_SKILL_NAMES` answers WHAT THE PACKAGED MOUNT ALREADY HOLDS, and nothing in
# this module re-implements either merge (RS-145, P-56, P-58). The two lookups stay two, because
# `resolve_skills` never sees the mount and `DEFAULT_SKILL_NAMES` never sees the tree.
def resolve_skill_path(proposal: Proposal) -> str: ...
                                              # <Topic>/skills/<name>/SKILL.md for scope="topic",
                                              # <kb>/skills/<name>/SKILL.md for scope="root".
                                              # `name` == paths.slugify(title) and MUST equal the
                                              # folder name, or SK-15's SKILL_NAME_MISMATCH refuses
                                              # the filing before the gate (RS-152)
def render_skill(session_id: str, proposal_id: str) -> tuple[str, str]: ...
                                              # (SKILL.md bytes, ORIGIN.md bytes). Both ids resolve
                                              # inside the CALLING THREAD's scratch (RS-120). The
                                              # shadow notice of RS-150 is the SKILL.md's first body
                                              # line when the name collides, from adopt_skill's own
                                              # `_fork_notice` rather than a second copy of it
def shadowed_by(kb_root: Path, proposal: Proposal) -> Shadow | None: ...
                                              # NEW_NAME | SHIPPED(<name>) | EXISTING(<path>).
                                              # Read from DEFAULT_SKILL_NAMES and resolve_skills,
                                              # keyed on the rendered FRONTMATTER name (P-58);
                                              # EXISTING takes RS-149's path, not RS-150's

# The two guards from F-36, both in code and neither in a prompt (decision BC).
def recorded_origin(content: str) -> Origin | None: ...   # reads ORIGIN_HEADING back (RS-142)
def skill_origin(kb_root: Path, skill_dir: Path) -> Origin | None: ...
                                              # reads <skill-folder>/ORIGIN.md back. A skill folder
                                              # with none is the HUMAN's and every amendment path
                                              # refuses it (RS-149). Separate from `recorded_origin`
                                              # because a SKILL.md carries no such block by rule:
                                              # its body is prompt text, and an origin block there
                                              # would be read as procedure
def render_append(base: str, session: Session) -> str: ...
                                              # `base` is REQUIRED and is the file's current bytes,
                                              # read in this turn. There is no overload that takes a
                                              # path and reads it later, and none that takes only a
                                              # session: a signature with no way to skip the base is
                                              # what makes RS-141 structural rather than procedural.
def parse_answer(text: str, proposals: tuple[Proposal, ...]) -> Answer | None: ...
                                              # keep / drop / `<id>: prose`, read in code BEFORE any
                                              # classification, only while a proposal stands (RS-121)
def close_session(session: Session, *, offered: bool) -> Command[Any]: ...  # RS-104

@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class Proposal:
    proposal_id: str                 # "p2": stable, printed, never reused (RS-11's shape)
    kind: Literal["lesson", "summary", "skill"]  # WHAT IT FILES AS, never what the session was
                                           # (RS-98). `"skill"` added 2026-08-09 by the human's
                                           # ruling on Q54 (decision BD, RS-143). Original comment,
                                           # kept: "No `"skill"` member: Q54's default is encoded
                                           # here, so nothing proposes a skill until somebody adds
                                           # the member." Somebody did, and the member alone is not
                                           # the change: RS-144 decides the kind, RS-145 the path,
                                           # RS-146 the gate, RS-149 the origin, RS-150 the collision.
    topic_path: str                        # the expert whose subtree it lands in (RS-114). For a
                                           # kind="skill" proposal with scope="root" it names the
                                           # expert that PROPOSED, never where the file lands.
    scope: Literal["topic", "root"] = "topic"    # kind="skill" only (RS-145). "root" lands at
                                           # <kb>/skills/<name>/ and is written by _file_skill,
                                           # RT-18's fifth sanctioned harness writer (RS-148).
    text: str                              # the human's words when they gave them (RS-108)
    bears_on: tuple[str, ...]              # prior quotes it was paired against (RS-107)
    # The metadata the renderer needs. The MODEL supplies these inside the proposal; CODE renders
    # them into the frontmatter, and a missing title or description refuses before the gate
    # (RS-124). A tag absent from the registry gets its own marked line in the proposal message,
    # because the tool key already skipped NEW_TAG (RS-50, C-50).
    title: str
    description: str                       # required on every knowledge file (README §1.4), and
                                           # for kind="skill" it is deepagents' own progressive-
                                           # disclosure field: VA-6 refuses the file without it,
                                           # before the gate (RS-152)
    tags: tuple[str, ...]                  # EMPTY for kind="skill": a SKILL.md carries no PKB
                                           # frontmatter and contributes no tags (VA-6, P-55)
    related_topics: tuple[str, ...]        # how a SPANNING insight lands once (RS-114)

@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class FiledLine:
    """One `## Filed` entry, written by code at every successful filing (RS-123)."""
    proposal_text: str
    path: str        # KB-relative POSIX, the file that landed
    on: date

@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class Answer:
    """The human's reply to an outstanding proposal, parsed in code ahead of any model (RS-121)."""
    keep: tuple[str, ...]                        # proposal ids
    drop: tuple[str, ...]
    revise: tuple[tuple[str, str], ...]          # (proposal_id, the human's words, VERBATIM)

# The tools the EXPERT holds. Six since the skills amendment, all typed, all thin
# (RS-2, RS-13, RS-87, RS-116, RS-146).
# `start_research` and `continue_research` are NOT in GATED_TOOLS: gating a question would ask a
# human to approve a search before anyone knows what it found. `file_research` IS, and it carries
# a session id and nothing else, so the model never holds the bytes it is asking to land.
def research_tools(host: ResearchHost, agent_id: str) -> list[BaseTool]: ...
```

**The gate seam, exactly (RS-50, RS-87, RS-88).** Four edits in `pkb.agents.gates`, and no change to
the `write_file` if-chain:

```python
GateReason.RESEARCH_OUTPUT = "research-output"          # a new member
GateReason.SESSION_LESSON  = "session-lesson"           # the second one (RS-116)
GateReason.SKILL_PROPOSAL  = "skill-proposal"           # the third one (RS-146)
RENDERED_DECISIONS: Final = ("approve", "reject")       # `edit` would edit a session id (RS-88)
GATE_DECISIONS[GateReason.RESEARCH_OUTPUT] = RENDERED_DECISIONS
GATE_DECISIONS[GateReason.SESSION_LESSON]  = RENDERED_DECISIONS
GATE_DECISIONS[GateReason.SKILL_PROPOSAL]  = RENDERED_DECISIONS
_REASONS_BY_TOOL[FILE_RESEARCH_TOOL] = frozenset({GateReason.RESEARCH_OUTPUT})
_REASONS_BY_TOOL[FILE_LESSON_TOOL]   = frozenset({GateReason.SESSION_LESSON})
_REASONS_BY_TOOL[FILE_SKILL_TOOL]    = frozenset({GateReason.SKILL_PROPOSAL})

# requires_approval gains THREE branches, at the top, keyed by the tool:
if tool == FILE_RESEARCH_TOOL:
    return GateReason.RESEARCH_OUTPUT        # always. No path question, no ordering question.
if tool == FILE_LESSON_TOOL:
    return GateReason.SESSION_LESSON         # same shape, different framing (RS-116)
if tool == FILE_SKILL_TOOL:
    return GateReason.SKILL_PROPOSAL         # third framing: this changes how the expert WORKS

# GateReason.SKILL_OVERLOAD is UNTOUCHED and its path branch keeps firing. Executed (P-54): a
# `write_file` to `Cooking/skills/x/SKILL.md` returns `skill-overload` today, and the permission
# list allows that write for the owning expert (P-53), so the branch guards a reachable call
# rather than a dead one. The two coexist by construction: a tool branch is answered before any
# path is looked at (RT-65), and `file_skill` carries no path to look at (RS-146).

# GateEnv gains one injected field, the way it already carries the path normaliser:
research_render: Callable[[str], tuple[str, str]] | None   # session_id -> (rel_path, content)
# `proposed_content` gains the matching case, so `describe_write` shows a new-file diff of the
# bytes `render_research_file` produced, which are the bytes that land (RS-89).
```

### 5.3 `pkb.agents.mcp`: the catalog, the bindings, the Protocol

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class McpServerSpec:
    name: str
    kind: Literal["system", "topic"]   # REQUIRED by RS-65; never inferred from the bindings
    transport: Literal["stdio", "http"]
    command_or_url: str
    use: Literal["research", "expert"]         # RS-94: who the tools reach
    operator_asserts_read_only: bool           # named for what it is: the OPERATOR's assertion
    read_only_tools: frozenset[str]            # RS-66: an ADVERTISED tool absent from this set
                                               #        refuses the attach, naming the tool

class McpToolset(Protocol):            # everything below is drivable against a fake (RS-67)
    async def connect(self, spec: McpServerSpec) -> None: ...
    async def tools(self) -> Sequence[BaseTool]: ...
    async def aclose(self) -> None: ...

# On RuntimeConfig (RG-21, deployment configuration, NEVER read from the knowledge base):
#   mcp_servers:  Mapping[str, McpServerSpec]          # the global catalog (RS-58)
#   mcp_bindings: Mapping[str, tuple[str, ...]]        # agent id -> server names (RS-59)
#   every budget and TTL named in RS-68, with the defaults given there
#
# Startup check (RS-64, RS-65): a binding naming the Librarian and a `kind == "topic"` server is a
# STARTUP ERROR naming both. Not a warning: a warning here runs misconfigured for months.
#
# Connection is ASYNC and the graph build is SYNC, so they are split (RS-60). `AgentRegistry.get`
# holds a threading.RLock across the build and is called from worker threads, so nothing may be
# awaited inside it:
async def warm_mcp(agent_id: str) -> None: ...   # owned by PkbRuntime; caches Sequence[BaseTool]
                                                 # per server name
# build_expert reads that cache synchronously. A server not yet warmed compiles DEGRADED, which
# RS-61 and RS-62 already cover, and the degraded entry expires after mcp_degraded_ttl_seconds.
```

### 5.3a `pkb.agents.search`: the two tools, the client and the credential

*Added 2026-08-09 (search settled). This module is what decision AR's amendment carves out of §5.3:
search reaches the loop without a catalog, without a binding and without an MCP server.*

```python
# NO `import os`, NO `environ`, NO `getenv`, NO socket of its own (RS-134, RS-91 amended).
# The client arrives constructed, with the Bearer header already on it (RS-133).
# Executed: `ollama.AsyncClient(headers={"Authorization": f"Bearer <key>"})` exposes both methods
# as coroutines, so nothing here offloads to a thread (P-52).

@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class SearchResult:
    """One `WebSearchResult`, kept only when `url` and `content` both arrived (RS-128).

    Every field of the vendor type is optional and defaults to None (executed, P-51), so a
    result missing either is DROPPED and COUNTED rather than admitted with an empty haystack.
    """
    title: str
    url: str
    content: str          # 8,701 to 22,448 chars measured (F-27). THE bytes RS-28 checks against.
    retrieved_at: date

@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class PageText:
    title: str
    text: str             # EXTRACTED text, never raw bytes. Ingestion's copy is RS-130's job.
    links: tuple[str, ...]
    url: str
    retrieved_at: date

class SearchClient(Protocol):          # the whole loop is drivable against a fake (RS-78, C-44)
    async def web_search(self, query: str, max_results: int = 3) -> Any: ...
    async def web_fetch(self, url: str) -> Any: ...

# The two tools a researcher holds, and the only two (RS-128, RS-129).
# `max_results` is NOT a parameter: code supplies research_search_results (RS-68).
async def search_web(query: str) -> tuple[SearchResult, ...]: ...
async def read_page(url: str) -> PageText: ...

# Three failures, three meanings (RS-76 amended). None of them raises out of the tool.
#   ConnectError / TimeoutException / 5xx -> search down          -> RS-131
#   429                                   -> quota, no fallback   -> RS-131
#   ValueError("Authorization header …")  -> DEPLOYMENT fault     -> RS-133, never retried
# A zero-result search is NOT a failure: it is a gap (RS-70).

# On RuntimeConfig (RG-21): research_search_results (3), and NOTHING ELSE for search.
# The credential is NOT here and never becomes a str field on any config object (RS-133, C-53):
#   pkb.daemon reads OLLAMA_API_KEY -> builds the AsyncClient -> hands the CLIENT down.
```

### 5.4 The Layer 1 amendment (`FM-16`, `VA-42`, `VA-43`, `VA-32` restated)

`FM-16` was checked and is free. `VA-33` was **not**: it is `RELATED_TOPICS_PREFIXED`, a *warning*,
implemented at `validation.py:530` and cited by id in MW-12. The value check is `VA-42` and the
presence check is `VA-43`, the next two free ids in a sequence that runs to `VA-41`.

```
FM-16  `provenance` is a recognised OPTIONAL field. Parsed as text, serialized after
       `source_type` in CANONICAL_ORDER, surfaced on `Metadata.provenance: str | None`.
       Absent means unknown; nothing infers or backfills one (RS-47).

VA-42  `provenance` carries one of exactly four values: `ingested`, `researched`, `practised`,
       `stated`. Any other value is an error naming the four. The check is a value check ONLY:
       no combination of folder, `source_type` and `provenance` is refused (RS-46).

VA-43  A file in a topic's `sessions/` folder with `source_type: summary` and NO `provenance`
       is an error naming the field. This is the one place location is consulted, and it is a
       presence check rather than a value cross-check, so RS-46 stands (RS-45).

VA-32  restated. The hint becomes:
       "Check for a typo of a known field (title, description, topic, tags, …); the key is
        preserved either way, and a key you did not add is not yours to remove."
```

Three files move with these in the same commit, because the goldens are copied from each other:
README §1.4's frontmatter block, `tests/core/test_frontmatter.py` (whose header states the blocks
are byte-for-byte substrings of `README.md`), and VA-32's golden finding message.

### 5.5 The Layer 2 amendments (`RT-65`, `RT-32`, and RT-18's allow-list)

`RT-49` was **not** free: it is "Layer 2 deliberately exposes no thread listing" at Layer 2 rules
line 263, cited at `runtime.py:766,893,961` and `service/proposals.py:11`. RT runs to RT-64, so the
amendment is `RT-65`.

```
RT-65  GateReason gains RESEARCH_OUTPUT, carrying ("approve", "reject") rather than
       WRITE_DECISIONS, because the gated tool's args hold a session id and an `edit` decision
       could change no byte of the file (RS-88). The reason is keyed by the TOOL:
       `requires_approval` gains one branch returning RESEARCH_OUTPUT for `file_research`,
       ahead of every path question, and the `write_file`/`edit_file` if-chain is untouched.
       Declaration order is not the mechanism: `requires_approval` is a hand-written if-chain
       (`gates.py:333-361`), so a member's position in the enum decides nothing on its own, and
       keying on the tool removes the question rather than answering it (RS-50, RS-87).
       Amended 2026-08-09 (session model): GateReason gains SESSION_LESSON on the same terms,
       keyed by `file_lesson`, carrying ("approve", "reject"), sharing GateEnv.research_render.
       Two reasons rather than one because the human reads the reason: a practised note and a
       research summary are different claims on their attention (RS-116).
       Amended 2026-08-09 (skills): GateReason gains SKILL_PROPOSAL on the same terms, keyed by
       `file_skill`, carrying ("approve", "reject"), sharing GateEnv.research_render. Three
       reasons rather than two for RS-116's own argument carried one step: a skill changes how
       the expert works on every later turn in its scope, and "a lesson is ready to file" is the
       wrong sentence to read before approving that. SKILL_OVERLOAD is untouched and keeps its
       PATH branch, which fires on a model's own write_file into any skills/ folder; executed as
       P-54, that call is reachable today and the permission list allows it for the owning expert
       (P-53), so the two branches guard two different callers rather than one (RS-146).

RT-32  amended. RT-32 reads "Write gates allow exactly ["approve","edit","reject"]", and both new
       reasons carry ("approve", "reject"). The clause that changes is that one, not RT-32's
       other half ("a client may narrow the decision set, never widen it"), which RS-88 cited and
       which is about clients rather than about the table. A gate over bytes CODE rendered from a
       CONTENT-FREE tool call allows ("approve", "reject"), because an `edit` would edit a session
       id and change no byte of the file. `DELETE_DECISIONS` is the precedent already in the table:
       the set is per reason, and RT-32's "exactly" is about the KB write gates whose args hold
       the bytes. `respond` stays absent from both, for RT-32's own reason (RS-88, RS-116).

RT-18  the allow-list of sanctioned harness writers gains a fourth entry:
       {(skills.py, adopt_skill), (ingestion.py, _write), (ingestion.py, _copy_original),
        (research.py, _file_research)}
       with the same two obligations: validate before writing, and record into `kb_touched`.
       RT-18 says a fourth "would need the same argument made again in the same place"; C-39 is
       that argument and RS-86 is the amendment (RS-20, RS-86).
       Amended 2026-08-09 (skills): a FIFTH entry, (research.py, _file_skill), on the same two
       obligations. The argument made again in the same place: a `topic`-scoped skill is inside
       the expert's own subtree and the permission list already allows it (P-53), so that half
       needs no carve-out at all; a `root`-scoped skill lands at <kb>/skills/<name>/, which every
       agent is denied and must stay denied, and the writer is therefore code rather than a
       widened permission (RS-147, RS-148). The precedent is the first entry on this same list:
       adopt_skill already writes <kb>/skills/<name>/ as harness code, gated at the call site,
       outside the permission layer. `_file_skill` writes the same directory for the same reason,
       and it writes ORIGIN.md beside SKILL.md in the same call (RS-149).
```

### 5.6 Import contracts

No new contract, and no existing one changes. `pkb.agents` may already import `deepagents`,
`langgraph` and `langchain_core`; `mcp` and the client join that list. `pkb.core` gains a field and
no import. ~~`pkb.sources` gains `fetch_text`, which imports nothing it did not already import:
`httpx` is declared and `check_fetchable` and `extract` are in the module.~~ **Amended 2026-08-09
(search settled): `pkb.sources` gains nothing at all** (RS-90 as amended), and `pkb.agents` gains
`ollama`. That import needs no contract change and no exception: `ollama` is installed in this venv
today as a dependency of `langchain-ollama`, which `pkb.agents.models` already imports for the
models, and its own requirements are `httpx` and `pydantic`, both declared (executed, `P-50`).
Naming it directly in `pyproject.toml` is one line and zero packages. **Two forbidden contracts are
worth checking against rather than assuming past**: `CX-1` forbids `httpx` in `pkb.core`, and
`pkb.agents.search` is not `pkb.core`; the transports contract forbids `pkb.agents` inside
`pkb.server`, `pkb.tui` and `pkb.clients`, and `pkb.daemon` sits in none of those three, so the
composition root building an `ollama.AsyncClient` and handing it down breaks neither. The layers contract keeps
`pkb.core < pkb.packs < pkb.agents` intact, because `research.py` and `researcher.py` sit inside
`pkb.agents` and reach Layer 1 through the same surface every other module there uses. **`make layers` stays at 5 kept, 0 broken**, and that is an assertion
in the suite rather than an expectation.

---

## 6. Test strategy

Arch §9's intent holds completely: free, fast, keyless, networkless by default. The whole loop runs
against a scripted model and a fake toolset.

### 6.1 Four tiers, one job each

| Tier | Tests | Why not the others |
|------|-------|--------------------|
| **Pure, no model** (`tests/agents/test_research_render.py`) | Checkpoint rendering, research-file rendering, the append-only property, the declined round-trip, candidate id stability, the state machine, budget arithmetic, admissibility over fixture bytes. | Every one of these is code by rule (RS-10, RS-27, RS-32, RS-52), so a model in the fixture would only add flakiness to an assertion about determinism. Golden bytes are the point. |
| **Scripted model + fake toolset** (`tests/agents/test_research.py`) | The loop end to end: plan, brief, fan out, admit, weigh, checkpoint, direct, synthesize, gate. The prior-absence assertion (RS-37), the fabricated-quote hold (RS-28), the fabricated-URL hold (RS-29), the WikiContradict shape (RS-38: a model labelling everything `unrelated` still surfaces every pair), every budget bound, knowledge-base mode. | The only fixture that can script a stalled researcher, a fabricating one, a failing one and a model that answers in prose after a checkpoint, in one run, with no socket. `RuntimeConfig.default_model` already accepts a `BaseChatModel`, which is what makes it possible today. |
| **Real `PkbRuntime` on `tmp_path`** (`tests/agents/test_research_seam.py`) | `file_research` raises a real interrupt whose reason is `RESEARCH_OUTPUT` and whose description is `render_research_file` byte for byte; approving it lands those exact bytes; the touched paths reach MW-18 so `updated`, the index and the tag registry all move; RT-15 refuses a write outside the subtree; `validate_tree` returns zero errors on the landed file; the scratch session survives a rebuilt runtime. | Executed precedent: the whole Layer 2 suite runs on `tmp_path` with a scripted model. The middleware interaction is the part that cannot be faked, because its whole job is to be unskippable. |
| **Live** (`@pytest.mark.live`, deselected) | §6.4's vendor facts only, six of them since the search settlement. | Deselected by default, and every rule in §1 is covered without it (RS-78). |

### 6.2 Three fixtures that are not optional

```python
@pytest.fixture
def fake_toolset() -> FakeMcpToolset:
    """Scriptable per-server: connect failure, handshake timeout, a tool that claims to write,
    recovery after N seconds (for RS-62's TTL), and a search tool returning a fixed result set.
    Records every call in order, so "no researcher ran in KB mode" is directly assertable."""

@pytest.fixture
def fetched_bytes() -> FetchLog:
    """The bytes the harness holds, keyed by URL. RS-28 and RS-29 are BOTH assertions about this
    object: a quote is checked against it, and a URL absent from it is inadmissible. Fabrication
    tests write the fabricated quote nowhere in it, and that is the whole test.

    Amended 2026-08-09 (search settled): the log is filled by `fake_search`, not by a fetcher, and
    the retrieval counter on it is what RS-127 asserts. A test that admits a quote with the counter
    above zero has caught a re-fetch this design forbids."""

@pytest.fixture
def fake_search() -> FakeSearchClient:
    """Scriptable `SearchClient` (§5.3a), with no socket and no key. Six scripts, one per rule:
    an honest three-result return at the measured 22,448-char ceiling (F-27, RS-34's arithmetic);
    a result with `content=None` and one with `url=None`, both LEGAL per the installed client's
    types (P-51, RS-127, RS-128); a connection error and a 429, both search-down (RS-131);
    the credential `ValueError` with P-49's exact text, which is a deployment fault and never
    retried (RS-76, RS-133); a zero-result return, which is a GAP and not a failure (RS-70);
    and a page whose body reads "ignore previous instructions", for RS-92's fence."""

@pytest.fixture
def scripted_researcher() -> ScriptedResearcher:
    """Returns canned `ResearchReturn`s: one honest, one fabricating a quote, one fabricating a
    URL, one stalled, one over `max_chars`, one raising. Every §1.3 and §1.4 rule is one of these
    six against the real admission code."""
```

### 6.3 The headline assertions, by area

| Area | Fixture | Headline assertions |
|------|---------|---------------------|
| **the loop** | scripted model | a checkpoint leaves `pending is None` and the next message is accepted (RS-8); the last message is `render_checkpoint` byte for byte with zero model calls after the tool (RS-79); a prose reply changes nothing (RS-13); a second `start_research` is refused (RS-7); `start_research` has no `goal` parameter (RS-2); the session survives a rebuilt runtime byte for byte (RS-4); a `running` session found after a restart closes as `interrupted` and the thread researches again (RS-80); a planted model write to the session file is refused (RS-82); ten turns leave ten append-only blocks (RS-5). |
| **the researcher** | fake toolset | the researcher's tool list and its model request both carry no `task`, and the expert's request carries none while a session is open (RS-15, RS-83); `list_agents()` is unchanged while three researchers run (RS-16); a planted write tool fails (RS-17); zero reads under `/kb/` (RS-18); one raising researcher leaves two returns (RS-19). |
| **verification** | `fetched_bytes` | a fabricated quote is held and the file gains nothing (RS-28); a fabricated URL is held (RS-29); `research.py` imports `normalise_for_quote_match` and `quote_in` from `ingestion` and defines neither (RS-28); a synthesis citing `[7]` with six findings does not reach the gate and an uncited sentence lands in `## Unsupported` (RS-32); an empty admissible set refuses `outcome="file"` (RS-85); two failing drafts land an `## Unsupported` section (RS-33); `.inbox` is byte-identical after thirty fetches, and a daemon killed between round 1 and the synthesis resumes with round 1 still citable (RS-34); a 302 to a link-local address is a `FetchError` naming the hop (RS-91); a page whose body reads "ignore previous instructions" produces a quoted, attributed finding and no note (RS-92). |
| **the prior** | scripted model | `inspect.signature(plan_directions)` is `(goal: str, topic_name: str)` (RS-37); a golden pair selection over the Jaccard threshold, plus one pinned no-overlap miss (RS-38, RS-39); a model labelling every pair `unrelated` still surfaces every pair (RS-38); three contradictions leave every note byte-identical with no tag change (RS-40); the ordering is golden and a dissonant finding is not demoted (RS-41). |
| **provenance** | pure | four values validate and a fifth is `VA-42` (RS-42); the field round-trips in canonical order; a tree written before the field validates unchanged and no derived file changes bytes (RS-47); the VA-32 hint carries the clause (RS-44). |
| **outcomes** | real runtime | every `file_research` call gates with `RESEARCH_OUTPUT` and `allowed_decisions` is `("approve", "reject")` (RS-50, RS-88); the bytes on disk equal the bytes the gate described (RS-89); `accept=("c9",)` with no `c9` at the last checkpoint is refused (RS-89); a research file reaches the research pack and a `status.draft` file reaches neither (RS-93); a surfaced contradiction files `status.conflict-review` with a `review_note` (RS-51); a declined URL round-trips through `## Declined` and is marked rather than dropped on the next session (RS-53, RS-54); one accepted candidate produces exactly one `ingest_source` call (RS-55); a multi-topic fan-out files one file per topic and none outside a topic root (RS-57). |
| **MCP** | fake toolset | 50 topics open zero connections, `warm_mcp` opens the bound ones and `get()` opens none ever (RS-60); a `research`-use server is absent from the expert's request and present in every brief (RS-94); a note written on a turn that called an external tool is refused (RS-95); one dead server leaves a working expert (RS-61); recovery after the TTL restores the tools and a healthy graph is never recompiled on a timer (RS-62); a Librarian grant of a topic server fails startup naming both (RS-64); a server exposing a write tool is refused at attach (RS-66). |
| **the session** | scripted model | a channel opens exactly one session and `/close` is the only edge into `closed` (RS-96, RS-6); two channels for one agent with different goals both open and route to one agent (RS-97); grep finds no session-kind field (RS-98); a session that files is `open` on the next turn (RS-99); the literal on its own runs `/learn` and the literal inside a sentence does not (RS-100); a `/learn` leaves `pending is None` and the next message is accepted (RS-101); a `/learn` over a compacted history proposes a week-one trial quoted from the file (RS-102); twenty turns leave twenty verbatim log entries and an over-length one is cut with the cut recorded (RS-103); the first `/close` offers and the second closes, and a `/close` with a pending gate is refused (RS-104); ask, answer, `/close` leaves `kb_root` byte-identical (RS-105); a contradicting instruction is answered in the same turn with both quotes and the note byte-unchanged (RS-106); a human-dictated lesson contradicted by the log surfaces the log entry before filing (RS-107); the filed lesson carries the human's words plus the pair and `status.conflict-review` (RS-108); a lesson is `source_type: solution` + `type.solution` + `provenance: practised` and the old pairing is asserted to be a `VA-11` error (RS-109); a goal spec leaves `kb_root` byte-identical (RS-110); no sandbox backend is constructed anywhere in `pkb.agents` and `execute` returns the inert error (RS-111); a Librarian `/learn` fans out through `parse_takes` and writes nothing on its own thread (RS-112); a four-note fan-out with two kept holds **two pendings at once** on two derived threads, and a two-proposal set from one expert is sequential (RS-113); a spanning insight lands once with `related_topics` (RS-114); a `NOTHING` expert leaves its subtree byte-identical (RS-115); `file_lesson` gates with `SESSION_LESSON` and its bytes match the description (RS-116); a directive **and an ordinary message** on a closed session are both refused with zero model calls (RS-117); one `route` call names the fan-out's targets (RS-119); each expert's brief lands in its own derived scratch and resolves with the Librarian's session file deleted (RS-120); `p2: drop the second part` re-invokes `p2`'s expert verbatim with zero classifier calls (RS-121); an expert that took something and called no tool is named as failed, distinctly from `NOTHING` (RS-122); two `/learn`s with no new trials propose one lesson and land one file (RS-123); a proposal missing `description` is refused before the gate and an unregistered tag gets its own line (RS-124); a taken lesson path refuses and the tree is byte-identical, and a landed file's stem slugifies to its own title (RS-125); a fan-out leaves four derived threads with no session and no log (RS-126). |
| **budgets and offline** | scripted model | every budget is a hard stop in code (RS-68); a stalled researcher stops at `NO_PROGRESS_STEPS` (RS-71); the default deadline passes a fallback-speed session (RS-72); one failover line per session (RS-73); zero researcher invocations in KB mode and no URL in any citation (RS-75); an empty catalog runs the whole loop with no socket (RS-77). |
| **search** | `fake_search` | `search_web`'s signature carries `query` alone and a model asking for 50 results gets `research_search_results` (RS-128); every returned result is in the fetch log before the model sees it and a quote admits with the retrieval counter at zero (RS-127); a result with `content=None` and one with `url=None` are dropped, counted and rendered as a gap (RS-127, RS-128); the researcher's tool names are exactly `search_web`, `read_page` and the bound `research`-use MCP tools (RS-129); thirty results at the 22,448-char ceiling reach `research_max_candidates` and leave `research_max_cache_bytes` unreached (RS-34); an accepted candidate's `ingest_source` call stages bytes from `_http_fetch` rather than from the session cache (RS-130); `pkb.agents.search` opens no socket of its own and `search_web`/`read_page` are absent from every expert model request in the suite (RS-91, RS-94). |
| **search down** | `fake_search` | a connection error and a 429 both mark the enquiry search-down, the credential `ValueError` renders a distinct line and is never retried, and a zero-result search leaves the enquiry running with one `## Gaps` line (RS-76); an unreachable search answers from the tree in the same turn with exactly one attempt per direction, leaves the session `open`, and the recovered client searches on the next enquiry with no restart (RS-131); a fixture failing both the model and the search renders two lines, files a valid KB-mode file and finishes inside `research_deadline_seconds` (RS-132); a researcher built in KB mode holds neither search tool (RS-75); setting the credential is the only change that moves a fixture from KB mode to searching (RS-77). |
| **the credential** | pure | `OLLAMA_API_KEY` appears in `pkb/daemon.py` and nowhere else in `src/` (RS-133); a daemon with no key starts, logs one line naming the variable, and answers in KB mode (RS-133); the AST scan over `research.py`, `researcher.py` and `search.py` finds no `os`, `environ` or `getenv`, **and finds `paths.py`'s import when pointed at it**, so the scan is proved non-vacuous (RS-134); the key reaches no log record across a searching run, `/health` names search as configured with no value, and a full session leaves the literal nowhere in `kb_root` (RS-134). |
| **what a session must not file** | golden session records + scripted model | `LEARN_EXCLUSIONS` reaches the distillation context byte for byte and is one constant (§1.13); a record whose enquiries all end `abandon` proposes zero lessons (RS-135); a proposal reporting that nothing worked is refused naming the rule; no prompt template in `pkb.agents.research` contains either of Hermes's two biasing sentences, asserted against them as fixtures (RS-140); an empty proposal set renders one line and files nothing. The five defect cases each get a **live** twin over a real model, because a scripted model asserts the plumbing and never the judgement (RS-136 … RS-139) |
| **the two guards** | real runtime | `render_append` has no overload that omits `base`, asserted from its signature (RS-141); an append with the read stubbed out is refused and the file is byte-unchanged; a hand-written `sessions/` file with no `## Sessions` block refuses every append (RS-142); a file another thread filed against the same goal is refused naming the owning session (RS-125 amended); the deny rule permits `/kb/<Topic>/sessions/**` and refuses `/sessions/**` (RS-82 amended) |
| **the skill a session proposes** | real runtime + fixture tree | the four permission answers hold in both directions and `SKILLS_DENY_GLOBS == (SKILLS_MOUNT + "**",)`, so a widening fails (RS-147); `file_skill` gates with `SKILL_PROPOSAL` carrying `("approve", "reject")` and a `write_file` to a `skills/` path still gates with `SKILL_OVERLOAD` (RS-146); a `topic` filing resolves for its topic alone and a `root` filing for every topic, asserted through `pkb.core.resolve_skills` (RS-145); a filed skill validates with zero findings, reaches no index and no tag registry, and its `ORIGIN.md` does the same (RS-145, RS-149); a proposal stating a subject fact with `kind="skill"` is refused quoting the sentence, and the mirror case too (RS-144); a skill folder with no `ORIGIN.md` refuses every amendment and is byte-identical after (RS-149); a proposal named `research` renders the shadow line in the message, in the gate and as the file's first body line, from one notice function (RS-150); `resolve_skills` over a fixture returns nothing from `packaged_skills_root()`, so the shipped lookup and the tree lookup are asserted as two (RS-150, P-58); a name that differs from its folder is refused naming `SKILL_NAME_MISMATCH` before the gate, in both directions (RS-152); a proposal naming an existing file with the read stubbed out is refused and nothing is rendered (RS-153); RT-18's allow-list test asserts a five-element set and no `file_skill` is bound to the Librarian (RS-148); the abandoned-session golden proposes zero skills as well as zero lessons (RS-151). |
| **invariants** | CI | `make layers` stays 5 kept 0 broken; `make lint`; `make types`; the Layer 1 golden findings updated in the same commit as `VA-32`'s hint. |

### 6.4 What needs a live tier

**Nothing in §1 does**, which is the point of `McpToolset`, `SearchClient` and the injectable
`Fetcher`. **Amended 2026-08-09 (sessions): §1.13 is the one exception, and it is an exception for a
reason the rest of §1 does not have.** Original claim, kept, and it holds for every rule whose
guarantee is a signature, a permission or a rendered byte. RS-135 … RS-139 forbid a **judgement**,
and a scripted model asserts that the exclusion list reached the context while proving nothing about
whether a real model obeys it. So each of the five carries both tiers, stated in its own row: a
**no key** assertion on the plumbing and a **live** assertion over a golden session record built to
produce that defect. RS-140 stays **no key** throughout, because a prompt that does not contain a
sentence is checkable without a model. **Amended 2026-08-09 (search settled): six rows rather than four, and the search rows are
the ones to write first.** RS-127 is this design's strongest claim and it rests on a vendor
behaviour, so the row that pins it earns its place ahead of the MCP rows. Facts
about the outside world are worth pinning once, marked `@pytest.mark.live` and skipped when the
relevant environment variable is unset:

| Fact | Why a fake cannot settle it |
|------|------------------------------|
| The MCP handshake and tool-listing shape of a real server | Our fake implements the spec; only a real server proves the fake is right, and RS-66's read-only check reads the real tool metadata. |
| A real page's bytes surviving the quote check after extraction | RS-28 compares against extracted text, and extraction is where whitespace, ligatures and soft hyphens actually differ. The grounding executed this once against the live internet; pinning it stops a future extractor change from silently making every quote inadmissible. **Amended 2026-08-09 (search settled): the extractor is now the provider's rather than `trafilatura`'s on the research path**, so the change that would break this is a change nobody here ships and nobody here sees in a changelog. The ingestion path keeps `trafilatura`, so the row covers two extractors and says which is which. |
| ~~A search server's result shape and its rate limits~~ **`web_search` and `web_fetch` returning content, and their field types** | **Amended 2026-08-09 (search settled), and this row is now the most valuable one in the table.** RS-127's whole argument rests on `content` arriving with the result. A provider that switched to snippets, or that started returning `content=None` on most results, would leave every rule in §1.4 passing against the fake and admitting almost nothing in production, with the session file reporting fabrication where there is none. The live test asserts one search returns at least one result carrying non-empty `content`, and records the length. Skipped when `OLLAMA_API_KEY` is unset. |
| **A 429 and a missing-credential error being distinguishable** | RS-76 splits three failures three ways and RS-131 acts on two of them. Executed here, a missing key raises `ValueError` from client code with a specific message (`P-49`), which is a string a vendor may reword. A fake asserts our branching; only the live call proves the branch it lands in. The credential half runs with the variable deliberately unset and needs no key at all. |
| A real server's advertised tool list against `read_only_tools` | RS-66 refuses an advertised tool absent from the operator's allow-list, and only a real handshake proves the fake advertises the way a server does. |
| The fallback model's real turn duration on this machine | RS-72's default is sized from a measurement (284 seconds), and hardware changes. |
| **A real model obeying the five exclusions** | RS-135 … RS-139 forbid a judgement, and a scripted model returns whatever the fixture told it to. The golden records are the fixtures: all-failed trials, an environment fault, a capability complaint, a retry that worked, and a nine-turn chronology. Added 2026-08-09 (sessions). |

A live test never calls `start_run`, never files, and never writes to a knowledge base. A live test
that files is a live test that writes to a tree with no undo. The §1.13 rows honour that
unchanged: each one runs `propose_lessons` over a golden record and asserts what came back, and no
row of that table reaches `_file_research`.

---

## 7. Explicitly out of scope

**Already Layer 1 or 2: cite it, never reimplement it**
- Source staging, extraction, sectioning, the reading record, the provenance block, re-ingestion
  reconciliation and the gainful test (`pkb.sources`, `pkb.agents.ingestion`, LS-1 … LS-16). RS-55
  hands an accepted source to `ingest_source` and writes no reference file.
- The quote check itself. RS-28 calls LS-13's normalisation and comparison; it does not copy them.
- The gate table, the diff, the validation label, the write lock, the flush, the escalation
  (`gates.describe_write`, RT-34, RT-35, MW-*).
- Validation, index generation, tag-registry generation, byte-idempotence (`pkb.core`).
- Attributed merge and the expert roster (LB-18). Multi-topic research reuses it verbatim.

**Stated residual risk, not a solved problem**
- **Prompt injection through a fetched page.** RS-92 fences retrieved text as data in both prompts
  and renders every quote attributed, so attacker prose never speaks in the system's voice. Fencing
  is mitigation. A page written to be read by an agent still enters a researcher's loop with tools
  bound to it and the expert's synthesis context, and no rule in this document claims otherwise.
  What bounds the damage is structural rather than textual: a researcher holds no write tool
  (RS-17), reads no KB file (RS-18) and has no `task` (RS-15, RS-83); the expert's notes path is
  refused on any turn that touched external content (RS-95); every claim needs a quote found in
  bytes the harness fetched (RS-28); and the file the human approves is rendered by code from the
  admissible set (RS-87, RS-89).
- **A citation that resolves but does not support** (RS-32). A sentence citing a real finding and
  overstating it passes every check here, exactly as LS-13 states its own limit. What compensates is
  that the quote sits beside the claim, so a reader can check.
- **The overlap threshold's recall** (RS-39). A contradiction that shares no vocabulary is never
  selected and never shown. The checkpoint reports how many pairs the threshold rejected, and a
  fixture pins one known miss, so the limit is measured rather than assumed away.
- **What the search ranker chose** (RS-128). The results a query returns are ranked by a provider
  nobody here configures, and a topic where the top three results agree with each other produces
  three findings that agree, which reads as corroboration. RS-38 pairs findings against the human's
  own notes and never against each other, so nothing in this design detects a ranker's consensus.
  The compensation is thin and worth naming as thin: the human sees every candidate with its quote
  and its URL at the checkpoint, and RS-25's gaps are the only other signal.
- **The provider's own SSRF posture** (RS-91 as amended). `read_page` follows a link inside the
  provider's infrastructure, so `check_fetchable`'s cloud-metadata threat cannot reach this machine
  on that path, and this document makes no claim beyond that. Nobody here has measured what the
  provider refuses to fetch.
- **One provider serving the models and the search** (F-29, RS-132). The correlated outage is
  ruled rather than removed. A deployment that wants the two decoupled would need a second search
  provider, a second credential and a second failure mode, and RS-131's answer is what makes the
  coupling survivable rather than what makes it disappear.

**Named defects this design does not fix**
- **AI-authored notes land unattended** (P-44). RS-50 gates research output and RS-95 refuses the
  notes path on turns that touched external content. The wider hole, any AI-authored note on any
  turn, stays open and deserves its own rule in the Layer 2 spec. Named here so nobody reads RS-50
  or RS-95 as having closed it.
- **The conflict-scan worker can never start** (P-45): `build_app` never passes a `scan_queue`. RS-40
  routes a filed reference to the ordinary scan, and today that scan does not run. A Layer 3 fix,
  one wiring line, and it belongs with the Telegram empty-slot precedent. **RS-108 depends on it
  too, and says so**: a lesson that contradicts an older note tags the new file alone, and README
  §1.7's two-sided tagging is reached by the same scan. Until the wiring lands, the older side of
  such a disagreement carries no marker at all, and a reader who opens that note sees nothing.
