- **`.inbox` has no lifecycle** (LS §14.4). RS-34 keeps this loop out of it entirely, which is a
  containment rather than a fix.
- **`_http_fetch` sends no User-Agent** (LS-14, specified and unimplemented), so Wikipedia answers
  403. One line, and RS-29's fetch depends on it, so §8 increment 2 carries it. **Amended 2026-08-09
  (search settled)**: RS-29's fetch no longer depends on it and RS-130's ingestion does, so the fix
  is owed to the same increment for a different caller. The defect stayed exactly as serious: an
  accepted candidate is a URL a human chose, sent to `ingest_source`, and a 403 there is the human
  watching their accepted source fail to land after they approved it.
- **A research view on any transport.** §7 keeps it deferred, and RS-81 supplies the answer a human
  actually needs: an open session re-announces itself on its own thread. "Show me every open
  session across every thread" still has no answer anywhere, which is the same shape as `.inbox`'s
  invisibility and belongs with that fix.

**Deferred**
- A plan checkpoint before round 1 (Q39) and unattended sessions (Q40).
- Rejection with a typed reason at the checkpoint. The human types a sentence and RS-53 records it
  verbatim; a taxonomy of decline reasons is a later, additive change.
- Cross-session memory of research beyond the session summaries themselves. The files are the
  memory, and a second store is a second thing that can be wrong.
- ~~**A `/learn` that proposes a skill rather than a note** (Q54)~~ **In scope since 2026-08-09**, on
  the human's ruling. §1.14 rules it as `RS-143 … RS-153` and §8's increment 2c builds it. The
  original bullet is kept struck through rather than deleted, because three of the four obstacles it
  stated were checkable and wrong, and `P-53 … P-56` print the calls that settled each one. The narrower thing that
  stays out of scope is named in `Q55`: a session amending a skill the human wrote.
- A research view on any transport: no `/research` route, no MCP tool, and no Telegram command that
  lists or inspects enquiries. A session is a conversation, and every channel already renders
  conversations. **Amended 2026-08-09 (session model)**: `/learn` and `/close` are commands and they
  are not that. Both are directives about the session in front of the human, relayed as ordinary
  messages (RS-100), and neither lists, inspects or addresses a session anywhere else.
- Any parallelism beyond one round's directions. Rounds are sequential because the human is in
  between them.

**Never, at any layer**
- **Execution of any kind: a shell, an interpreter, a scheduler, a job queue or an outbound
  trigger** (RS-111, decision AY). The knowledge base writes goal specs and reads results.
- **A session type, a lifecycle taxonomy, or a question asking the human to declare what a
  conversation will become** (RS-98, decision AW).
- **A `/learn` proposal raised as an approval** (RS-101), or a `/learn` that reads the conversation
  in place of the session file (RS-102).
- **A turn count, an idle timer or any code path that closes a session without `/close`**
  (RS-6, RS-14, RS-104).
- **A session that files something and closes itself** (RS-99).
- **A trace under `kb_root` from a session that filed nothing** (RS-105, RS-115).
- **A rendered filing that writes over an existing note, or that suffixes a slug its title does not
  carry** (RS-125). A taken lesson path refuses.
- **A `/learn` proposal for a lesson that already landed** (RS-123).
- **A fan-out target list from anything but one `route` call over the session record** (RS-119), or
  a session id resolved across a thread boundary (RS-120).
- **A session log or a directive on a derived expert thread or a scan thread** (RS-126).
- **A model supplying the frontmatter bytes of a rendered file.** The model supplies the proposal's
  metadata; code renders it, and a missing `description` is refused before the gate (RS-124).
- **A researcher that writes anything, anywhere** (RS-17, RS-20).
- **A `task` delegation for research** (RS-15).
- **An approval as the walk-with-me checkpoint** (RS-8).
- **The prior in the query-planning context, or in any researcher's context** (RS-18, RS-37).
- **A model as the filter on contradiction detection** (RS-38).
- **An offset, page number or character range as a locator** (RS-30).
- **A URL the harness did not fetch, treated as a source** (RS-29).
- **A research fetch staged under `<kb>/.inbox/`** (RS-34).
- **A synthesis filed as `type.note`, or without `provenance: researched`** (RS-45, C-40).
- **A research write outside the four-call order, or a fifth entry on RT-18's allow-list**
  (RS-20, RS-86, C-39).
- **A note written on a turn where an external tool returned content** (RS-95).
- **A search tool on an expert's ordinary turn** (RS-94).
- **A model call that renders a checkpoint, a merge, a sources list or a research-file section**
  (RS-10, RS-27, RS-32).
- **A topic MCP server on the Librarian** (RS-64).
- **A gated MCP tool, or an MCP tool that writes** (RS-66).
- **A research session that runs a model with no search capability and calls it research** (RS-75).
- **A re-fetch to verify a claim** (RS-127). The bytes the search returned are the bytes the check
  compares against, and no rule anywhere retrieves a page a second time to settle admissibility.
- **A quote admitted against an empty or absent `content`** (RS-127). A result missing it is a
  failed candidate with that reason.
- **`max_results` as a model-supplied argument** (RS-128), or any search budget stated in a prompt.
- **A search tool reachable from an MCP catalog entry, a binding or a `use` field** (RS-94, C-52).
  The two tool names are fixed in code and no deployment can rebind them.
- **Raw bytes taken from the research path into ingestion, or extracted text standing in for the
  copy beside a reference file** (RS-130).
- **Waiting, retrying on a timer, queueing an enquiry, or polling for recovery when search is
  unreachable** (RS-131). The tree is still there and the answer comes from it, in the same turn.
- **A session or a channel closed because search went down** (RS-131, RS-6). Only `/close` closes.
- **Search used as a fallback for a model, or a model used as a fallback for search** (RS-132).
- **One line reporting "degraded" when the human lost two capabilities** (RS-132).
- **`OLLAMA_API_KEY` in `RuntimeConfig`, in a `repr`, in a log, in `/health`, in the knowledge base,
  or read anywhere but `pkb.daemon`** (RS-133, RS-134, C-53). The repo is public.
- **The module-level `ollama.web_search`** (RS-133), which reads the key from the environment inside
  the library and would move the secret's entry point into `pkb.agents`.
- **Deployment configuration read from knowledge-base content** (RS-58).
- **A proposal filed as a working method by a session that found none** (RS-135), and the four other
  exclusions of §1.13 (RS-136 … RS-139).
- **A prompt telling a model that producing nothing is a missed opportunity** (RS-140, decision BB).
  Hermes ships that sentence and can roll back; D6 says this project cannot.
- **An amendment to bytes nobody loaded in this turn** (RS-141), including a render that
  reconstructs an existing file from the session record rather than from the file.
- **An autonomous amendment to a file carrying no recorded session origin** (RS-142). A file with no
  `## Sessions` block is the human's.
- **A backfill of `provenance` onto files already in the tree** (RS-47).
- **An empty `<Topic>/sessions/` folder** (RS-105, decision BA). The folder is minted by the first
  filing and never by a session opening.
- **A skill written into the packaged mount** (`RS-147`). `SKILLS_DENY_GLOBS` is `/skills/**` and it
  never narrows; the mount is package data inside an installed wheel, so it is unwritable whatever
  any rule says.
- **A widening of `SKILLS_DENY_GLOBS` to `/kb/**/skills/**`, or of any expert's write allow beyond
  its own subtree** (`RS-147`, RT-15). The four executed answers in `P-53` are what the acceptance
  test pins, in both directions.
- **A skill filed at the knowledge-base root by a model's own write tool**, at any scope
  (`RS-148`). The write is `_file_skill`, RT-18's fifth sanctioned harness writer.
- **A `file_skill` tool bound to the Librarian's graph** (`RS-148`, `RS-112`). The Librarian writes
  nothing, and the expert that ran the session is the one that proposed.
- **An origin block inside a `SKILL.md`** (`RS-149`). The body is prompt text, so the record goes in
  `<skill-folder>/ORIGIN.md`.
- **An autonomous amendment to a skill folder carrying no `ORIGIN.md`** (`RS-149`). That folder is
  the human's.
- **A skill that shadows a shipped name with no notice in the proposal, in the gate and in the file**
  (`RS-150`), and equally **a refusal of that collision**, which `SK-4` already blesses as the most
  obvious thing a human wants here.
- **A skill approved and filed that no expert will ever load** (`RS-152`). `check_skill_dir` runs
  before the gate, and a name that does not match its folder is refused rather than approved.
- **A proposal describing, amending or superseding a file the `/learn` run did not read**
  (`RS-153`), which is `RS-141`'s guard one step earlier.
- **A prompt telling a model that a good session leaves a better way of working behind it**
  (`RS-140` as amended, `C-59`). `F-34`'s bias was written for a system that learns procedure, and
  procedure is the kind this ruling adds.
- **A rename of `provenance: researched`** (decision BF). The value names the route the content
  took; the folder names where a session filed; `RS-46` forbids reading either off the other.

---

## 8. Build order

Eight increments. Each one ships on its own, each one leaves the human something they can use, and
each one is green under `make check` before the next starts. Increment 0 is separated out because it
is a **prerequisite in another spec** that increment 1 cannot be built without, and burying that
inside a bullet is how a build order stops being honest.

**Amended 2026-08-09 (session model): the session is increment 2 and everything after it moves down
one.** The order was research first and the session implied by it, which builds the bounded thing
this document just corrected and then unpicks it. The session is the object every later increment
hangs off: an enquiry runs inside one, a lesson files out of one, a fan-out distils one. Building it
first also ships the half the human said matters more, the try-and-feedback loop, before any of the
search machinery exists.

**Amended 2026-08-09 (search settled): increment 4 becomes the internet pillar whole, and increment
6 stops blocking it.** The order was written when search was an MCP server, so increment 4 shipped a
loop that could read URLs the human named and nothing else, and the feature the human asked for
waited for increment 6, which waited for Q37, which waited for a dependency measurement, which
waited for a vendor and a key. `P-50` cut every link in that chain: the client is installed, the
credential is one environment variable, and the tool is two functions. Increment 4 now ships
research. Increment 6 ships MCP for the capabilities MCP is still for, and a slow answer to Q37
costs a recipe server rather than the internet. Increment 7 keeps the offline work and gains the two
rules that were missing, because a session that meets an outage is a session that met one after the
loop existed to meet it with.

**Amended 2026-08-09 (skills): increment 2 gains a third half, 2c, and no increment number moves.** The
skill work hangs off `/learn`, which is 2a, so it lands inside increment 2 rather than after
increment 7 where a new feature would otherwise go. Nothing below it moves, because the placement
rule, the file class, the artifact exclusions and the gate reason are all built (`P-53 … P-56`), and
because the increment touches `pkb.core` and `pkb.agents.permissions` for nothing at all. The three
skill **files** this document owes are placed in the increments that produce the work they describe:
`lesson-proposal` in 2a, `skill-proposal` in 2c, `research planning and synthesis` in 4.

**0 · LS-13, with its normalisation exported. One spec, no research code.**

*Satisfies*: LS-13, and RS-28's dependency on it.
*Touches*: `pkb.agents.ingestion`, the large-source spec's §14 status line, `CLAUDE.md`'s build-order
table.

Implement LS-13's quoted-span check in `parse_takes`, and expose `normalise_for_quote_match(text)`
and `quote_in(haystack, quote)` as public functions of `pkb.agents.ingestion` so RS-28 calls them
rather than writing a second copy. LS-13 says only "the check is code in `parse_takes`", so without
this clause the factoring is undecided and the second implementation is what actually gets written.

*What the human gets*: the fabricated quotation that files verbatim today stops filing. That is worth
shipping with no research feature attached to it, and it is the reason this is increment 0 rather
than a footnote.

**1 · `provenance` in `pkb.core`. No LLM, no network, no research code.**

*Satisfies*: RS-42 … RS-48, FM-16, VA-42, VA-43 (against `sessions/`, decision BA), the VA-32 hint.
*Touches*: `pkb.core.frontmatter`, `pkb.core.models`, `pkb.core.validation`, README §1.4,
`tests/core/test_frontmatter.py`'s golden, VA-32's golden finding message.

The field, the closed vocabulary, the presence check, the amended hint. Nothing emits it yet, which
is RS-43's ordering: Layer 1 recognises the key before any writer produces one.

*What the human gets*: `servings:` on a recipe stops being a key the model is advised to delete.

**2 · The session. No research, no vendor, no key, no network.**

*Satisfies*: RS-96 … RS-126, **RS-135 … RS-142**, **RS-143 … RS-153**, RS-6 and RS-7 as amended,
RT-32 and RT-65's `SESSION_LESSON` and `SKILL_PROPOSAL` halves, RT-18's fifth entry, decisions AU to
AZ, `BB`, `BC`, `BD`, `BE` and `BF`, and Layer 5's §10 list.
*Touches*: `pkb.agents.research` (the session half), `pkb.agents.gates`, `pkb.agents.permissions`,
`pkb.agents.routing` (RS-119's one `route` call over a session record), `src/pkb/agents/skills/`
(two new shipped skills, `lesson-proposal` in 2a and `skill-proposal` in 2c), `pkb.server.telegram`
and the Layer 5 rules, `pkb.core` for nothing at all.

**Two of the three skill files this document owes land in this increment**, and naming them here is
what stops §2.4's gap outliving the feature. `P-57` records that the shipped `research` skill covers
the breadth-first pass over the tree and says nothing about a session, a round, the internet or
`/learn`, so **lesson proposal** has no file today and lands in 2a beside the machinery it drafts
for, and **skill proposal** lands in 2c. The third, **research planning and synthesis**, lands in
increment 4 with `C-38`'s narrowing of the shipped `research` skill, because the rounds it plans do
not exist before then. README §1.9 names two more that ship nowhere, an **article, post or clip**
extraction and a **manual or reference work** extraction; both belong to the large-source ingestion
spec and neither is owed here. `C-38`'s deeper rule binds all three of ours: the skill drafts, and
every guarantee stays in code (`C-56`).

**2a · One expert, one channel.** The session as a thread with its five states and its one close
edge (RS-96, RS-6, RS-104), the threads that carry no session (RS-126), the append-only log written
verbatim by code (RS-103, RS-82's deny rule), the two directives matched in code (RS-100), `/learn`
distilling from the file through `ingestion.model_asker` (RS-102) and proposing as a message
(RS-101), the push-back machinery on both paths (RS-106's `after_agent` seam, RS-107, RS-108) with
RS-38's selection built here rather than in increment 5 because push-back is what needs it first,
the lesson with its gated tool, its path resolver and its metadata (RS-109, RS-116, RS-124, RS-125),
the read-before-propose guard (RS-153) and the shipped `lesson-proposal` skill this document owes,
the `## Filed` repeat guard (RS-123), **the exclusion list and the inverted prior (RS-135 … RS-140)**,
**the two guards from `F-36` (RS-141, RS-142) with the origin block `_file_research` writes**,
discard leaving nothing (RS-105), and the closed session's refusal (RS-117). **This half is shippable on its own** and it is where every acceptance test that
does not need a second agent lives.

**2b · The Librarian's channel.** The fan-out: one `route` call for the scope (RS-119), the brief
that carries the record into each expert's own scratch (RS-120), collection through `parse_takes`
(RS-112), the expert that takes nothing (RS-115) and the expert that takes something and files
nothing (RS-122), the answer read in code ahead of classification (RS-121), and the concurrent gates
LB-16 already parks on derived threads (RS-113, RS-114). It lands second because every one of its
rules is 2a's machinery reached through LB-15, and because a fan-out debugged before the single
channel works is a fan-out debugged through four threads at once.

Layer 5 lands its half in the same increment: the purpose key, the seven commands, the topic close,
the goal argument.

The three halves land in order and **2a is green under `make check` before 2b starts, and 2b before
2c**, which is this build order's rule applied inside an increment rather than an exception to it.
*Amended 2026-08-09 (skills): there were two halves and there are now three.*

**2c · Skills at `/learn`.** *Added 2026-08-09 (skills), on the human's ruling on `Q54`.* The third
proposal kind and everything it drags behind it: `Proposal.kind` and `Proposal.scope` (RS-143), the
counterfactual test that decides note against skill (RS-144), the two placements resolved through
`pkb.core.resolve_skills` and the packaged mount read off `DEFAULT_SKILL_NAMES`, two lookups because
they are two sources and neither one is re-implemented here (RS-145, P-58), the `file_skill` tool with
`GateReason.SKILL_PROPOSAL` and its shared `research_render` (RS-146), `_file_skill` as RT-18's
fifth sanctioned harness writer with the root scope written by code (RS-148), the `ORIGIN.md`
sibling and the amendment boundary it draws (RS-149), the three collision cases with the shipped
fork notice reused rather than copied (RS-150), the exclusions and the scope line on the skill kind
(RS-151), `check_skill_dir` run before the gate (RS-152), and the read-before-propose guard (RS-153).
**Also the third skill file this document owes**: a shipped `skill-proposal` skill drafting what a
skill should say, which is `Q57`'s option (c) reached as a build item.

*Satisfies*: RS-143 … RS-153, decisions BD, BE and BF, C-56 … C-59, Q54, Q55, Q56, Q57.
*Touches*: `pkb.agents.research`, `pkb.agents.gates`, `src/pkb/agents/skills/` (one new skill),
`pkb.core` for nothing at all, `pkb.agents.permissions` **for nothing at all** (RS-147, P-53).

It lands third because every one of its rules is 2a's `/learn` machinery reached through a second
tool, and because the gate, the file class and the placement rule are all built already: the
increment is a proposal kind, a tool and a writer, and nothing below it moves. **The acceptance test
to write first is `RS-147`'s**, because it asserts the four permission answers in both directions and
it is the test that fails if somebody builds this increment by editing the deny list.

*What the human gets after 2a*: the try-and-feedback loop, which is the half they said matters more.
A channel per goal, a conversation that keeps its own record while the thread compacts around it, an
expert that objects while they can still act on it, and a lesson filed as `type.solution` when the
work earned one. No search of any kind, and nothing filed unless they approved the bytes. A session
that tried five things and found none that worked files nothing at all, and says so (RS-135, RS-140).
*What 2b adds*: the same loop on the topics that cross, where one `/learn` proposes a portfolio
lesson and a trading lesson and the human takes one.
*What 2c adds*: a session that learned a better way of **working** can say so, and the human approves
the exact bytes of a skill that changes how the expert works from the next turn on. A skill named
after a shipped one says three times that it shadows it. A skill nobody wrote by hand can never be
edited by anything autonomous.

*Deliberately not here*: anything that reaches past the tree. A session with no research in it is
still the object the human described, and building it first means every later increment plugs into a
session that already works.

**3 · Knowledge-base research. No vendor, no key, no network, no MCP, no researcher.**

*Satisfies*: RS-1 … RS-14, RS-35 … RS-37, RS-49 … RS-54, RS-79 … RS-89, RS-93, RS-84.
*Touches*: `pkb.agents.research`, `pkb.agents.gates`, `pkb.agents.permissions`, `pkb.packs`.

The enquiry inside the session increment 2 built: the scratch file with its deny rule (RS-4, RS-5,
RS-82) now carrying enquiry blocks beside the log, the three enquiry states with the restart
recovery (RS-6, RS-80), the prior pass (RS-35, RS-36), the early exit (RS-84), the planner
whose signature cannot reach the prior (RS-37), the checkpoint that ends the turn with code-rendered
bytes (RS-8 … RS-13, RS-79, RS-81), the gated `file_research` tool with its `GateEnv` seam and
RT-18's fourth entry (RS-50, RS-86 … RS-89), the research file with its fixed sections (RS-52), the
declined round-trip (RS-53, RS-54), and research files in the research pack (RS-93).

*What the human gets on day one*: a topic expert that walks them through what the knowledge base
already holds on a question, tells them when the tree already answers it, and files a research
summary they approved byte for byte. No vendor, no key, no socket.

*Deliberately not here*: pairing a **web finding** against a note. RS-38's Jaccard selection ships in
increment 2, where push-back needs it against the human's own claims (RS-106, RS-107). Increment 5
gives it its second caller, and it waits because a contradiction between a note and a *verified web
finding* needs increment 4 to produce one.

**4 · The internet pillar, whole. One environment variable, no vendor, no MCP.**

*Amended 2026-08-09 (search settled). It was titled "through fetch alone" and satisfied RS-90, which
is now withdrawn. Search moved in from increment 6 and the increment stopped being a half-measure.*

*Satisfies*: RS-15 … RS-34, RS-55, RS-91, RS-92, **RS-127 … RS-134**, LS-14, LS-15, LS-16, and
`C-38` whole.
*Touches*: `pkb.agents.researcher`, **`pkb.agents.search`**, `pkb.daemon`, `pyproject.toml`,
`src/pkb/agents/skills/` (the `research` skill narrowed and one new skill).
~~`pkb.sources`~~ is untouched (RS-90 as amended).

**`C-38` lands here whole, and it is two files rather than one rewrite.** `P-57` read the shipped
`research` skill: it covers the breadth-first pass over the tree, it says *"Research files nothing on
its own"*, and no sentence in it mentions a search, a URL, a round, a session or `/learn`. So the
narrowing is a title, a description and a scope line on a skill that already means one thing, and
**research planning and synthesis** is a new file: turning a goal into directions, briefing one
researcher per direction, weighing what returns against the topic's own notes, and drafting the
session summary. It lands in this increment because every step it describes exists only from here.
`tests/agents/test_shipped_skills.py` moves in the same commit, and `DEFAULT_SKILL_NAMES` grows by
one here and by one more in increment 2c, which is where `SK-1`'s count stops being ten.

The credential first, because nothing else here runs without it: `OLLAMA_API_KEY` read in
`pkb.daemon` beside `PKB_TELEGRAM_TOKEN`, the `AsyncClient` built there and handed down, the
module-scoped seam scan proved non-vacuous against `paths.py` (RS-133, RS-134). Then
`pkb.agents.search` with its two tools, its three failure meanings and its dropped-result counting
(RS-128, RS-129, RS-76). Then the researcher module (RS-15 … RS-27) with a toolset holding those two
and nothing else, admissibility over the bytes search returned (RS-28 … RS-34, RS-127), the
injection fencing at its real volume (RS-92), the research-versus-ingestion split with LS-14's
User-Agent and RS-91's per-hop guard on the ingestion side (RS-130), and the accepted-source outcome
through the built `ingest_source` (RS-55, RS-89). The offline rules land here rather than in
increment 7 (RS-131, RS-132), because an outage is reachable the moment the first search is, and a
rule about what a session does without search is untestable before a session has one.

*What the human gets*: research. Ask a question, watch three directions run, read every finding with
its quote checked against the page the searcher actually read, and approve a summary or an
ingestion. This is where fabricated quotations and fabricated URLs stop. **The whole cost of turning
it on is one line in a `.env` the deployment already has**, on the account that already runs the
models.

*Deliberately still here*: `read_page` for a URL the human names, which is the only thing the
original increment 4 delivered. It costs nothing to keep and it is what a researcher uses to follow
a link out of a result.

**5 · Weighing the prior.**

*Satisfies*: RS-38 … RS-41.
*Touches*: `pkb.agents.research`.

The Jaccard pair selection, its stopword list and its threshold already exist from increment 2, where
push-back pairs the human's own claims against the prior. This increment adds the second caller: web
findings paired against the prior, the bounded labelling call on that path, both reported counts, and
the fixed checkpoint group order. It lands after increment 4 because a contradiction between a note
and a *verified web finding* is the object the human asked to see, and increment 4 is what produces
one. One selection function with three callers is RS-107's ruling made structural.

*What the human gets*: the second pillar starts doing work. A note that disagrees with what the
internet says is shown as a pair with both quotes, and nothing touches the note.

**6 · MCP clients.**

*Satisfies*: RS-58 … RS-67, RS-94, RS-95.
*Touches*: `pkb.agents.mcp`, `pkb.agents.runtime`, `pkb.agents.registry`.

The catalog, the bindings, the async warm-up and the sync cache read (RS-60), the degraded build and
its TTL, the invalidation, the Librarian check, the per-tool read-only allow-list, the `use` split
and the notes refusal (RS-94, RS-95). Q37 is answered before the commit.

*Amended 2026-08-09 (search settled): this increment no longer carries search, and nothing waits on
it.* RS-94's `use` split now governs MCP servers alone, because RS-128's two tools are `research`-use
by rule and no catalog entry can move them (C-52). RS-95's notes refusal is unchanged and lands here
still, since an `expert`-use MCP server is the thing that puts external content on an ordinary turn.

*What the human gets*: ~~a bound search server turns increment 3's named-URL loop into research~~ a
bound recipe server answers a Cooking question in one conversation with no session at all, and a
bound domain server gives a researcher a source the open web does not carry. Research itself arrived
in increment 4.

**7 · Budgets, the offline path, and multi-topic.**

*Satisfies*: RS-68 … RS-78, RS-57, C-43.
*Touches*: `pkb.agents.research`, `pkb.agents.runtime`, `pkb.agents.routing`.

Every budget as a hard stop with the defaults RS-68 lists, the stall check, the fallback-sized
deadline and the failover notice, knowledge-base mode made explicit as the default, and the
one-round multi-topic fan-out (RS-57). The budgets land last because their acceptance tests are
about what happens *at the bound*, and those are worth writing against a finished loop.

*Amended 2026-08-09 (search settled): RS-131 and RS-132 moved forward to increment 4 and the rest of
the offline work stays here.* An outage rule is testable the moment a search exists to fail, and
increment 4 is where one does. Everything about **bounds** stays here, and everything about
availability moves: RS-68's hard stops now including `research_search_results`, RS-70's exhausted budget
as an ordinary outcome, RS-71's stall check, RS-72's fallback-sized deadline, RS-73's failover line,
and RS-74's, RS-75's and RS-77's knowledge-base mode made explicit. RS-76's three failure meanings
land in increment 4 with the tool that raises them.

*What the human gets*: a session that says which budget stopped it, a session that keeps working
when the cloud quota is gone, and one goal researched by two topics in their own voices.

## 9. What the review changed, and where it was wrong

Three independent reviews read this document against the built code on 2026-08-09. Everything below
was checked in the source before it was accepted or refused, because that is this project's standing
rule and it has been right every time.

### 9.1 Confirmed defects, fixed here

| What was wrong | How it was verified | Where it is fixed |
|----------------|---------------------|-------------------|
| `VA-33` and `RT-49` were already taken, and `F-13`, `Q35`, `AI`, `AJ` are claimed by the uncommitted Layer 5 change on this same branch | grep over `docs/`, `src/`, `tests/` in the working tree; `validation.py:530`, Layer 2 rules line 263, `runtime.py:766` | §"Why the prefix", §5.4, §5.5 |
| No mechanism existed to turn a harness write into an approval a human could answer | `ingestion._write` returns `(False, reason, [])` on a gate; interrupts fire only on model tool calls (`gates.py:820,831`); `service/proposals.py:14` says v1 "cannot apply" | RS-87, RS-88, RS-89, §5.2's gate seam |
| RS-2's `directions` argument reintroduced a plan formed with the prior in context, defeating F-24 | the argument would be filled by the expert's model, whose context holds the survey | RS-2 (no arguments), RS-37 (signature) |
| The code-rendered checkpoint was handed back to a model, which then authored what the human read | the tool-answer shape returns a `ToolMessage` and the model gets another step | RS-79 |
| MCP connection is `async` and `AgentRegistry.get` is sync under a `threading.RLock`, called from worker threads | `registry.py:277-300` and its own docstring | RS-60 (async warm-up, sync cache read) |
| `pkb.sources` has no public fetch that avoids `.inbox`; `_http_fetch` is private and follows redirects unchecked | `__all__` at `sources.py:63-93`; `_http_fetch` at line 931 | RS-90, RS-91 |
| RS-34's process cache did not survive the restart RS-4 exists for | a process cache dies with the process | RS-34 (verdict persists, bytes do not) |
| `sessions/` (`research/` before decision BA) is not structural, and making it so is a `pkb.core` change | `STRUCTURAL_DIRS` at `paths.py:86`; `scaffold.py:184-187` | RS-49, README §1.2 |
| No pack loads `FileRole.EXTENSION_ITEM`, so RS-56's ordering reached nothing | `packs.py:100-182` | RS-93 |
| Prompt injection appeared nowhere, in a repo that already carries the threat model | `check_fetchable`'s docstring; `runtime.py:498` | RS-92, §7's residual risk |
| An expert holding a search tool on an ordinary turn routes around all of §1.4 | the notes path is un-gated (P-44) | RS-94, RS-95 |
| `RS-5`, `RS-15`, `RS-9`, `RS-37`, `RS-53` were enforced by cooperative fixtures, greps or AST checks | RT-15's permission list defaults to allow; `routing.py` itself contains the word `task` | RS-82, RS-83, RS-9, RS-37, RS-13 |
| Constants with no values or homes | `research_deadline_seconds`, `NO_PROGRESS_STEPS`, `MAX_PAIRS`, `SESSION_IDLE_TURNS`, the overlap threshold | RS-68, RS-72's arithmetic |
| RT-18's allow-list needed amending or `make check` fails | RT-18's acceptance test is an AST check over the exact three-element set | RS-86, C-39 |
| A session stuck in `running` after a restart wedges the thread forever | RS-4 makes the state survive; RS-7 then refuses every `start_research` | RS-80 |
| Multi-topic research had no return path for direction | LB-14's derived thread, LB-18 ends the turn, LB-2 classifies the reply as new | RS-57 (one round, no loop) |

### 9.2 Where a reviewer was wrong, and what settled it

1. **"RS-50's justification is false: `requires_approval` already gates this file."** Half right, and
   the correction is recorded in RS-50 and P-44. `_is_curated` does cover extension-folder content
   (`gates.py:593-607`) and `_introduces_approved` does fire on a brand-new file carrying
   `status.approved` (`gates.py:626-634`), so a research file tagged `status.approved` gates today
   as `STATUS_APPROVED`. The hole is the other tag: a new research file tagged
   `status.conflict-review` (RS-51's case) with no new tags reaches no branch of the chain and lands
   unattended. `RESEARCH_OUTPUT` stays, keyed by the tool, and its *Why* now states the framing
   argument rather than the wrong factual one.

2. **"Accepting a source needs its own gate reason."** Refused, and Q45 records it with the
   alternative fully specified. The human has just typed `ingest c3` against a candidate whose
   claim, quote and URL were printed one message earlier; a gate fired one message later asks the
   same question twice and trains them to click through gates. The real worry underneath, that the
   *model's belief* the human said yes is enough, is answered structurally instead: RS-89 requires
   every id in `accept` to have been printed at the last checkpoint and takes the URL from the
   session's fetch log, so the model cannot hand `ingest_source` a page nobody saw. That is a
   stronger guarantee than a second button.

3. **"Rename `research/` to `enquiry/`, or the module to `pkb.agents.enquiry`."** Refused. Research
   is the human's own word for what they asked for, it is already in README §2.7 and §1.2, and
   renaming their vocabulary to relieve a collision among our module names is the wrong way round.
   §0.2 names all four uses of the word once, which is what a reader actually needs.

   **Overturned in part on 2026-08-09 (sessions), by the human.** The original text is kept above
   because the verdict was wrong in an instructive way. The reviewer was right that the folder name
   was wrong and wrong about the reason, and the refusal was right about the principle and wrong
   about the fact. The reviewer read a **collision** among our own module names; the human read a
   **claim the folder makes about its contents**, and said so in one sentence: *"Sessions are just
   long conversation that could be research, could be collaborative discussion, or just really
   anything."* So `enquiry/` would have been the same mistake with a different word, since an enquiry
   is one more thing a session sometimes does. The folder is `sessions/` (decision BA), the module
   stays `pkb.agents.research`, and the principle the refusal defended survives untouched: the folder
   now carries the human's word for the object rather than this document's word for one of its
   activities. **The lesson worth keeping is about who the name was tested against**: the refusal
   checked the name against our module list and never against what a human would expect to find in
   that folder six months later.

4. **"Rename the field to `acquired_by`, because `ingestion.PROVENANCE_HEADING` owns the word."**
   Refused as a rename, accepted as a gap. The two coexist and answer different questions: the body
   block names *which source and where its copy sits*, written by code and read back by
   `_recorded_origin`; the frontmatter field names *by which route the content entered the tree*.
   RS-42 now says so explicitly, which is the fix the observation deserved.

5. **"A knowledge-base-mode file needs a fifth provenance value."** Refused. `provenance` names the
   route the file's content took into the tree, and a research session is that route whether or not
   it reached the internet. A knowledge-base-mode file cites KB paths and no URL, which is where a
   reader learns it never left the tree. A fifth value would need its own place in
   `PROVENANCE_ORDER` and its own answer to "what does it mean on a note", for one file class.
   RS-45 states the ruling.

### 9.3 The second review pass, on §1.11 alone

A fourth review read the session model against the built validator, `routing.py`, `gates.py` and
`telegram.py`. Everything it confirmed was re-executed here before it was accepted.

| What was wrong | How it was verified | Where it is fixed |
|----------------|---------------------|-------------------|
| RS-109 filed a lesson as `source_type: note` tagged `type.solution`, which VA-11's bijection refuses | executed against `validate_content` on a fixture tree: `SOURCE_TYPE_TAG_MISMATCH error VA-11`; `source_type: solution` with the same tag returns zero findings | RS-109, C-49 |
| The fan-out had no producer for its target list: LB-1 gives the Librarian one judgement and RS-100 skips every model call for a directive | LB-1, LB-2, LB-15 read | RS-119 |
| The fan-out had no way to carry the session record across a thread boundary; `file_lesson(session_id, …)` resolved a session on a thread the caller cannot read | `_build_backend`'s docstring (`runtime.py:1723`): each thread gets its own scratch filesystem | RS-120, RS-116 |
| The human's keep/drop had no landing place on a Librarian thread, which is the failure RS-57 already wrote down for checkpoints | LB-2, `RouteMiddleware` ends the run at the `route` call | RS-121, C-51 |
| RS-113 specified a gate serializer nothing asks for and credited RT-39, which is per thread | LB-16's docstring (`routing.py:572`) says fan-out gates park on their own threads and coexist | RS-113 |
| An expert that answered with takes and never called `file_lesson` was indistinguishable from one that answered `NOTHING` | P-34's measurement in this repo | RS-122 |
| `resolve_slug` is references-only and origin-keyed, so it resolves nothing for a rendered file; and a `-2` suffix on a note whose title did not change is a VA-35 warning | `ingestion.py:523` read; VA-35 executed on `brisket-rub-2.md` titled *"Brisket rub"* | RS-125, RS-87, RS-116, Q42 |
| Nothing stopped a second `/learn` re-filing the first lesson beside it | RS-38's label is a hint by rule; RS-125 refuses rather than deduplicates; PK-10 loads solution notes | RS-123 |
| RS-102 asserted a distillation context with no thread messages, which the obvious in-graph implementation cannot have | the graph's state *is* the message list | RS-102 |
| RS-106 required a code-rendered block beside a model's answer and named no seam | RS-79 exists because the first draft handed rendered bytes back to a model | RS-106 |
| Neither filing tool said where `title`, `description`, `tags` or `related_topics` come from, and the tool key had already skipped `NEW_TAG` | README §1.4, §1.5; RS-50 | RS-124, C-50, Q51 |
| RS-108 fired `status.conflict-review` on any **surfaced** pair, and RS-38 surfaces every selected pair whatever the label | RS-38, RS-51's predicate, README Part 4's escalation rule | RS-108 |
| RS-96 claimed a session is unreachable without a channel, which the TUI contradicts, and left derived and scan threads unruled against RS-103 | Q47; LB-14; RT-58 | RS-96, RS-126 |
| RS-117 covered directives and left an ordinary message on a closed session unruled | Telegram permits reopening a topic; TG-78 keeps `reopenForumTopic` absent | RS-117, §10 |
| §10 named TG-86 for `/new` and missed TG-25, TG-27 and TG-39, and missed that `/new` was the only exit from a wedged thread | TG-27, TG-39 read; RT-39 and RS-104 both refuse on a pending interrupt | §10, RS-104 |
| RS-100's exact match fails silently on `/learn@YourBot`, which is what Telegram sends from a group menu | `telegram.py:1179` routes on the first character and the unknown branch never reaches `_turn` | §10's TG-86 relay row |
| §5.5 changed a decision set without recording an amendment to RT-32 | RT-32's text: *"Write gates allow exactly …"* | §5.5 |
| RS-111's grep scope was narrower than its claim | `_build_backend`'s docstring: `execute` bypasses the permission layer and is inert by backend choice | RS-111 |
| README §1.4 said everything filed at `/learn` is `practised` and that the harness refuses either kind without provenance | `Proposal.kind` allows a summary; only VA-43 exists, and RS-109 rules out the `notes/` half deliberately | README §1.4 |
| README §1.9's Tier 2 read as if the `/learn` pairing lives in skill text | C-38 rules that no guarantee here is carried by a skill | README §1.9 |
| C-47 described a README that no longer exists | the README landed in the same change | C-47, marked resolved in place |
| RS-105 and README §2.7 stated an archive guarantee over storage this system does not own | TG-82 and TG-83 exist because humans delete topics | RS-105, README §2.7 |

**Three of the review's own fixes were narrowed or refused, and one was widened.**

1. **"RS-113: drop the sequencing claim."** Accepted for the fan-out and refused as a blanket rule.
   LB-16 is right that four experts on four derived threads hold four pendings, and RS-113 now says
   so. A set proposed by **one** expert on **one** thread is the other case, and there RT-39 does
   serialise, so the sequential statement survives where it is true. Deleting it wholesale would
   have left the single-expert `/learn` with no rule about its second gate.

2. **"A proposal introducing an unregistered tag is refused before the gate."** Narrowed. Refusing
   would make the one path in this design that files a lesson unable to grow the tag registry at
   all, against README §1.5's ruling that the AI proposes new tags and the human approves them, and
   the human's alternative is registering a tag by hand on a phone before the lesson can use it.
   RS-124 names the tag on its own line in the proposal message instead, so the human answers it
   before the gate and reads it again in the diff. Q51 records the stricter option and its cost.

3. **"Restate RS-96 in Layer 2's vocabulary: a session is a thread."** Accepted, and the consequence
   is stated rather than smoothed over. Every channel is a session and not every session is a
   channel, because the TUI creates threads with no channel and Q47 rules `/learn` reachable there.
   Decision AU is untouched, since AU rules what a **channel** is.

4. **"Add the missing-call case to RS-112."** Accepted as its own rule (RS-122) rather than a clause,
   because it is the one failure in the fan-out that produces a correct-looking tree.

### 9.4 The rename and the Hermes reconciliation, 2026-08-09

*Neither of these came from a review. The first is the human's ruling on a name and the second is
their instruction to read another project and reconcile it, so this sub-section records what changed
and what was deliberately left out rather than a set of verdicts.*

| What changed | What settled it | Where |
|--------------|-----------------|-------|
| The extension folder is `sessions/` | the human's own sentence about what a session is; `STRUCTURAL_DIRS` excludes both names, so Layer 1 classifies either as `FileRole.EXTENSION_ITEM` | decision BA, §1.1a, RS-45, RS-49, RS-52, RS-93, RS-105, RS-109, RS-110, VA-43 |
| The scratch root moved with it, to `/sessions/` | `SESSION_ROOT` named the object and its value named one activity; no code exists yet, so the string costs nothing to move | RS-4 amended, RS-82 amended, §5.2 |
| The rename creates one hazard and it is written down | the knowledge base now holds a `sessions/` folder too, so RS-82's deny must stay anchored at the scratch root | RS-82's acceptance test, §0.2's layout comment |
| Five exclusions became rules | `F-35`, read from `background_review.py:288-296` | RS-135 … RS-139 |
| The prior on filing inverted rather than being copied | `F-34` against D6: Hermes has archive, snapshots and rollback, and this tree has no undo | decision BB, RS-140, C-55 |
| Two guards were adopted and both stay in code | `F-36`, read from `skill_manager_tool.py:425-451` and `skill_provenance.py` | decision BC, RS-141, RS-142, C-54, RS-125 amended |
| The two objects got one name each, shared with the README | the rename left "session record" naming the scratch file here and the filed file there, so one phrase meant opposite things in the two documents; the filed file carries `type.summary`, so **the session summary** is its name and **the session record** stays the scratch file | §1.1a, §0.2, README §2.7 |
| The filed file's last heading is `## Sessions` | `## Session record` gave the same phrase a third meaning inside the summary itself | RS-52 amended, `ORIGIN_HEADING` in §5.2, RS-142 |
| A refused rename was reopened and recorded rather than edited away | the human overturned it; the original verdict and its reasoning are kept | §9.2 row 3 |

**Four things Hermes does that this design deliberately does not take, each with the reason.**

1. **Its cadence.** `F-31`: skill review fires every ten tool iterations and memory review every ten
   user turns, and `turn_finalizer.py:768-773` notes `on_session_end()` is not called there. The
   human's `/learn` at close is novel against that, so no number Hermes publishes is evidence for a
   once-per-session write, and no rule here cites one as if it were.
2. **Its forked reviewer.** `F-32`: a separate agent with a runtime whitelist, at ~30,000 tokens an
   event. RS-102 already reaches a model directly through `ingestion.model_asker` with a context
   assembled in code, which gives the same isolation for one call, and RS-100 puts the human at the
   trigger, so a second agent would buy an autonomy this design does not want.
3. **Its human-in-the-loop suppression.** `F-33`: Hermes skips the review on cron sessions. RS-126
   already reaches the same place from the other direction, since a derived expert thread and a scan
   thread carry no session and refuse a directive, so nothing unattended has a record to distil.
4. **Its writing bias**, for the reason decision BB gives.

**One thing it exposes that this design cannot answer yet**, and `Q54` is where it sits: Hermes
learns how to work and writes procedure into a skill library; this project learns what is true and
writes knowledge into a tree. The seed of the other half is already shipped, in the `voice` skill's
profile, which is the one place the system knows something about the human rather than about their
domain. The obstacle is SK-3, SK-5 and SK-17, and it is named in the question rather than solved in a
rule.

### 9.5 The skills ruling, 2026-08-09

*This one came from the human's ruling on `Q54` rather than from a review, so the table records what
changed and what the ruling's own brief got wrong. Three of the four obstacles `Q54` stated were
checkable, and all three were false. Each row names the call that settled it, because a design
argument built on a wrong reading of built code is the failure this whole document's §2 exists to
catch, and this time §2 caught it about itself.*

| What changed | What settled it | Where |
|--------------|-----------------|-------|
| `/learn` may propose a skill | the human's ruling on `Q54`, and their sentence that skills need not be read-only | decision BD, §1.14, `RS-143`, `Proposal.kind` |
| The permission list changes for nothing | executed: `_check_fs_permission` over `kb_permissions("Cooking")` returns **allow** for `/kb/Cooking/skills/**` and deny for the other three | `P-53`, `RS-147`, §0.2's layout comment, §7's two new refusals |
| The gate was already built | executed: `requires_approval` returns `skill-overload` for a `skills/` write today, at both scopes | `P-54`, `RS-146`, `RT-65` amended |
| The artifact exclusions were the file class working | executed: a `SKILL.md` validates clean on two fields, reaches no index and no tag registry, and a sibling `ORIGIN.md` validates clean too | `P-55`, `RS-145`, `RS-149` |
| The placement rule was already the merge order | executed: `resolve_skills` returns the topic's `research` and shadows the root's for that topic alone | `P-56`, `RS-145`, `RS-150` |
| The shipped shadow is a second merge, at a second seam | executed: `resolve_skills` returns nothing from `packaged_skills_root()`, and deepagents keys its own last-wins merge on the frontmatter `name` | `P-58`, `RS-145`, `RS-150`, `RS-152` |
| The root write is code, and the Librarian gains nothing | `adopt_skill` and `create_topic` are the precedent, on RT-18's own allow-list | decision BE, `RS-148`, `C-58`, §5.5 |
| Revision is bounded by authorship rather than forbidden | `F-36`'s two guards, with the origin record moved out of `SKILL.md` because its body is prompt text | `RS-149`, `RS-141` amended, `RS-142` amended, `Q55` |
| The read-before-revise claim got a rule id | the README asserted it and no rule carried it; `RS-141` bound the renderer and nothing bound the proposal | `RS-153` |
| `provenance: researched` keeps its name | the value names the route, the folder names the container, and `RS-46` already forbids reading either off the other | decision BF, §7's last refusal |
| Three skill files are placed in the build order | `P-57`: the shipped `research` skill mentions no search, no round, no session and no `/learn` | `C-38` amended, §8's increments 2a, 2c and 4 |

**The ruling's brief was wrong in one place, and it is stated here rather than corrected in silence.** It read:
*"Every agent is denied every skill write, before its own-subtree allow is even considered. An expert
therefore cannot write `<its own topic>/skills/**` today, deliberately."* Both sentences are false.
`SKILLS_DENY_GLOBS` is `(SKILLS_MOUNT + "**",)`, and `SKILLS_MOUNT` is `"/skills/"`, a
`CompositeBackend` route that is not `/kb/`, so the rule the brief pointed at never sees a
knowledge-base path at all. Executed, an expert writes its own topic's skills today and gates on
`skill-overload` when it does. **The consequence matters more than the correction**: read the brief's
way, the work is a permission edit, and the obvious edit is to narrow a glob on a first-match-wins
list whose own module docstring opens with *"Read the ordering before touching this list."* An edit
made there for a reason that turns out to be imaginary is how an expert gains write access to a
neighbour's tree with every test about its own topic still passing. `RS-147` pins all four answers,
in both directions, so the next reader checks rather than reasons.

**The reconciliation pass found one more, and this document made it too.** Both documents written on
the ruling said a knowledge-base skill shadows a **shipped** skill through `pkb.core.resolve_skills`.
It does not: `resolve_skills` merges the knowledge base's own root against the topic chain and never
reads the packaged mount, which `expert.base_skill_sources` lays underneath both and deepagents
merges last-wins on the frontmatter `name`. `P-58` prints the calls. The correction is small on the
page and it moves two rules. `RS-150`'s two lookups stay two rather than collapsing into one, because
one of them cannot answer the other's question. And `RS-152` stops being tidiness: a folder and a
frontmatter name that disagree fail in **two** directions, one leaving a shipped skill in force under
a file the human approved and one shadowing a shipped skill from a directory entry that says
otherwise, both silent, both warned about in a log nobody reads. Conflating the two merges is how a
later reader deletes the `DEFAULT_SKILL_NAMES` comparison as a duplicate and takes the shipped-shadow
notice off the one case it was written for.

---

## 10. Requirements on Layer 5

`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-08-pkb-telegram-layer5-rules.md` is owned elsewhere and **nothing
here amends it**. This section states what §1.11 requires of it, one row per `TG-` rule, so a later
pass makes the amendments with the reason already written down. Every row names the rule, what
changes, and the failure the change prevents. Layer 5 is where a session becomes visible, so a rule
in §1.11 that Layer 5 does not carry is a rule that holds in code and not on the human's phone.

| `TG-` rule | What has to change | Why, and what breaks without it |
|---|---|---|
| **TG-77** | **The uniqueness key moves from `(chat_id, agent_id)` to `(chat_id, agent_id, purpose)`**, and the directory row gains the goal. A `/channels` naming an agent **and a goal** that already has a channel here creates nothing and points at it; the same agent with a different goal creates a second channel. | RS-97. TG-77's stated harm is an **accidental** split: *"that expert's history splits in half with nothing on screen saying which half a message went to"*. A channel named for its goal is a deliberate split whose title says which half the human is in, so the rule's reason survives and its key was wrong. Without the change, the second goal for one expert is refused and the human runs both lines of work in one thread, where every turn replays the other one's history until the 170,000-token compaction blends them. |
| **TG-78** | **`closeForumTopic` joins the `BotApi` Protocol**, reached from one call site, triggered by `/close` alone and by a session Layer 2 reported closed. `deleteForumTopic`, `reopenForumTopic` and `unpinAllForumTopicMessages` stay absent for the reasons TG-78 already gives. | RS-104, RS-105. `/close` ends the session and the channel, and a channel that stays open after its session closed is a conversation the human keeps typing into with an expert that stopped keeping the record. Closing rather than deleting is the whole point of RS-105's asymmetry: the tree keeps nothing and the phone keeps everything, so the topic has to survive as a closed, readable archive. |
| **TG-86** | **The command surface becomes seven: `/channels`, `/threads`, `/agents`, `/pending`, `/cancel`, `/learn`, `/close`. `/new` is removed.** | Decisions AU and AV. `/new` rotates a channel onto a fresh conversation, which splits a session in half now that a channel is a session, and it does it with no warning and no way back. Starting new work means opening a new channel. Leaving `/new` on the surface leaves a command whose only effect is to destroy the continuity the session model exists to provide. |
| **TG-25, TG-27, TG-39** | **The `/new` removal reaches three more rules.** TG-27 (*"Rotation is explicit only (`/new`)"*) loses its only trigger and the rule becomes "a channel never rotates". TG-39 still lists the surface as `/new`, `/threads`, `/agents`, `/pending`, `/cancel` and moves with TG-86. TG-25 as amended reads *"never two channels in one chat (TG-77)"*, which RS-97 breaks as directly as TG-77's own key. | RS-97, decisions AU and AV. §10's first draft named TG-86 and stopped, and a command removed from one rule while three others still define what it did is a command that comes back the next time somebody reads TG-27. **The wedge is the part worth stating**: `/new` was also the only way to abandon a thread whose gate could not be answered, since RT-39 refuses every turn while an interrupt is pending and RS-104 refuses `/close` in the same state. `/pending` is now the only escape, it reaches the interrupt from outside the blocked thread, and RS-104's refusal message names it. |
| **TG-86** (second) | **`/learn` and `/close` are relayed as ordinary messages carrying `LEARN_DIRECTIVE` and `CLOSE_DIRECTIVE`, and the adapter decides nothing about either.** The adapter **strips a trailing `@<botname>`** before relaying. The adapter's own work on `/close` is the topic close, after Layer 2 reports the session closed. | RS-100, RS-104, Q46. The offer-once rule, the pending-approval refusal and the distillation all live in Layer 2 where the session state is. An adapter that decided when a session closes would be a second implementation of RS-104, and the two disagree on the case that matters: a `/close` typed while an approval is pending. **The `@<botname>` strip is what makes RS-100's exact match safe to keep exact**: Telegram appends the bot name when a command is tapped from a menu in a group, so `/learn@YourBot` arrives, RS-100's match fails, and the directive becomes an ordinary turn whose text is a command the model has never seen. The adapter routes on the first character today (`telegram.py:1179`) and its unknown-command branch answers `I know …` without reaching `_turn` at all, so an unstripped suffix is silent in both directions. |
| **TG-87** | **`/channels <agent-id>` gains a goal: `/channels <agent-id> <goal>`.** The bind-here form takes one too. A creation with no goal is legal and leaves the goal empty until the human's first message in the channel sets it, in code, verbatim. | RS-97, RS-2's precedent (code reads the goal off the thread and the model never states it). A channel with no goal is a session with nothing to name it, so the title cannot carry both names and the picker cannot tell two Trading channels apart. Reading the first message as the goal is what keeps the picker's one-press path alive without asking the human to type a form. |
| **TG-105** | **The title the bot writes is the agent's catalog title and the goal**, in that order, and a second rename fires once when a goal-less channel gets its goal from the first message. Every rename after that belongs to the human, as TG-105 already rules. | RS-97 and decision AE. AE lets a reply inside a channel omit the agent attribution on the ground that the topic header supplies it, so a header carrying the goal alone deletes what AE rests on, and a header carrying the agent alone leaves two Trading channels indistinguishable in the topic list. TG-105 names a topic once at the moment of ownership; a goal that arrives one message later is the same moment, reached in two steps. |
| **TG-88** | **A `/pending` keyboard goes to the channel of the approval's *thread*, not to its agent's channel.** | RS-97. An agent now has several channels, so "that approval's agent's channel" no longer names one place. A gate raised in `Trading · Trend Signal` that lands in `Trading · Market Regime` asks the human to approve bytes whose conversation is in the other topic, which is the mis-file TG-1 exists to prevent, one level down. |
| **TG-85** | **Attribution names the channel's goal as well as the agent** wherever a message is delivered outside its own channel. | RS-97, decision AE. The prefix exists so a message outside its channel says where it belongs, and with several channels per expert the agent id alone no longer says which one. |
| **TG-102, TG-103, TG-104** | **Every repair path keys on the directory row rather than on `(chat_id, agent_id)`.** Recreation counts, General fallbacks and pins belong to one channel, not to every channel an expert has. | RS-97. These three rules were written when one agent meant one row. Keyed on the agent, a dead `Trend Signal` topic spends `Market Regime`'s recreation allowance and pins both channels to General, so one broken topic takes out every line of work with that expert. |
| **TG-96, TG-97, TG-101** | **The picker lists one row per existing channel plus one "new channel" row per agent**, and a press on the new-channel row creates a goal-less channel that TG-87 and TG-105 then name from the first message. TG-101's "one press creates at most one channel" holds unchanged. | RS-97. The picker is the phone-friendly path and a purpose key with no picker support pushes every human to type `/channels topic/trading trend signal` on a phone keyboard. `callback_data` is 64 bytes, so a goal cannot ride in the press, which is why the goal arrives as the first message instead. |
| **TG-78** (second) | **A closed channel refuses a new turn and says so**, rather than minting a session on the next message typed into it. Telegram lets a human reopen a closed forum topic, and TG-78 keeps `reopenForumTopic` absent, so the bot never learns it happened. | RS-117 as amended. Layer 2 refuses the turn and Layer 5 is where the human reads the refusal. Without the row, the human reopens a topic, types, gets an answer, and now holds a channel whose session file says `closed` and whose log stopped weeks ago. |
| **TG-89** | **A `SESSION_LESSON` keyboard raised on a derived expert thread is posted into the originating channel**, which is what TG-89 already does, and this becomes load-bearing. | RS-113, LB-16. A fanned-out `/learn` parks each gate on `<librarian-thread>::<agent-id>`, so several are pending at once and every one of them belongs to a conversation the human is having in the Librarian's channel. Answering them needs `resume(agent_id=<expert>, thread_id=<derived>)`, which is LB-16's own contract, and the human never sees a derived thread id. |
| **TG-64 and the keyboard family** | **A `/learn` proposal carries no keyboard.** It is an assistant message and the human answers it in prose. | RS-101. A keyboard is the visual grammar of an approval, and an approval blocks every other message on the thread with a 409, so *"that lesson is half right, drop the second part"* stops being expressible. The **filing** that follows carries the keyboard, on `file_lesson`'s `SESSION_LESSON` gate (RS-116). |
| **TG-93** | **No change, and it becomes load-bearing.** The turn lock is already per channel. | RS-97. Several channels for one expert now run concurrently by design, so a lock keyed per chat would serialise a human's two lines of work behind each other and a lock keyed per agent would do the same. |
| **New rule** | **A `SESSION_LESSON` gate renders like every other write gate**, with `("approve", "reject")` and no `edit`, which the existing keyboard already produces. | RS-116, RT-32. The keyboard drops `edit` already, so this needs a test rather than a change, and the test is what stops a later widening from offering an `edit` that would edit a session id. |
| **New rule** (second) | **A `SKILL_PROPOSAL` gate renders on the same terms, and its message carries the scope and the shadow lines.** `("approve", "reject")`, no `edit`, and the rendered body is whatever `RS-150` and `RS-151` put in the gate's description, unchanged by the adapter. | RS-146, RS-150, RS-151. The adapter already renders a gate's description verbatim, so this is a test rather than a change, and the test is the one that matters: a root skill changes every expert in the tree, the human reads that on a phone, and a truncation or a re-wrap that drops the scope line takes away the only sentence saying so. |

**Layer 4 owes one line, not a rule.** `/learn` and `/close` are messages, so the TUI sends them by
typing them (Q47). A shortcut is additive whenever somebody wants it, and `pkb.clients` stays
transport-free and UI-free either way.
